| `ARCHUncertaintySet(; n_sim = 1)` with an ellipsoid or a norm ball | Accepted. The fit raised an error that named neither the cause nor the remedy. | Raises a `DomainError` when you build the estimator. A box set still accepts one resample. |
| The mean set of `OrthogonalUncertaintySet` under `IdiosyncraticVarianceScaling()`, when the idiosyncratic covariance makes the scaling indefinite | Returned a set with a complex map `L`. | Raises a `LinearAlgebra.PosDefException`. Only a loadings block that you build by hand can give such a scaling. |
| A risk measure whose observation weights `w`, its own or those of the prior, have a length other than the number of observations | Ran. The weighted quantile kernels read the weights through the sort permutation of the returns, so a long vector passed in silence. | Raises a `DimensionMismatch` that names the weights, at the first evaluation or model build that reads them. |
| A risk measure with observation weights that sum to zero, or `LowOrderMoment` with a negative weight | Accepted. | Raises a `DomainError` when you build the measure. A dynamic weight raises when it resolves. |
| `LowOrderPrior` whose `sigma` holds a value that is not finite between two investable assets | Accepted. | Raises an `IsNonFiniteError`. An asset whose `mu` or variance is not finite is not investable, and its row and column are not checked. |
| `CombinatorialCrossValidation` with `n_test_folds == n_folds`, or a negative `purged_size` or `embargo_size` on it or on `KFold` | Accepted. `n_test_folds == n_folds` gave one split with an empty training set. | Raises a `DomainError`. |
| `DateWalkForward` with an integer `train_size = 0` | Accepted. Every fold trained on no row. | Raises a `DomainError` when you build the scheme. |
| `split` or `n_splits` of a `DateWalkForward` with a fold whose training window holds no row after `expand_train` and `purged_size`, for example `DateWalkForward(2, 1; period = Day(1))` over business days, where the two dates before a Monday are a weekend | Returned the fold with an empty training range: 12 of the 86 folds over the weekdays of 2024 Q1. | Raises an `IsEmptyError` that names the date where the test of the fold starts. Increase `train_size`, or reduce `purged_size`. |
| A `CrossSectionalFactorPrior` that holds a `CompositeExposure` or `DerivedExposure` whose `bw` is not the `bw` of the prior | Accepted. The member read whatever field of the panel had the name of its own `bw`, or failed on a missing field. | Raises an `ArgumentError` when you build the prior. A fit on a panel that already holds a field of the name `bw` of the prior also raises, where v0.32 replaced the field. |
| A `CompositeExposure` or `DerivedExposure` that reads a blank benchmark weight on an active cell, under `strict = true` | Read the blank as zero, with no message. | Raises an `ArgumentError`. Under `strict = false` it reads the blank as zero and warns once with the count, the first observation and the assets. A blank on an inactive cell stays a silent zero. |
| `EntropyPoolingPrior` or `MeucciEntropyPoolingPrior` over a `LowDimensionEnsemblePrior`, or over a `FactorPrior` whose factor prior is one | Ran. The posterior weights met the views, but `mu` and `sigma` were those of the ensemble with no view, because the ensemble reads no weights. | Raises an `ArgumentError` when you build the estimator. |
| `EntropyPoolingPrior` or `MeucciEntropyPoolingPrior` over a factor prior or a Black-Litterman prior that holds an `OpinionPoolingPrior` with `pe1` | Ran, and the views of the entropy pooling prior missed `mu`. | Raises an `ArgumentError` when you build the estimator. Give the pool to the entropy pooling prior directly as its `pe`. |
| The first `partial_fit!` of an `EmpiricalPrior` or a `HighOrderPriorEstimator` whose member already holds a state, for example `EmpiricalPrior(; me = partial_fit!(SimpleExpectedReturns(), X1))` | Ran with no message. The warm member read its earlier rows as well as the new rows, and every other part of the prior read the new rows alone. | Raises an `ArgumentError` that names the path of the state, such as `me.cache`. Pass the member with its `cache` at `nothing`. |
| `cov` or `cor` of `ImpliedVolatility` with no `iv`, or a prior that holds one over a `ReturnsResult` with no `iv` | Raised an `UndefKeywordError`, or a `TypeError` on the keyword `iv`. | Raises an `IsNothingError` that names `iv` and the two remedies: pass `iv` to `cov` or `cor`, or fit the prior on a `ReturnsResult` that carries `iv`. |
| `cov` or `cor` of `ImpliedVolatility` with a gap in `iv` | Read past the gap and priced the asset. | Raises an `IsNonFiniteError`. |
| A named Descriptor on an asset panel whose price, market capitalisation, share count or total assets is at or below zero at an active, observed cell: `BookToPrice`, `CashFlowToPrice`, `SalesToPrice`, `EarningsToPrice`, `ForwardEarningsToPrice`, `DividendToPrice`, `ForwardDividendToPrice`, `ShareholderYield`, `MarketLeverage`, `DebtToAssets`, `GrossProfitability`, `ReturnOnAssets`, `AssetTurnover`, `CashFlowToAssets`, `AccrualsCashFlow`, `AnalystDispersionToPrice`, `ShortInterest`, `LogMarketCap`, `EarningsChangeToPrice`, `CapexToAssetsChangeInIntensity`, `EWShareTurnover` or `EWAmihudIlliquidity` | Wrote `NaN` in the cell, and the asset left the cross-section of that observation with no message. | Raises a `DomainError` that names the panel field, the observation and the asset. `PanelFieldRatio`, `PanelFieldLog`, `ChangeToScale` and `ChangeInIntensity` with no guard keep the `NaN`. The two EW presets take `gt0 = nothing` to keep it. |
| `BookLeverage` or `MarketLeverage` with a negative debt, `GrossMargin` with negative sales, or `EWShareTurnover`, `EWAmihudIlliquidity` or `DaysToCover` with a negative volume, share count, price or short interest | Gave a number or `NaN` with no message. `EWShareTurnover` read a negative volume into its state, and `DaysToCover` gave a negative value. | Raises a `DomainError`. Zero sales still give `NaN` under `GrossMargin`. `nonneg = nothing` on the EW presets and on `DaysToCover` keeps the v0.32 numbers. |
| `EWMacroSensitivity` whose reference return holds an infinite value | Ran. | Raises an `IsNonFiniteError` that names the observation. A `NaN` is still a gap that the recursion skips. |
| A batch cross-validation of more than one fold over an estimator that holds a `ReturnsTracking` | Ran. A fold whose training rows had the length of the series solved against other rows with no message. | Raises an `ArgumentError` that names the field. Use `DataBenchmarkTracking`, `WeightsTracking` or a `TimeDependent` schedule of one series per fold. |
| `partial_fit!` of an optimiser that holds a `ReturnsTracking`, or a `ReturnsTracking` whose length is not the number of rows | Raised a `DimensionMismatch` from the solver on an expanding window. Under `max_history` it ran against rows that did not align. | Raises an `ArgumentError`. An `OnlinePortfolioSelection` with a series in a risk ceiling or in a `RiskLoss` raises when you build it, where v0.32 raised at the first row. |
| A graph verb, such as `clusterise`, `phylogeny_matrix` or `centrality_vector`, over a prior result with a keyword other than `ivpa`, `branchorder`, `strict` and `dims`, for example `brt = true` | Ran, and ignored the keyword. | Raises a `MethodError`. |
| A graph verb over a prior result whose returns data `rd` has a gap in `X` | Read the `X` of the prior, which fills the gap. | Raises an `IsNonFiniteError` that names the asset. State `x_src = PriorSource()` on the estimator to read the prior. |
| A `Pipeline` with an uncertainty-set step whose target is not `:mu`, `:sigma` or `:both`, a bare uncertainty-set step, a `writes` that is not the slot of its estimator, or a nested `Pipeline` that writes a data slot again | Built, and gave the wrong slots or failed later. | Raises an `ArgumentError` when you build the pipeline. |
| `NestedClustered` whose inner optimiser holds a `TimeDependent` schedule of precomputed results, such as `LinearConstraint` values | Built. The fit raised a `DimensionMismatch` when a constraint of the whole universe reached a cluster. | Raises an `ArgumentError` when you build the estimator, as a precomputed result outside a schedule did in v0.32. |
| `fit_preprocessing` of a `MissingDataFilter` whose row filter keeps no observation of the training window | The fit ran, and `apply_preprocessing` raised the `IsEmptyError`. | The fit raises the `IsEmptyError`. Under `alg = SinglePassFilter()` the fit runs, as in v0.32. |

### [Calls that now answer](@id migration-0-33-answers)

| Call | v0.32 | v0.33 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `partial_fit!` of a prior or of an optimiser, and `optimise` or `partial_fit!` of an `OnlinePortfolioSelection` head, on a `ReturnsResult` that carries `iv` | `ArgumentError`: the online step takes no implied-volatility surface. | The step carries `iv` and `ivpa` with the returns, and `optimise` or `prior` with no data equals the batch fit over the folded rows. |
| `Posdef`, `Denoise` or `Detone` on a covariance with a constant asset, a zero variance | `ArgumentError: matrix contains Infs or NaNs` | The zero row and column stay, and the step runs on the block of the positive variances. `Denoise` reads the ratio of observations to assets of the block. |
| A factor prior, `Kurtosis` or a norm-ball set on a singular positive semidefinite covariance | `PosDefException` | The eigen square root, under the default `mtx_sqrt` |
| A prior whose covariance has a `CoveragePolicy`, on two assets that each have a variance and share too few observations for a covariance | Raised an `IsNonFiniteError` from the matrix repair, because the pair is `NaN` inside the finite block. | Warns, and answers on the other assets. The default `peel = MinimalPeel()` removes the fewest assets that leave no such pair, and each removed asset is no longer investable. `strict = true` refuses with an `ArgumentError` that names the assets. State `peel = NoPeel()` to keep the v0.32 refusal. |
| `ConditionalValueatRisk`, `EntropicValueatRisk`, `ConditionalDrawdownatRisk`, `EntropicDrawdownatRisk`, `RelativeConditionalDrawdownatRisk` or `RelativeEntropicDrawdownatRisk` with `alpha = 0` | `DomainError` | The largest loss among the observations of positive weight. Unweighted, it is the value of `WorstRealisation` or `MaximumDrawdown`. Every other tail measure still takes `0 < alpha < 1`. |
| `Fees` with a short fee `s` between `-l` and zero | `DomainError` | The short side earns the credit `-s`. `s = -l` is the linear fee `l w`. A value below `-l` is not convex, so it still raises a `DomainError`. |
| `MaximumRatio` with an ℓ1 constraint set or a fee, on a problem where the return form was infeasible | The solve failed as infeasible. | The ratio takes the risk form, so the problem solves. |
| `MaximumRatio` with `BudgetCosts` or `BudgetMarketImpact` at `ohf = 1e-2` or above | The solve failed. | The cost rows scale with the ratio variable, so every `ohf` solves to one ratio. |
| `NestedClustered`, `Stacking` or `SubsetResampling` on integer returns | `InexactError` | The weights of the returns as floating point numbers. A `Rational` return keeps its type. |
| `MultipleRandomised` with a window of all the observations, `window_size == T` | `ArgumentError: range must be non-empty` | One window of every row. |
| `EntropyPoolingPrior` or `MeucciEntropyPoolingPrior` with a view over a prior with `max_scenarios`, such as `EmpiricalPrior(; max_scenarios = 40)` or a factor prior over one | `DimensionMismatch` | The moments of the scenarios under the posterior weights. The posterior gives no weight to an observation that the cap removed. |
| `partial_fit!` of a `CrossSectionalFactorPrior`, or of an optimiser that holds one, on returns data whose asset panel holds panel fields | `ArgumentError`: the online step carries a time-varying asset panel through its masks alone. | The prior folds the rows. Under the default `carry = FoldOrRefit()`, a part that does not fold is fitted again over its rows, and `prior` or `optimise` with no data equals the batch fit over the folded rows. |
| `CrossSectionalFactorPrior` with `c` above one, for example `c = 2.0` | `DomainError` | The prior scales the orthogonal part of the return forecast by `c`. A stated `c` is finite and at least zero. |
| `exposure_coverage` or `factor_model_summary` on a `CrossSectionalFactorModel` with no regression weights `rw` | `IsNothingError` that names `rw` | The coverage over every asset. |
| `TargetReturnForecast` with a cross-validation in `cv`, on fewer than two valid samples per fold | `ArgumentError`: an out of fold calibration needs at least two samples per fold. | Under the default `warmup = NaNWarmup()` the calibration coefficient is `NaN`. `warmup = InSampleWarmup()` calibrates on the in-sample predictions. |
| A `CrossSectionalFactorPrior` on a sub-universe in which one level of a factor has no member, for example inside `NestedClustered` or `SubsetResampling` | `ArgumentError: matrix contains Infs or NaNs` | The empty factor has a zero return and no variance. |
| A `FactorPrior` whose `pe` has a scenario cap | Raised an error at the fit. | Fits on the rows that the cap keeps. |
| `factor_attribution(pred, pr)` of a `MultiPeriodPredictionResult` with a prior fitted over the whole sample | Refused. | Matches each fold to its block by a row key. The realised attribution of a block under a scenario cap answers too, where v0.32 raised a `BoundsError`. |
| `SubsetResampling` or `MultipleRandomised` on about 70 assets or more at their defaults | `OverflowError`, for example from `binomial(100, 80)` | The weights of the resampled subsets. A `Rational` `subset_size` or `window_size` answers too, where v0.32 raised a `MethodError`. |
| An `OnlinePortfolioSelection` whose fallback needs rows, such as `EqualWeighted`, `InverseVolatility`, an optimiser with an estimator prior, or `PreviousWeights(; fb = ...)`, when the fallback answers | `ArgumentError` or `IsNothingError`: the fallback got no rows, and a walk-forward stopped. | The head steps its fallback on its rows, so the fallback answers as it does in a batch fit. |
| `cross_val_predict(pipe, data, split(cv, data))` of a `Pipeline` with a combinatorial or a randomised split | A combinatorial split raised a `MethodError`. A randomised split gave one series over every asset. | The same answer as the scheme passed directly. `Resume(res)` over `MultipleRandomised` raises an `ArgumentError` that names the cause, where v0.32 raised a `MethodError`. |
| `fit_and_predict(res, prices)` of a `PipelineResult` on price data | `MethodError` | Takes the price data, as `predict(res, prices)` does. |
| A search over a field of `FactorPrior`, a Black-Litterman prior, an entropy pooling prior or a high order prior | Raised an error from `setproperties`. | Searches the field. |
| A `covariance_forecast_evaluation` step with no active asset | Refused the whole evaluation. | Keeps the step, with no score. |
| `investable_risk_gradient` on a panel that does not cover an asset | `DimensionMismatch` | The gradient over the investable assets. |
| A histogram of returns with a zero interquartile range under `FreedmanDiaconis()`, or under `Knuth()` | `FreedmanDiaconis()` raised `InexactError: Int64(Inf)`. `Knuth()` searched with no upper bound, and on such data could exhaust the memory. | `FreedmanDiaconis()` takes the range over five as the width, and `Knuth()` searches from one bin to `T` bins. A constant column has one bin. |
| `AdaptiveSubgradient` at `delta = 0` when an asset has no gradient mass yet, because each of its price relatives so far is zero or because a `RiskLoss` reads a window that does not price it | `DomainError` | Steps. The raw step leaves the weight of that asset as it is, and the projection takes the limit of a positive diagonal: the assets with mass keep their projected weights, and the others take the rest of the budget nearest the raw step. |
| `AdaptiveSubgradient`, or a `GramProjection`, on a `ProgrammeAllocationSet` when the set of investable assets changes between steps | `DimensionMismatch` | Solves over the investable assets. |
| `std(ve::SimpleVariance, state)` of a folded `SimpleVariance` | `MethodError` | The square root of `var(ve, state)`. |
| `risk_contribution` or the finite-difference `risk_gradient` with integer weights, and `risk_gradient` of a vector of measures with integer weights | `InexactError` | The contributions or the gradient, in the type of the step `delta`. |

### [Results that move](@id migration-0-33-numbers)

- **A clustering, phylogeny or centrality estimator at the default reads the returns data `rd.X`.** v0.32 read the `X` of the prior, `x_src = :prior`. The two differ when the prior changes its returns: a factor prior gives a reconstruction of rank equal to the number of factors, a prior with a scenario cap keeps fewer rows, and a prior that fills a gap writes a value where the returns data holds none. State `x_src = PriorSource()` on the graph reader to keep the v0.32 numbers. Under a prior whose moment estimators carry a `CoveragePolicy`, an asset that has a gap in the rows that the graph reader reads now gets a zero weight, and the optimiser warns once and names the asset, because the default `ce` of a graph reader has no `CoveragePolicy`. State the same `CoveragePolicy` on the `ce` of the graph reader, or `x_src = PriorSource()`, to keep the asset.
- Every regime statistic of `RegimeAdjustedExpWeightedVariance` and `RegimeAdjustedExpWeightedCovariance` divided a realised square by an estimated variance, and the inverse of an estimate is too large on average. So under a correctly calibrated model the regime multiplier read the risk too large. Under the default method the variance was 1.3 % too large at the default half-life of 40, and 5 % too large at a half-life of 10. A `MahalanobisTarget` block with fewer observations than assets gave a statistic of about `1e12`. The statistic now divides by the exact bias that its method reads. Every default fit of a `CrossSectionalFactorPrior` moves, because its `ve` is a regime-adjusted variance and its `pe` holds a regime-adjusted covariance.
- The default location of `ExpWeightedVariance`, `ExpWeightedCovariance` and the two regime-adjusted estimators started at zero and was not divided by the sum of its weights, so it estimated `(1 - λ^k)` times the mean, and its deviations kept part of the mean. The variance was 2.4 % to 36 % too large in the warm-up, and 3.5 % too large at a half-life of 10 after it. The default `EstimatedCentring()` takes the normalised exponentially weighted mean, and divides each squared deviation, or product of a pair, by its exact factor `1 + 1 / n_eff`, or `1 + c_ij`. The estimate is unbiased from the second valid return, and the first return of an asset gives no term, so an asset needs two valid returns. Under HAC the factor also reads the mean of each lagged product.
  `ZeroStartCentring()` gives the v0.32 location and its numbers. `PreCentred()` does not move. The defaults of `ce`, `ve` and `pe` of `CrossSectionalFactorPrior` use it now, but in v0.32 only `ce` did. The bullet on `CrossSectionalFactorPrior` below gives the v0.32 defaults.
- `ExpWeightedCovariance` and `RegimeAdjustedExpWeightedCovariance` divide each pair by its own weight. On a holiday, v0.32 shrank each correlation that met the holiday towards zero, and on the separate correlation path a late listing shrank each correlation of the late asset towards zero. A sample with no holiday and no late listing keeps its numbers.
- The two regime-adjusted estimators clamp the multiplier of an estimate only. v0.32 also clamped the multiplier of one before `regime_min_obs`, and the multiplier of `regime_method = nothing`, so a clamp with a lower bound above one inflated a variance that had no regime estimate.
- `RegimeAdjustedExpWeightedCovariance` with a separate `cor_decay` reports the variance `0` for a constant asset, where v0.32 reported `min_val`.
- **Every default fit of a `CrossSectionalFactorPrior` moves.** On one panel of 60 assets and 300 observations, `mu` moved by 82 % of its largest entry and `sigma` by 16 %. The new defaults that [Renamed keywords and changed defaults](@ref migration-0-33-renames) lists cause most of it. These corrections also move a fit, and no keyword undoes them:
  - The idiosyncratic variance and the regime statistic read the active mask and the estimation mask. An asset that delisted and listed again kept its variance over the inactive stretch in v0.32, and the regime statistic read active assets outside the estimation universe.
  - The prior divides the weights of the scenarios of its factor prior by their sum.
  - Each asset reads the factor moments only on the support of its loadings, so a factor with no moment changes no asset with a zero loading on it, and the investable set holds each asset whose loadings read only factors with moments. The fit refuses only when no asset has such loadings.
  - An asset in the warm-up of its variance has a finite mean and finite systematic covariances. v0.32 wrote `NaN` over its whole mean, row and column. The asset is still not investable, because its variance is not finite.
  - The idiosyncratic correlation under a threshold `th` above zero reads the residuals with no fill. v0.32 read the residuals that the scenarios read, which fill a holiday or a late listing with the mean of the other assets, and so correlated the asset with each of them.
  - A return forecast with `min_obs = 1` gives a forecast earlier in the history, and `TargetReturnForecast` calibrates prequentially. See the two bullets on them above.
- **An unseen member of a constrained factor family has a return of zero.** The exposures lag the returns, so the zero-sum condition of an observation reads the benchmark weights of the observation before it. When the last asset of a member leaves the sample, the condition still weights the member, and the design is rank deficient. In v0.32 the solve algorithm of `cre` then gave the member a return that depended on the dropped member of the family. Under `unseen = ZeroUnseenMember()` the member has a return of zero at that observation, and the condition holds over the other members. The factor returns, `mu` and `sigma` move only on a panel with such an observation. State `unseen = SolvedUnseenMember()` to keep the v0.32 numbers.
- **A `CrossSectionalFactorPrior` reads the residual of an asset that is alone in its level as a gap.** When an asset is the only member of its level of a one-hot factor at an observation, the regression fits its return exactly, so its residual is zero whatever the return. v0.32 divided that zero by the variance of the asset, so an asset with a positive variance got a scaled residual of zero, and an asset with a zero variance got the mean scaled residual of its observation. v0.33 gives every such asset that mean in the scenarios `X`, and the idiosyncratic correlation leaves it out, so `sigma` moves under a threshold `th` above zero. A design in which every such asset has a zero variance keeps its numbers, and so does a design with no such asset. State `l1mask = UnmaskedLeverageOne()` to keep the v0.32 numbers.
- **The chi-squared radius of a full covariance set reads `N(N + 1) / 2` degrees of freedom**, the free entries of a symmetric matrix. This moves the Normal ellipsoid, the Normal norm ball and the bootstrap ellipsoid under `diagonal = false`. v0.32 read `N²`, so the set was too large for its level: at `N = 5` and `q = 0.05` the radius was `6.136` and now is `5.000`, and the old set covered 0.999 of the errors at a stated 0.95. A bootstrap ellipsoid with `M` resamples reads at most `M - 1` degrees of freedom, on both axes. To keep the v0.32 numbers, give the radius as a number, `method = sqrt(quantile(Chisq(N^2), 1 - q))`. The default `diagonal = true` and the bootstrap norm ball do not move.
- **The view of a `CompactCovarianceUncertaintySet` is the projection of the set.** A hierarchical or clustered optimiser takes this view of a set that you fit before. v0.32 fitted the basis again on the view, so a cluster portfolio paid less than the full set charges it, by 6.9 % and 14.9 % of the penalty on two examples. Now it pays what the full set charges it. An `OrthogonalUncertaintySet` estimator inside the optimiser still fits on the data of the cluster.
- **A covariance or a correlation with a `CoveragePolicy` peels the assets of an undetermined pair.** v0.32 wrote `NaN` at the cell of a pair whose assets share too few observations, and kept both assets. v0.33 removes the assets that the `peel` rule chooses, writes `NaN` across the row and the column of each, and warns with their indices. The mean and the variance do not move. A call with no such pair keeps its numbers. On a fold, the peel runs each time the folded estimator answers, on the counts folded so far, so a walk-forward warns at each step that has such a pair, and the removed set can change from one step to the next. State `peel = NoPeel()` to keep the v0.32 numbers.
- **The default leaf order is the exact optimal order.** `clusterise`, the phylogeny estimators, DBHT and every hierarchical and meta-optimiser at the default `branchorder = :optimal` can give another order of the leaves. The order is the input of the recursive bisection, so the weights of `HierarchicalRiskParity` and of the Schur complement optimiser can move. The clusters and every cut do not move. State `branchorder = :barjoseph` to keep the v0.32 numbers.
- **An empirical Value at Risk reads the upper `alpha`-quantile of the returns.** `ValueatRisk` and `ValueatRiskRange` under the default `MIPValueatRisk()`, `DrawdownatRisk` and `RelativeDrawdownatRisk` take the order statistic at position `floor(alpha T) + 1`, the definition of Equation 7.50 of [cajas2025](@cite). v0.32 took the position `floor((alpha - s) T) + 1` with the slack `s = 1e-5`. The two positions differ when `alpha T` is an integer, for example at the default `alpha = 0.05` on 100, 200 or 1000 observations, where the value moves by one order statistic: on 100 normal returns from `1.727` to `1.596`. They also differ when the fractional part of `alpha T` is below `1e-5 T`, so on 100 000 observations or more the value moves at every `alpha`. The risk of the MIP model moves in the same way, so the weights of an optimiser that reads one of these measures can move.
  With observation weights, the position is the first one whose cumulative weight exceeds `alpha` times the total weight, where v0.32 took the first one that reached it. The default `s` is now `1e-9`, and the cardinality row of the MIP adds it to `alpha`, where v0.32 subtracted it. The conditional measures do not move. No keyword gives the v0.32 position.
- **`MaximumRatio` with a variance on the lifted weight matrix maximises the Sharpe ratio.** This is a `Variance` that the model states on the lifted matrix `W`, or a box, ellipsoid or norm-ball `UncertaintySetVariance`. v0.32 divided the excess return by the variance, not by its square root, so the weights moved. Risk contribution rows `rc` under `MaximumRatio` now relax as they do under `MinimumRisk`. State `ratio_alg = LinearRatio()` to keep the v0.32 ratio. It gives the v0.32 weights to about `2e-4`.
- **A weighted `LinearModel` or `GeneralisedLinearModel` scales its weights to their Kish count.** GLM reads weights as frequencies, so v0.32 read probability weights that sum to one as one observation: every p-value was 1, and the AIC and the BIC read `n = 1`. A `StepwiseRegression` inside an entropy pooling or opinion pooling prior then kept one factor for each asset. The coefficients do not move, but the selected factor sets, the p-values, the AIC and the BIC do. `FrequencyWeights` keep the v0.32 numbers.
- **`CombinatorialCrossValidation` puts every trailing row in the last fold.** When `mod(T, n_folds) >= div(T, n_folds)`, v0.32 put a trailing row in no fold, so the row was in every training set and in no test set: row 13 of 13 at `n_folds = 5`. The splits move in that case only.
- **The factor attribution of a held asset in the warm-up of its variance.** Under the default `unknown = EntrywiseUnknown()` its exposures and its systematic numbers stay, and each number that reads the unknown variance is `NaN`, where v0.32 set the whole row to zero. The split and the standard error of a pair of leverage one are `NaN`, and a pair with no idiosyncratic return adds nothing to any component. A static loadings matrix reads every observation at any lag, and `trim = true` cuts it. A held asset with no return on an active cell is a holiday. A held asset that is not active warns, or raises under `strict = true`. The predicted totals set to zero the row of an asset that is not investable. No keyword gives the v0.32 numbers.
- **The readers of an asset panel read only the active, observed cells.** `FeatureDistance` reads each asset and each pair at its own active rows, and an asset that it cannot read leaves the investable set at a fit. The panel collapse of `NestedClustered` and `Stacking` weighs only the members that are active at each observation, under `pcol = RenormaliseActive()`. A panel with no inactive and no unobserved cell keeps its numbers.
- **A `RiskLoss` reads the last `window` rows** when its head holds more rows. A tracking or turnover ceiling in a `ProgrammeAllocationSet` with `fixed = false` follows the allocation of each step, where v0.32 held the weights that you passed.
- **A forecast evaluation keeps the weights of its book after a date with no asset that it can score**, so the turnover of the book moves.
- **`ForwardPanelFill` and `BackwardPanelFill` stop at the edge of a stretch in which the asset is not active.** v0.32 walked each column over the whole axis, so an asset that delisted and listed again took, at a blank first cell of its second listing, the last value of its first listing, and `lim` counted the inactive cells as part of the run. A column with one stretch of activity keeps its numbers.
- **`opt_weight_bounds` with a `JuMPWeightFinaliser` leaves your weight vector unchanged.** Under the two relative formulations v0.32 wrote `eps` into each zero entry of the vector that you passed: `[0.7, 0.3, 0.0]` became `[0.7, 0.3, 2.2e-16]`.
- **The cross-sectional diagnostics return a `FactorDiagnosticResult`.** `exposure_coverage` and the other diagnostic verbs over a `CrossSectionalFactorModel` returned a `Vector` in v0.32. The vector is now the field `X` of the result, which also holds the names and the families of the factors.
- **`prices_to_returns` computes each return from the change in price,** `(p_t - p_{t-1}) / p_{t-1}`, and the log return as `log1p` of it. v0.32 took the simple return through a logarithm and lost about three bits, and its log return cancelled. Each return can move in its last bits, and so can each value computed from it.
- **`MaximumRatio` with an ℓ1 constraint set or a fee takes the risk form.** Both forms state one optimum, so a problem that solved in v0.32 keeps its ratio, and its weights can move within the tolerance of the solver.
- **`MaximumRatio` with `BudgetCosts` or `BudgetMarketImpact`** solves for weights that no longer move with `ohf`. v0.32 gave `sum(w) + cost` of 0.877 at `ohf = 2.5e-4` and 0.970 at `ohf = 1e-3`, where the budget states 1.
- **`BudgetMarketImpact` under a constraint scale `sc` other than one.** The power cone of the charge did not scale its middle entry, so the charge grew by `sc^((1 - beta) / beta)`, and the weights stayed near the start portfolio `w`. At the default `sc` nothing moves.
- **The mean of `BodnarOkhrinParolya`** divides its `beta` by the quadratic form of the target, as equation 7 of [bodnar2019](@cite) states, where v0.32 divided by that of the sample mean. Every target of the library is a multiple of the vector of ones, so the three targets now give the same estimate.
- **The monotonic search of the Schur complement optimiser** refines below `tol` and reaches the boundary where the augmented block stops being positive definite, so its `gamma` can move to a value of lower variance. A repaired block is written back after the repair, so a later split augments the repaired block.
- **`UlcerIndex` and `RelativeUlcerIndex` read the weights of the prior,** as every other drawdown measure does. Under a weighted prior the index moves. Under no weights it does not.
- **The blended inverse-variance weights of a `CrossSectionalFactorPrior` with zero variances** take the limit as those variances go to zero. With half or more of the variances of an observation zero, v0.32 dropped the inverse-variance part of the row, so the row summed to `1 - lambda`, and with every variance zero it took the cap weights alone. A quantile level of the clamp that lands on an order statistic beside a zero variance gave no bound, and now gives that order statistic.
- **`MultipleRandomised`** draws each start of a window, the last one included. A seeded draw can move.
- **An entropy pooling prior over a factor prior keeps the model of the fit that its views read.** `EntropyPoolingPrior` and `MeucciEntropyPoolingPrior` over a `FactorPrior` or a `FactorBlackLittermanPrior` fitted the regression again under the posterior weights, so the scenarios moved, and a mean view missed `mu`: a view of `0.004` read `0.0040224` on one example. The posterior now weighs the same scenarios and keeps the loadings, the intercepts and the residual variances, so each mean view holds on `mu`. A nested entropy pooling prior or opinion pool keeps its model and does not solve its views again.
- **A high order prior fits its coskewness and cokurtosis under the weights of the prior that it wraps.** Over an entropy pooling prior or an opinion pool, `HighOrderPriorEstimator` and `HighOrderFactorPriorEstimator` fitted them with no weights, so a skewness or kurtosis view reached no co-moment. `HighOrderFactorPriorEstimator` also takes off the residual variances that the prior added, where it measured them again. Under equal weights nothing moves.
- **A `prior(...)` reference in a skewness or kurtosis view reads the weights of the prior.** In `EntropyPoolingPrior` and `MeucciEntropyPoolingPrior`, v0.32 read the skewness or the kurtosis of the reference from the scenarios with no weights. It now reads them under the prior probabilities `w` with `H0_EntropyPooling`, and under the weights of the prior of the stage with `H1_EntropyPooling` and `H2_EntropyPooling`, as a value at risk reference does. A view such as `sk_views = LinearConstraintEstimator(; val = "A1 == prior(A1) * 1.2")` then states a different target, so the posterior moves.
- **The covariance of an entropy pooling or opinion pooling posterior takes the correction of reliability weights,** `1 / (1 - sum(p .^ 2))`, where v0.32 took `T / (T - 1)`. Under a view, `sigma` grows by the factor `(n_eff / (n_eff - 1)) / (T / (T - 1))`, with `n_eff = 1 / sum(p .^ 2)`: by `2.2e-3` with one mean view on 60 observations. The posterior weights are `AnalyticWeights`. Give the prior `w = pweights(...)` to keep the v0.32 correction. With no view nothing moves.
- **`MissingDataFilter` reads the column shares again over the observations that its row filter keeps, until no asset drops.** v0.32 read them one time, over the whole training window. So when `row_thr` dropped an observation, a kept asset could miss more than `col_thr` of the observations that stayed. The universe moves only when both thresholds are below one and the row filter drops an observation. `alg = SinglePassFilter()` gives the v0.32 universe.
- **A `DateWalkForward` drops a fold whose test window holds no row.** Two dates of the range map to one row when the data has no row between them, for example the two days of a weekend under `period = Day(1)` over business days. v0.32 returned and counted such a fold: `DateWalkForward(5, 1; period = Day(1))` over the weekdays of 2024 Q1 gave 83 folds, and 24 of them tested no row. v0.33 gives the 59 folds that test a row, and `n_splits` counts them.
- **A date of a `DateWalkForward` range after the last timestamp ends the range under `previous = true` too.** A positive `period_offset`, or an `adjuster`, can make such a date. v0.32 mapped it to the last row, so the last fold counted as full and its test window left out the last row: `DateWalkForward(2, 1; period = Day(3), period_offset = Day(2), previous = true)` over 20 daily rows tested `[7:9, 10:12, 13:15, 16:18, 19:19]`. v0.33 tests `[7:9, 10:12, 13:15, 16:18]`, as v0.32 did under `previous = false`. State `reduce_test = true` to test the last rows, `19:20`.
- **`DimensionReductionRegression` fixes the sign of each component.** Each column of the loadings `L` whose sum is negative changes sign, so the fit no longer depends on the sign that the projection returns. v0.32 kept that sign. The factor risk contributions and the span of the loadings do not move. The `"loadings"` field of a `RegressionPanel`, and the factor weights that `FactorRiskBudgeting` and `FactorRiskContribution` read under this regression, can move.
- **`AdaptiveSubgradient` takes the step of a zero gradient in its own norm.** A `RiskLoss` gives a zero gradient while the head holds fewer than two rows. v0.32 took the Euclidean projection of the weights at such a step. v0.33 projects in `diag(delta + s)`. While no asset has mass that norm is a multiple of the identity matrix, so the step is the Euclidean projection, except on a `ProgrammeAllocationSet` with a penalty in its objective: there the weight of the penalty against the distance changes, and at `delta = 0` the step minimises the penalty and takes the weights nearest the raw step among its minimisers.
- **A projection in the entropic, Tsallis or log-barrier geometry on a `ProgrammeAllocationSet` sets each weight at or below `sqrt(eps)` to its floor**, zero unless the set states a positive floor. v0.32 raised only a negative weight to zero, so the sign of the rounding of the solver decided whether a weight that the set forces to zero could recover. Such a weight is now zero whatever the sign of the rounding, and it stays at zero.
- **`risk_contribution` and the finite-difference `risk_gradient` keep their step under a narrow weight type.** v0.32 rounded the step to the type of the weights: with `Float32` weights each contribution was about 1.3 % off, and with `Float16` weights each contribution was zero. v0.33 returns the vector in the type of the weights promoted with the step `delta`, a `Float64` by default.
- **`EntropyPoolingPrior` and `MeucciEntropyPoolingPrior` leave the weights `w` that you pass as they are.** v0.32 normalised a mutable `w` in place when you built the prior, so `v = [1.0, 3.0]; EntropyPoolingPrior(; w = pweights(v))` changed `v` to `[0.25, 0.75]`. v0.33 builds new weights of the same kind. The prior does not move.
- **A `w` that you pass to an entropy pooling prior over a nested pooling prior is the prior of the innermost scenarios.** In `EntropyPoolingPrior` and `MeucciEntropyPoolingPrior` the nested prior solves its views from your `w`, and the outer prior solves its own views from the nested posterior, as it does when you pass no `w`. v0.32 solved the outer views from your `w`, so the views of the nested prior did not reach the posterior. A nested prior that does not reweight its scenarios, such as `EmpiricalPrior`, keeps your `w`, and its result does not move.

### [If you extend the library](@id migration-0-33-extending)

- **A centring, a debias rule, a HAC floor, a rule for the timing of the volatility, a rule for the volatility of the separate correlation path, a rule for an indefinite block or a rule of the level recursion of the Mahalanobis target of your own** subtypes `AbstractCentring`, `AbstractRegimeDebias`, `AbstractHacFloor`, `AbstractHacVolatilityTiming`, `AbstractCorrelationVolatility`, `AbstractIndefiniteBlockRule` or `AbstractLevelRecursion`, which are public. A centring implements the verbs that the `# Interfaces` section of `AbstractCentring` names. One of them, `centring_law`, names the law of the deviations that the bias tables of a regime-adjusted estimator read: `PreCentred()` or `EstimatedCentring()`. A centring with a law of its own returns itself, and adds a method of `regime_bias_table` and of `mahalanobis_level_bias`, which are public, for that law.
  A debias rule implements `debiases` and `reads_dependence`, a HAC floor implements `hac_floor!`, a timing rule implements `volatility_before_update`, a rule for the volatility of the separate path implements `correlation_variance`, a rule for an indefinite block implements `indefinite_block`, and a rule of the level recursion implements `mahalanobis_recursion_ratio`.

- **A prior of your own** states its source with a method of `returns_source`, which is now public. A prior with no method reads the raw returns.
- **`LowOrderPrior`** has the fields `x_src`, `ss_gaps` and `pc` after `fpr`, so its positional constructor takes three more arguments. `prior` over a `ReturnsResult`, and the result that an online fold gives, record in `x_src` the source of the prior. A returns-matrix method records `nothing`. A Prior Result of your own records no source, and an optimiser reads its returns as raw.
- **`CoveragePolicy`** has a field `peel` after `alg`, so its positional constructor takes three arguments. The keyword constructor does not change. A peel rule of your own subtypes `AbstractPeel`, which is public, and implements `peel_assets`.
- **`CrossSectionalFactorPrior`** has twelve more fields, so its positional constructor takes 31 arguments, where v0.32 took 19. The new fields are `unseen` after `families`, `pdm` after `ce`, `srep` after `mp`, and `ofit`, `l1mask`, `lx`, `mtx_sqrt`, `ex`, `choice`, `carry`, `cache` and `x_src` after `c`. A method that dispatches on the position of a type parameter after `families` moves. The keyword constructor keeps every v0.32 keyword.
- **The thirteen estimators that take `mtx_sqrt`**, and `NormalUncertaintySet`, which takes `dc`, each have one more field, so their positional constructors take one more argument. `Variance` and `UncertaintySetVariance` also have `ratio_alg` and `corner` after `mtx_sqrt`, so they take three more. The keyword constructors do not change.
- **`CompactCovarianceUncertaintySet`** has a field `R` after `Q`, the factor of the rows that a view drops. A fitted set has no row in it. Write `CompactCovarianceUncertaintySet(; kappa, C, Q, val)` where v0.32 wrote the four positional arguments `(kappa, C, Q, val)`. A consumer of your own reads the penalty `min_z ‖C w − Q z‖² + ‖R z‖²`.
- **A radius algorithm of your own** subtypes `AbstractUncertaintyKAlgorithm` and receives the dimension of the set as a fifth argument `df`. An ellipsoid calls `k_ucs` and a norm ball calls `k_norm_ball`, which is now public, so an algorithm that a norm ball reads implements both.
- **`UlcerIndex` and `RelativeUlcerIndex`** have a field `w` after `settings`, so their positional constructors take two arguments. The keyword constructors do not change.
- **A rule for the diagonal of the Normal covariance set** subtypes `AbstractDiagonalConstruction` and implements `diagonal_sigma_shape`, which are public. **A square-root algorithm** subtypes `AbstractMatrixSquareRootAlgorithm`, which is public, and implements `matrix_square_root`.
- **A regression target of your own** subtypes `AbstractRegressionTarget`, which is now public, and implements the verbs that its `# Interfaces` section names. `CrossSectionalTargetRegression` calls `factory(tgt, w)` at each observation, and a target with no such method raises an `ArgumentError` that names the method. v0.32 called the generic `factory`, which returned the target unchanged, so the fit ignored the cross-sectional weights with no message. `StepwiseRegression` and `DimensionReductionRegression` take a target of your own, where v0.32 read `tgt.kwargs` and raised a `FieldError` when you built the estimator. `DimensionReductionRegression` reads the weights of the target with `regression_target_weights`, which is public.
- **A matrix processing estimator of your own** that a factor prior repairs under implements `matrix_processing_posdef`, which is public. It gives the positive definite matrix estimator of the repair, or `nothing` to skip it. v0.32 read the field `pdm` of the estimator by name. `CrossSectionalFactorPrior`, and `FactorPrior` and `FactorBlackLittermanPrior` with `rsd = true`, now raise an `ArgumentError` when you build them over a type with no method.
- **A cokurtosis estimator of your own** in `HighOrderFactorPriorEstimator` implements `cokurtosis_processor`, which is public, to give its matrix processing estimator, and `cokurtosis_expected_returns` for `rsd = true`. v0.32 read the field `mp` of the estimator by name, so a type with an `mp` field was processed, and a type with no `mp` field raised a `FieldError` when it was fitted. `HighOrderFactorPriorEstimator` now raises an `ArgumentError` when you build it over a type with no `cokurtosis_processor` method, under every `rsd`. A method that returns `nothing` skips the processing and, under `rsd = true`, the repair.
- **`ReturnsResult`** has the fields `ne` and `E`, the exogenous series, after `B`, so its positional constructor takes 12 arguments, where v0.32 took 10. `PredictionReturnsResult` has `ne` and `E` too, and `PricesResult` has `E`. The keyword constructors do not change.
- **More fields, so more positional arguments.** These types of v0.32 have new fields, and their keyword constructors keep every v0.32 keyword:
  - The fourteen covariance estimators `CorrelationCovariance`, `DistanceCovariance`, `GeodesicShrinkageCovariance`, `ImpliedVolatility`, `LowerTailDependenceCovariance`, `MutualInfoCovariance`, `RankOneCovariance`, `GerberCovariance`, `GerberIQCovariance`, `SmythBrobyCovariance`, `KendallCovariance`, `SpearmanCovariance`, `WindowedCovariance` and `WindowedVariance`, and the windowed expected returns, coskewness and cokurtosis, have `cache`.
  - `RegimeAdjustedExpWeightedVariance` has `hac_floor` and `debias`. `RegimeAdjustedExpWeightedCovariance` has `hac_floor`, `hac_vol_before`, `cor_vol` and `debias`. In both, and in `ExpWeightedVariance` and `ExpWeightedCovariance`, `centring` replaces `centred`. `PortfolioTarget` has `indefinite`.
  - `CrossSectionalLinearRegression` has `ex`, and `CrossSectionalTargetRegression` has `alg` and `ex`. `StepwiseRegression` has `choice`, `included` and `x_src`, and `DimensionReductionRegression` has `choice`, `proj` and `x_src`.
  - `DescriptorScores` has `nw` after `neutralise`, and `ex`. `TargetReturnForecast` has `warmup` after `cv`, and `intercept`.
  - The Descriptors that fold have `cache`. `PanelFieldRatio`, `PanelFieldLog`, `ChangeToScale` and `ChangeInIntensity` have `gt0`. `EWVolumeRatio` has `nonneg` and `gt0`, and `DaysToCover` has `nonneg`. Each new guard defaults to no guard. `EWMacroSensitivity` has `series` after `mcap`.
  - The results `CrossSectionalFactorModel` (`unseen`, `fx`, `fr`, `lambda`, `c`, `idx`, `ts`, `amr` and `hist`), `CrossSectionalRegression` (`h1`, `dc` and `lv`), `Regression` (`idx` and `ts`), `PredictionResult` (`idx`), `TargetReturnForecastResult` (`ocalib`), `FactorSummaryResult` (`nf` and `fam`) and `CovarianceForecastSummaryResult` (`step_weighting`).
  - `MissingDataFilter` has `alg` after `row_thr`, so its positional constructor takes four arguments.
- **A new order of the fields, so a new order of the positional arguments.** Four types of v0.32 list their fields in a new order that wastes no padding, and their keyword constructors do not change. `HindsightSplit` takes `start` before `prefix`. It has seven fields, as in v0.32, so a v0.32 positional call such as `HindsightSplit(true, 21, nothing, nothing, nothing, false, false)` still builds a split, with `start = true` and `prefix = 21`, and raises a `TypeError` only when the split runs. Write the call with keywords. `JuMPOptimiser` has `xbgt` after `linf`, `FactorBlackLittermanPrior` has `rsd` after `ve`, and `TargetReturnForecast` has `whole_history` and `calibrate` after `unit`.

## From v0.33 to v0.34

### [Names that were removed](@id migration-0-34-removed)

- **`AllocationSetConstraint`.** The re-solve of a `FollowTheLeader` rule with a JuMP leader passes the allocation set of the head, the set in which every allocation of the head lies, as a second block of the entry of the leader, and the type is gone. A custom constraint of yours that wrapped a set for a JuMP head of your own passes the set to the entry instead: `optimise(opt, rd; blocks = ((factory(set, w), X),))`, with the rows `X` that the set reads. `append_custom_constraint`, the prefix `:aset_` and the rule "state a kind on the set or on the held optimiser, not on both" go with it. A kind on both now gives two constraints.
- **`reduce_loadings` of a stack.** Write `reduce_exposures(fcb, Ms)` for a stack of loading matrices, `assets × factors × observations`. `reduce_loadings(fcb, M, t)` takes one loading matrix alone, and a stack raises a `MethodError`. The numbers do not move.
- **`expand_factor_mu`.** Write `expand_factor_returns(fcb, mu, t)` for a factor mean vector `mu` at observation `t`. Without `t`, the vector takes the ratios of the last observation, as before. Write `expand_factor_returns(fcb, M)` for a matrix `M` that holds one mean per observation, one per row. `expand_factor_returns` takes both forms, and the numbers do not move. A call of `expand_factor_mu` raises an `UndefVarError`.
- **`factory_child`.** Write `factory(x, args...; kwargs...)`. `factory` is a traversal, so it returns every value that has no method of its own with no change, as `factory_child` did: a data field, a number, `nothing`, a set of observation weights and a covariance estimator of `StatsBase`. It rebuilds an array of estimators, algorithms, results or covariance estimators element by element, in an array of the same shape, and it returns an array of data with no change. A call of `factory_child` raises an `UndefVarError`.
- **`orthogonal_forecast_result` and `orthogonal_forecast_step`.** An orthogonal forecast fit of your own states the regression that the member reads, with `orthogonal_forecast_regression(ofit::MyRule, cre)`, which returns `cre` or `nothing`. The prior calls `return_forecast(rfe, rd, csfm, orthogonal_forecast_regression(ofit, cre))` in the batch fit, and `return_forecast_step` with the same last argument in the carry fold. A rule that changes the fit of the member in another way returns its own return forecast estimator from `orthogonal_forecast_member`. Write `return_forecast(rfe, rd, csfm, cre)` where v0.33 wrote `orthogonal_forecast_result(OrthogonalPartCalibration(), rfe, rd, csfm, cre)`. The numbers do not move. A call of either removed name raises an `UndefVarError`.
- **The internal steps of the exponentially weighted beta Descriptors.** `EWBlockState`, `ew_beta_series`, `ew_beta_series!`, `ew_beta_reset!`, `ew_beta_output`, `ew_agg_series`, `ew_agg_vector`, `ew_beta_expand`, `ew_block_rows`, `ew_block_state`, `ew_block_copy`, `ew_beta_residual_variance`, `ew_active_returns`, `ew_macro_sensitivity_state`, `ew_macro_sensitivity_series!` and `ew_macro_carried` are gone. Write `ew_beta_state` and `ew_regression_series!` for the regression of the returns on the market, write `PortfolioOptimisers.CarriedReference(st)` for the counts that an `EWMacroSensitivityState` carries, and read `EWBetaState`, `EWMacroSensitivityState` and `EWDownsideBetaState` for the carried states. See [If you extend the library](@ref migration-0-34-extending).
- **`OrderedWeightsArrayConditionalValueatRiskRange` and `OrderedWeightsArrayTailGiniRange`.** Write `OWA_CVaR_RG(; alpha, beta)` where v0.33 wrote `OrderedWeightsArray(; w = OrderedWeightsArrayConditionalValueatRiskRange(; alpha, beta))`, and `OWA_TG_RG(; alpha_i, alpha, a_sim, beta_i, beta, b_sim)` for the tail Gini range. The two functions take the keywords and the defaults of v0.33, and they return an `OrderedWeightsArrayRange`, where v0.33 returned an `OrderedWeightsArray`. To build the range yourself, give the gain tail reversed: `OrderedWeightsArrayRange(; w1 = OrderedWeightsArrayConditionalValueatRisk(; alpha), w2 = reverse ∘ OrderedWeightsArrayConditionalValueatRisk(; alpha = beta))`, and the same with two `OrderedWeightsArrayTailGini` tails. `owa_cvarrg` and `owa_tgrg` still give the weights of each range. A call of either removed name raises an `UndefVarError`.
- **`StandardisedHighOrderMoment`.** Write `HighOrderMoment(; alg, ve)` where v0.33 wrote `HighOrderMoment(; alg = StandardisedHighOrderMoment(; ve, alg))`. The default `ve` of the wrapper was `SimpleVariance(; me = nothing)`, so `HighOrderMoment(; alg = FourthMoment(), ve = SimpleVariance(; me = nothing))` gives the v0.33 value of `HighOrderMoment(; alg = StandardisedHighOrderMoment(; alg = FourthMoment()))`. The default `ve = nothing` of `HighOrderMoment` gives the raw moment, as v0.33 did with no wrapper. The numbers do not move. A call of the removed name raises an `UndefVarError`.
- **Eleven singletons that stated a skipped step.** Write `nothing` where v0.33 wrote the singleton: `NoHacFloor()` in `hac_floor`, `RawStatistic()` in `debias` and `RawVolatility()` in `cor_vol` of the regime-adjusted moments, `NoPeel()` in `peel` of `CoveragePolicy`, `NoSlack()` in `slack` of `PassiveAggressiveMeanReversion`, `PlainGradient()` in `grad` of `MirrorDescent`, `ExponentiatedGradient` and `GradientProjection`, `NoPanelFill()` in `alg` of a panel input, `IdentityScaling()` in `scaling` and `IdentityMetric()` in `metric` of `OrthogonalUncertaintySet`, `IdentityMetric()` in `weighting` of the forecast evaluation, `NoSystematicRepair()` in `srep` of `CrossSectionalFactorPrior`, and `NoDecay()` in `decay` of `Proximity`. Where the singleton was the default, the default is now `nothing`. The numbers do not move. A call of a removed name raises an `UndefVarError`.
- **`AbstractLastObservationAlgorithm`, and `LastObservation` as a holder.** `LastObservation` is an abstract type with the two members `LastRow` and `LastActiveRow`. It is `public`, and it is not exported. Write `LastRow()` where v0.33 wrote `LastObservation()`, and `LastActiveRow()` where v0.33 wrote `LastObservation(; alg = LastActiveRow())`. `AbstractLastObservationAlgorithm`, the bound of the old field `alg`, is gone. A call of `PortfolioOptimisers.LastObservation()` raises a `MethodError`, and the bare name `LastObservation` raises an `UndefVarError`.
- **The sequential and the grid tail views of entropy pooling.** Write `SequentialTailView(; iters, tol)` where v0.33 wrote `SequentialConditionalValueatRiskView`, `SequentialEntropicValueatRiskView` or `SequentialRelativisticValueatRiskView` with the same keywords. Write `GridTailView(; pct, K, M, iters, tol, tilt_iters)` where v0.33 wrote `GridEntropicValueatRiskView` or `GridRelativisticValueatRiskView` with the same keywords. The tail measure of the view selects the formulation, so one type serves each measure. The numbers do not move. A call of a removed name raises an `UndefVarError`.
- **The four error weight finalisers.** Write `ErrorWeightFinaliser()` where v0.33 wrote `RelativeErrorWeightFinaliser()`, `ErrorWeightFinaliser(; alg = L2Norm())` for `SquaredRelativeErrorWeightFinaliser()`, `ErrorWeightFinaliser(; dev = AbsoluteWeightDeviation())` for `AbsoluteErrorWeightFinaliser()`, and `ErrorWeightFinaliser(; alg = L2Norm(), dev = AbsoluteWeightDeviation())` for `SquaredAbsoluteErrorWeightFinaliser()`. The model of a `DiscreteAllocation` registers the error row as `cerr`, where v0.33 registered `cabs_err`, `csqabs_err`, `crel_err` or `csqrel_err`. The weights and the shares do not move. `alg` also takes `LInfNorm()` and `LpNorm(; p)` with `p > 1`, and `LpNorm(; p = 1)` raises a `DomainError`. A call of a removed name raises an `UndefVarError`.
- **`OptimisticStep`.** Write `MirrorDescent(; predictor = LastGradient())` where v0.33 wrote `OptimisticStep()`, and `GradientProjection(; eta, predictor)` where v0.33 wrote `OptimisticStep(; alg = GradientProjection(; eta), predictor)`. The predictor is a field of the rule, and its default `nothing` gives no optimistic step. A transform of the gradient, such as `GradientMomentum()`, beside a predictor raises an `ArgumentError`, as the wrapper refused it. The paths do not move. A call of the removed name raises an `UndefVarError`.
- **`OptimisationCrossValidation`.** `NestedClustered` and `Stacking` hold `cv` and `scorer` as two fields. Write `NestedClustered(; cv, scorer, …)` where v0.33 wrote `NestedClustered(; cv = OptimisationCrossValidation(; cv, scorer), …)`, and the same for `Stacking`. The default of the wrapper was `cv = KFold()`, so write `cv = KFold()` where v0.33 wrote `cv = OptimisationCrossValidation()`. The weights do not move. [Calls that now raise](@ref migration-0-34-raises) lists the pairs that the constructors refuse. A call of the removed name raises an `UndefVarError`.
- **`ElasticNetPath`.** The elastic net is a regression target. Write `ElasticNet(; theta, strength = MiddleOfPath(; ratio), iters, tol)` where v0.33 wrote `ElasticNetPath(; theta, ratio, iters, tol)`, and `KernelTrendPattern(; tgt)` where v0.33 wrote `KernelTrendPattern(; path)`. The defaults are those of v0.33, so `ElasticNet()` fits as `ElasticNetPath()` did. `KernelTrendPatternTracking` also takes `ratio`. On 200 problems the coefficients do not move. `KernelTrendPattern()` does not move at `theta < 1` on the cases measured, and at `theta = 1` it moves by `1e-14` relative at most. As a target, `ElasticNet` also enters `StepwiseRegression` under `crit = :aic`, `:aicc` or `:bic`, and `DimensionReductionRegression`. A call of the removed name raises an `UndefVarError`, and the keyword `path` raises a `MethodError`.
- **The keyword and field `ucs_flag` of `NearOptimalCentering`, and the `flag` slot of `no_bounds_risk_measure` and `no_bounds_no_risk_expr_risk_measure`.** The copies without bounds that the anchors and the unconstrained centring model read keep the uncertainty sets of the risk measures and of the return terms. `expected_risk` of an `UncertaintySetVariance` and `expected_return` of an `ArithmeticReturn` with a fitted set give the worst case over the set, so each anchor is a value of the expression that the model bounds.
  With `ucs_flag = false`, v0.33 solved the anchors of minimum risk and of maximum return on the nominal moments. It computed the bounds of the region with the sets, and the centring model of `UnconstrainedNearOptimalCentering` read the nominal moments. So the bounds of the region did not match the expressions of its model. The removal does not move the numbers of the default `ucs_flag = true`. A return term with a fitted set moves the anchors, as [Results that move](@ref migration-0-34-numbers) states for `expected_return`. To centre on the nominal moments, write `Variance()` in place of the `UncertaintySetVariance`, and give the `ArithmeticReturn` no `ucs`. The keyword `ucs_flag` raises a `MethodError`, and so does a second argument to `no_bounds_risk_measure` or `no_bounds_no_risk_expr_risk_measure`.

### [Renamed keywords and changed defaults](@id migration-0-34-renames)

- **`PanelFieldTarget` takes `fields`, where v0.33 took `name`.** The field takes one field of the asset panel, a sum or a product of fields, or a descriptor estimator such as `EWVolumeRatio()`, as the `fields` of `PanelFieldScorer` does, and a sum, a product or an estimator is not a name. Write `PanelFieldTarget(; fields = "market_cap")` where v0.33 wrote `PanelFieldTarget(; name = "market_cap")`, and read `target.fields` where v0.33 read `target.name`. The v0.33 keyword raises an `UndefKeywordError`. The positional form `PanelFieldTarget("market_cap")` does not change.
- **`ProcessedJuMPOptimiserAttributes` is `ResolvedJuMPOptimiser`, and it holds its constraints in a `ResolvedConstraintBlock`.** The bundle holds `pr`, `fees`, `ret`, `sca`, `imsk` and `cbr`, the resolved constraint block of the fit. v0.33 held one field for each resolved constraint. The reads `jor.wb`, `jor.lt`, `jor.st`, `jor.lcsr`, `jor.ctr`, `jor.gcardr`, `jor.sgcardr`, `jor.smtx`, `jor.sgmtx`, `jor.slt`, `jor.sst`, `jor.sglt`, `jor.sgst`, `jor.tn` and `jor.plr` forward to the block, so `res.wb` of a JuMP Result does not change. The keyword constructor takes `pr`, `fees`, `ret`, `sca`, `imsk` and `cbr`. The verb `processed_jump_optimiser_attributes` is `resolve_jump_optimiser`, `ProcessedAttributes` is `AbstractResolved`, `ProcessedRiskBudgetingAttributes` is `AbstractResolvedRiskBudgeting`, `ProcessedAssetRiskBudgetingAttributes` is `ResolvedAssetRiskBudgeting`, and `ProcessedFactorRiskBudgetingAttributes` is `ResolvedFactorRiskBudgeting`. The v0.33 names raise an `UndefVarError`.
- **`fit_and_predict` over one split takes `train_idx`, `test_idx` and `cols` by position.** Write `fit_and_predict(opt, rd, train_idx, test_idx, cols)` where v0.33 wrote `fit_and_predict(opt, rd; train_idx, test_idx, cols)`. `cols` defaults to `:`. To read a stepped estimator or pipeline out, pass `nothing` as `train_idx`: `fit_and_predict(opt, rd, nothing, test_idx)`. The form on a result ignores `train_idx`. Each form passes its keywords to `predict`, so a keyword that `predict` does not take raises a `MethodError`. On a result, v0.33 dropped such a keyword with no error. The forms over a scheme, `fit_and_predict(opt, rd, cv; cols, ex, id)`, do not change.
- **Every scalariser field and keyword defaults to `nothing`, where v0.33 defaulted to `SumScalariser()`.** This holds for the `sca` of `JuMPOptimiser`, `HierarchicalRiskParity`, `RiskLoss`, `MeanReturnRiskRatio`, `ExpectedReturnRiskRatio` and the Results that copy it, for `scai` and `scao` of `HierarchicalEqualRiskContribution`, for `sca1` and `sca2` of `NonOptimisationRiskRatio`, and for the `sca` keyword of `expected_risk`, `expected_ratio`, `risk_contribution` and their siblings. With a vector of risk measures, `nothing` gives the sum, so no number moves. A read of the field, such as `opt.sca isa SumScalariser`, now reads `nothing`. Every slot still takes `SumScalariser()`.
- **The field `pa` of a JuMP Result is `jor`, and the field `prb` of a risk budgeting Result is `rbr`.** Read `res.jor.pr` where v0.33 read `res.pa.pr`, `res.jr.jor` where v0.33 read `res.jr.pa`, and `res.rbr.rr` where v0.33 read `res.prb.rr`. The keyword of `JuMPOptimisationResult` is `jor`, and the keyword of `RiskBudgetingResult` and `RelaxedRiskBudgetingResult` is `rbr`. A read through the forward rule, such as `res.pr`, `res.wb` or `res.rkb`, does not change. `res.pa` and `res.prb` raise a `FieldError`.
- **Three range keys of the JuMP model follow `<base>_range_risk_`.** Read `model[:drcvar_range_risk_1]` where v0.33 read `model[:drcvar_risk_range_1]`, `model[:evar_range_risk_1]` where v0.33 read `model[:evar_risk_range_1]`, and `model[:genvar_range_risk_1]` where v0.33 read `model[:genvar_range_1]`. The keys of the upper bound of each of the three ranges change with them. `range_risk_key(r)` gives the key of every range that decomposes. The numbers do not move.
- **The four fee rows of the model of a `DiscreteAllocation` are vector rows.** In `res.l_model` and `res.s_model`, `model[:cftn_ub]`, `model[:cftn_lb]`, `model[:cb_ub]` and `model[:cb_lb]` are each one `ConstraintRef` of a vector-valued row, where v0.33 held a vector with one row for each asset. `JuMP.value` and `JuMP.dual` of the entry give the vector of v0.33 values, and `model[:cftn_ub][i]` raises a `MethodError`. The shares do not move.
- **`supports_precomputed_returns` takes the kind of the measure last.** Write `supports_precomputed_returns(r, rk)` where v0.33 wrote `supports_precomputed_returns(rk, r)`, with `rk` a `NetReturnsInput`, a `WeightsInput` or a `WeightsReturnsFeesInput`. `rk` defaults to `risk_input_kind(r)`, so `supports_precomputed_returns(r)` does not change, and a measure of yours still declares its own one-argument method. The v0.33 order raises a `MethodError`. The answers do not change.
- **`cross_sectional_warmup` takes the name of each factor.** Write `cross_sectional_warmup(X, Ms, emsk, nf)` where v0.33 wrote `cross_sectional_warmup(X, Ms, emsk)`, with `nf` the name of each column of `Ms`, as `ax.nf` of `cross_sectional_factor_axis` gives it. The exposure history of the observed factors, `Zo`, is a fifth argument, and `nf` then names the columns of `Zo` after those of `Ms`. The v0.33 call raises a `MethodError`, and an `nf` of another length raises a `DimensionMismatch`. The count of cold observations does not change. When every observation is cold, the refusal names each factor whose exposure history holds no finite value, such as `"momentum"` or `"industry=Energy"`.
- **`EWBeta`, `EWMarketBeta`, `EWMacroSensitivity`, `EWDownsideBeta`, `EWResidualVolatility` and `EWResidualDownsideVolatility` take no `min_val`.** The floor that v0.33 added to each denominator is gone, because a beta is now `NaN` where its denominator does not vary, as [Results that move](@ref migration-0-34-numbers) states. Delete the keyword. `EWResidualVolatility` and `EWResidualDownsideVolatility` take `beta_min_obs = 10` in its place, the number of valid observations that the beta of an asset needs before a residual is formed from it. The positional constructor of `EWResidualVolatility` takes `beta_min_obs` after `beta_decay`.
- **`JamesStein` and `BayesStein` clamp their shrinkage intensity by the new field `intensity`, whose default `UnitIntervalIntensity()` clamps it into ``[0, 1]``.** v0.33 used the raw intensity of the formula. On a sample whose raw intensity is outside ``[0, 1]``, the default moves the expected returns. One strong common factor gave `JamesStein` a raw intensity of `-25865`, and means close to the grand mean gave `26.6`. The intensity of `BayesStein` is in ``(0, 1]`` under a positive semidefinite covariance, so its default moves only under an indefinite covariance. Write `intensity = nothing` to keep the raw intensity of v0.33. `NonNegativeIntensity()` clamps at zero alone. `SteinShrinkage` does not read the field.
- **The default `subset_size` of `SubsetResampling` is `PowerSubsetSize()`, which keeps `round(N^0.8)` assets in each subset.** v0.33 kept the fraction `0.8 N`. On 100 assets a subset now holds 40 assets, where v0.33 held 80, so the weights move. Write `subset_size = 0.8` to keep the subsets of v0.33. `PowerSubsetSize(; p)` takes another power in ``(0, 1]``.
- **`covariance_forecast_compare` and `log_wealth_regret` test the mean of the loss difference with the test estimator in the keyword `mt`, where v0.33 took `lags`.** v0.33 divided the mean difference by a Newey-West variance at a fixed number of lags, and read a normal law. That test rejects too often. On a null MA(1) difference with a break in the volatility, at a nominal 5 %, the test at one lag rejected 9.4 % of the draws at 60 steps and 9.6 % at 240 steps. The default `mt = WildBootstrapMeanTest()` rejects 5.2 % and 4.9 %, and `OverlapMeanTest()` is the rule of zero lags. `CovarianceForecastComparisonResult` and `LogWealthRegretResult` hold `t_stat`, `p_value`, `mean_ci_lower` and `mean_ci_upper`, where v0.33 held `variance`, `z`, `p` and `lags`. The keyword `lags` raises a `MethodError`, and `res.z` raises a `FieldError`. A step that one side did not score is missing at its position, and two evaluations that share no scored step raise an `IsEmptyError`.
- **`Skewness` and `Kurtosis` read their comoment matrix, and lose the fields that only their functors read.** `Skewness` keeps `settings` and `sk`, and loses `w`, `mu`, `ve` and `pe`. `Kurtosis` keeps `settings`, `kt`, `N`, `alg` and `mtx_sqrt`, and loses `w`, `mu`, `alg1` and `pe`. `alg2` of `Kurtosis` is `alg`, so write `Kurtosis(; alg = QuadRiskExpr())` where v0.33 wrote `Kurtosis(; alg2 = QuadRiskExpr())`. Give a prior estimator to the slot, as in `Kurtosis(; kt = HighOrderPriorEstimator())`, where v0.33 wrote `pe`. The centre, the observation weights and the semi moment belong to the estimator of the matrix, such as `Cokurtosis(; w, alg = SemiMoment())`. A removed keyword raises a `MethodError`. Each value that the v0.33 functor read from the returns has a measure of its own:
  - `Skewness(; w, mu)` is `ThirdCentralMoment(; w, mu, ve = SimpleVariance())`.
  - `Kurtosis(; w, mu, alg2 = QuadRiskExpr())` is `HighOrderMoment(; w, mu, alg = FourthMoment())`.
  - `Kurtosis(; w, mu)` is `LowOrderMoment(; w, mu, alg = EvenMoment())`.
  - `alg1 = SemiMoment()` is `FourthMoment(; alg = SemiMoment())` or `EvenMoment(; alg = SemiMoment())`.

  On 250 observations of 5 assets, eight of the nine forms give the v0.33 value bit for bit. `Kurtosis(; w)` against `LowOrderMoment(; w, alg = EvenMoment())` is `4.2e-16` apart. [Results that move](@ref migration-0-34-numbers) states the value of the two functors.
- **`EWMean`, `EWMomentum` and `ExpWeightedExpectedReturns` take `debias = ZeroStartDebias()`.** The default divides the state of the recursion, which starts at zero, by the sum of its weights `1 - decay^n`. `ExpWeightedExpectedReturns` divided in v0.33 too, so its default result does not move. `EWMean` and `EWMomentum` did not divide in v0.33, and [Results that move](@ref migration-0-34-numbers) states how their values move. `debias = nothing` keeps the undivided state, which shrinks the mean of an asset with a short history toward zero. The new field comes before `cache`, so a positional call of the inner constructor takes one more argument, and the type of each estimator takes one more parameter, before the type of `cache`. A positional call of v0.33 raises a `MethodError`.
- **`GradientMomentum`, `RootMeanSquareGradient` and `AdaptiveMomentGradient` take `debias = nothing`, and `EGE`, `EGR` and `EGA` take the keyword `debias`.** The default reads each moving average undivided, as v0.33 did, so no default result moves bit for bit. `debias = ZeroStartDebias()` divides each average by `1 - gamma^k`, where `k` counts the steps since the seed of the averages, as Adam does. `EGA(; gamma1 = 0.9, gamma2 = 0.999, debias = ZeroStartDebias())` is the exponentiated gradient under Adam. The new field comes last, so a positional call of the inner constructor takes one more argument, and the type of each transform takes one more parameter, last. A transform prints one more field.
- **`DriftedWeights` has no field `wd`.** Write `DriftedWeights()` where v0.33 wrote `DriftedWeights(; wd = SelfFinancingDrift())`, the default. `SelfFinancingDrift` was the one drift of the library, so `DriftedWeights()` gives every drift of v0.33. The keyword `wd` raises a `MethodError`. A previous-weights source of your own states its drift with a method `held_weights_drift(::Nothing, ::MySource)`, which is public.
- **`ImpliedVolatility` has no field `mp`.** A covariance estimator takes no matrix processing. Write `PortfolioOptimisersCovariance(; ce = ImpliedVolatility(; ppy, …), mp)` where v0.33 wrote `ImpliedVolatility(; ppy, …, mp)`. The keyword `mp` raises a `MethodError`. The default no longer repairs the matrix, as [Results that move](@ref migration-0-34-numbers) states.
- **`OrderedWeightsArrayRange` has no field `rev`, and it stores a given `w2` as given.** v0.33 reversed a given `w2` unless `rev = true`, and its default `w2` was `owa_tg`. Write `w2 = reverse(v)`, or `w2 = reverse ∘ f` for a builder, where v0.33 wrote `w2 = v` or `w2 = f`. Write `w2 = v` where v0.33 wrote `w2 = v, rev = true`. With no `w2`, v0.34 stores the reversal of `w1`, where v0.33 stored the reversal of `owa_tg`. Write `w2 = reverse ∘ owa_tg` to keep the v0.33 range of `OrderedWeightsArrayRange(; w1)`. A v0.33 call with a `w2` and no `rev`, or with a `w1` alone, builds and gives another result with no error. `OrderedWeightsArrayRange()` does not move, and the keyword `rev` raises a `MethodError`.
- **`OWAJuMP` defaults `sc` and `so` to `nothing`, where v0.33 defaulted each to `1.0`.** `nothing` resolves to the scale of the objective. `sc` resolves to `1.0`. `so` resolves to `1e-3` under `MaximumEntropy`, the default `alg`, and to `1.0` under the other objectives. A number that you state wins. So under another objective the resolved scales are those of v0.33. A read of the field, such as `ow.so == 1.0`, now reads `nothing`. Under `MaximumEntropy` the objective is new, as [Results that move](@ref migration-0-34-numbers) states. On 15 programmes, with `T` from 50 to 1000 and `k` from 4 to 6, the default `OWAJuMP(; slv)` falls back to `ncrra_weights` on none, and a stated `so = 1.0` falls back on 11.

### [Calls that now raise](@id migration-0-34-raises)

| Call | v0.33 | v0.34 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `Covariance`, `Coskewness`, `Cokurtosis`, `SecondMoment`, `EvenMoment`, `FourthMoment`, `EWVolatility` or `EWResidualVolatility` with an `alg` that is a Gerber, Smyth-Broby or Gerber IQ algorithm or a shrinkage target, such as `Covariance(; alg = IdentityTarget())` | Built the estimator. `cov` then overflowed the stack, and `coskewness` and `cokurtosis` raised a `MethodError`. | Raises a `TypeError` when you build the estimator. The `alg` of these types is `FullMoment()` or `SemiMoment()`. Give a Gerber algorithm to `GerberCovariance`, `SmythBrobyCovariance` or `GerberIQCovariance`, and a shrinkage target to `GeodesicShrinkageCovariance`. |
| `EWResidualVolatility` or `EWResidualDownsideVolatility` with a `ce` whose every window holds an observation before the beta of each asset exists, such as `ce = Covariance()`, `SimpleVariance()`, `PortfolioOptimisersCovariance()`, `WindowedVariance()`, which has no window, or `WindowedVariance(; window = collect(1:60))` | Built the estimator, and `descriptor` answered. v0.33 formed a residual from the first observation, so `Covariance()` and `SimpleVariance()` gave a finite value at 117 of the 120 cells of a panel of 40 observations of 3 assets with no gap. `PortfolioOptimisersCovariance()` raised `ArgumentError: matrix contains Infs or NaNs`. | Raises an `ArgumentError` when you build the estimator. The residual of an asset is `NaN` until its beta exists, see `beta_min_obs` under [Renamed keywords and changed defaults](@ref migration-0-34-renames). The series of a plain estimator keeps no asset with a `NaN` in its window, so such a `ce` would give `NaN` at every cell. Pass the default `ce`, an `ExpWeightedVariance`, an `ExpWeightedCovariance`, a `SimpleVariance` or a `Covariance` with `cvg = CoveragePolicy()`, a `PortfolioOptimisersCovariance` over one of them with no step in its `mp`, such as `mp = MatrixProcessing(; pdm = nothing)`, or a windowed estimator with an integer window, such as `WindowedVariance(; window = 60)`. A composite with the default `mp` passes the constructor, and `descriptor` then raises `ArgumentError: matrix contains Infs or NaNs` on the `NaN` row of the warm-up, as the docstring of `PortfolioOptimisersCovariance` states. An index window is admitted when its first index is at least `max(beta_min_obs, 2)`. |
| `JuMPOptimiser` or `ProgrammeAllocationSet` with an empty vector in `lcse`, `smtx`, `sgmtx` or `ple`, and `FactorRiskContribution` with an empty `frc_ple`, such as `lcse = LinearConstraintEstimator[]` | Built the estimator. `optimise` of a `MeanRisk` on such a `JuMPOptimiser` then raised a `MethodError`, or a `TypeError` for `ple`. | Raises an `IsEmptyError` that names the field when you build the estimator, as an empty `tn` or `cte` does. Pass `nothing` for no constraint. |
| A `NearestQuantilePrediction` called with a sign, `scorer(ppred, -1)`, and `quantile_by_measure(...; sign = -1)` | Multiplied the risk of each path by the sign. | Raises a `MethodError`. The orientation of each measure now comes from `bigger_is_better`, so remove the sign. A call with `sign = -1` for a measure of which a bigger value is better selects the same path without it. |
| `NearestQuantilePrediction` or `quantile_by_measure` with the reduction of a vector of measures in `r_kwargs`, such as `r_kwargs = (sca = MaxScalariser(),)`; `sort_by_measure(ppred, r)`, `sort_by_measure(ppred, r; sca = …)` and `quantile_by_measure(ppred, r, q; …)`; and the positional constructor `NearestQuantilePrediction(r, q, r_kwargs, q_kwargs)` | Passed `r_kwargs.sca` and the keyword `sca` to `expected_risk`, which reduced the vector with it. A call with no `sca` reduced it with `SumScalariser()`. | `r_kwargs.sca` raises a `ConflictingArgumentError`, and the other calls raise a `MethodError`. The reduction is the field `sca` of `NearestQuantilePrediction`, `NearestQuantilePrediction(; r, sca = MaxScalariser())`, and the first argument of the two verbs, `sort_by_measure(MaxScalariser(), ppred, r)` and `quantile_by_measure(MaxScalariser(), ppred, r, q)`. For one measure the reduction is inert, so pass `SumScalariser()` or `nothing`. |
| `GridSearchCrossValidation` or `RandomisedSearchCrossValidation` with a reduction in `kwargs`, such as `kwargs = (sca = MaxScalariser(),)`; and the positional constructors of the two searches | Passed `kwargs.sca` to `expected_risk`, where it was inert on the one measure that `r` took. The positional constructors took `r` and then `scorer`. | `kwargs.sca` raises a `ConflictingArgumentError` that names `r_sca`. The reduction of a vector of measures is the new field `r_sca`, `GridSearchCrossValidation(p; r = [ConditionalValueatRisk(), MeanReturn()], r_sca = MaxScalariser())`. The positional constructors take `r_sca` after `r`, so a positional call of v0.33 raises a `MethodError`. The keyword constructors keep their meaning. |
| `SearchCrossValidationResult(; opt, ranked, …)` with an `opt` that is not `ranked[1]` | Built the Result. | Raises a `DimensionMismatch`. `opt` is the winner, `ranked[1]`, as `idx` is `rank_idx[1]`. Pass `ranked` with `opt` first, or omit `ranked`, which defaults to `[opt]`. |
| `FiniteAllocationInput` with an infinite price, such as `prices = [10.0, Inf, 20.0]` | Built the input. `GreedyAllocation` then gave a `NaN` cash with an `OptimisationSuccess`, and `DiscreteAllocation` raised an `ErrorException` from JuMP. | Raises a `DomainError` when you build the input. Write an absent price as `NaN`. |
| `EntropyPoolingPrior` or `MeucciEntropyPoolingPrior`, and an `OpinionPoolingPrior` of them, with a `sets` whose asset list has more or fewer names than `X` has columns | Raised a `DimensionMismatch` from a matrix product, which named no field. | Raises a `DimensionMismatch` that names `pe.sets.dict[pe.sets.xkey]` and both lengths, as `BlackLittermanPrior` does. The names still map to the columns of `X` by their position in the list. |
| `EntropyPoolingPrior` or `MeucciEntropyPoolingPrior` with a view row that no reweighting of the scenarios meets, such as a mean target above the largest return of its asset, or a row that only a posterior with a zero on the support meets, such as a mean target equal to that largest return | Failed in the numerics with an error that named no view, and the type of the error changed with the size of the miss. The default `OptimEntropyPooling` raised an `ArgumentError`, such as `matrix contains Infs or NaNs` or `weights cannot contain Inf or NaN values`, or a `DomainError` from `sqrt`. `JuMPEntropyPooling` raised `Entropy pooling optimisation failed`. Two such views returned a posterior with no error: a mean target on the edge of its band put all the mass on one scenario, with `kld = 6.92`, and a correlation view outside its band under `H1_EntropyPooling` gave `kld = 6.22`. | Raises a `DomainError` that names the view and the values that a reweighting of the scenarios reaches, on both routes. A row that is constant on the support passes when its target is that constant, as in v0.33.
