Two rows that each pass alone and that no posterior meets together raise as in v0.33, unless you give solvers to the new field `diagnose`: the fit then raises a `DomainError` that names the view fields, see [If you extend the library](@ref migration-0-34-extending). |
| `prior` or `optimise` on a `ReturnsResult` with names, where a `UniverseSets` of the estimator lists the assets in another order than `rd.nx`, or lists a name that `rd.nx` does not have, such as `sets = UniverseSets(; dict = Dict("nx" => ["A", "B", "C"]))` with `rd.nx == ["A", "C", "B"]` | Fitted. A name resolved to its position in the list, so each view, bound and group bound the column at that position, which `rd` names differently. Only the `sets` of a `JuMPOptimiser` was checked. | Raises an `ArgumentError` that names the first position where the lists differ, or a `DimensionMismatch` when the lengths differ. The check reads every `UniverseSets` of the prior and of the optimiser, and the factor key against `rd.nf`. A universe in the factor prior of a `FactorPrior`, a `FactorBlackLittermanPrior` or an `AugmentedBlackLittermanPrior` names the factors of `rd`. Build the list from `rd.nx`, as in `Dict("nx" => rd.nx)`. |
| A moment, a regression, a Descriptor, a forecast, a distance or a diagnostic called with a bare integer matrix, such as `cov(ExpWeightedCovariance(), X)`, `mean(ExpWeightedExpectedReturns(), X)` or `var(RegimeAdjustedExpWeightedVariance(), X)` with `X::Matrix{Int}` | Converted the data to `Float64` and gave the fit of the float copy. | Raises, usually an `InexactError` or a `MethodError`. These kernels take the type of their data, and an integer holds no fraction and no `NaN`. Build a `ReturnsResult` or a `PricesResult`, which converts integer data once, or pass `float.(X)`. `Rational` data are unchanged, and an integer stays where it is the natural type: a share count, a lot size, a cardinality, a count, an index, a `Bool` mask, the codes of a `CategoricalPanelField`. |
| `clusterise` of a hierarchical or a k-means clustering, and a fit of `HierarchicalRiskParity`, `HierarchicalEqualRiskContribution`, `SchurComplementHierarchicalRiskParity` or `NestedClustered`, on a distance with a `NaN` between two assets, such as two assets that share too few observations under a reader with `CoveragePolicy(; peel = nothing)` | The hierarchical clustering raised `ArgumentError: Distance matrix should be symmetric.` from `Clustering.hclust`, and the k-means clustering raised `ArgumentError: weights cannot contain Inf or NaN values` from `Clustering.kmeans`. | Raises a `DomainError` that names the first `NaN` cell and its cause, two assets that share too few observations. |
| A weight finaliser, or `opt_weight_bounds`, on integer weights with an absent bound, such as `opt_weight_bounds(IterativeWeightFinaliser(), WeightBounds(; lb = nothing, ub = 1), [2, 1, 0])` | Read the absent bound as an infinity and gave the projection of the float copy. | Raises an `ArgumentError` that names the element type. An absent bound is an infinity of the element type of the weights, and an integer type has none. Pass float weights. |
| `FollowTheLeader` whose held estimator has a fallback of another kind, such as `FollowTheLeader(; opt = MeanRisk(; opt = JuMPOptimiser(; slv), fb = EqualWeighted()))`, or a precomputed result in its fallback chain | Built the rule. A fallback leader solved without the allocation set of the head, so a cap of 0.2 on the set gave a weight of 0.9999999937 on one asset. | Raises an `ArgumentError` that names the member. Each member of the chain must have the kind of the held estimator: a JuMP estimator under a JuMP estimator, and a `BestConstantRebalancedPortfolio` under a `BestConstantRebalancedPortfolio`. |
| `FactorRiskContribution` with a `SemiDefinitePhylogeny` or a `SemiDefinitePhylogenyEstimator` in the `ple` of its `JuMPOptimiser`, alone, in a vector or in a schedule, such as `FactorRiskContribution(; opt = JuMPOptimiser(; slv, ple = SemiDefinitePhylogeny(; A, p = 0.05)))` | Built the estimator, and solved with no row of the phylogeny and no message. The weights were those of `ple = nothing`, so the linked pairs kept their weight. | Raises an `ArgumentError` when you build the estimator. The head solves for the factor weights and writes no lift of the asset weights, so the phylogeny could add no row. Put a phylogeny of the factors in `frc_ple`. An `IntegerPhylogeny` in `opt.ple` still applies. |
| `OnlinePortfolioSelection` with a `FollowTheLeader` whose held estimator, or a member of its fallback chain, is a `FactorRiskContribution`, and a `ProgrammeAllocationSet` whose `ple` holds a `SemiDefinitePhylogeny` or a `SemiDefinitePhylogenyEstimator` | Built the head. The rows of the phylogeny sat on a lift of the asset weights that no term held down and that took no penalty, so they bound nothing. | Raises an `ArgumentError` when you build the head, as `FactorRiskContribution` refuses a semidefinite phylogeny in `opt.ple`. Put the phylogeny on a `MeanRisk` leader, or use an `IntegerPhylogeny` on the set. |
| `FollowTheLeader` whose held JuMP optimiser has a net budget that excludes one, such as `bgt = 0.5` or `bgt = BudgetRange(; lb = 1.1, ub = 1.2)`, or a `BudgetCosts` or a `BudgetMarketImpact` in `bgt`, also as an entry or the `default` of a `TimeDependent` schedule | Built the rule. With `bgt = 0.5` the leader solved at no step, and the head traded nothing, so on 40 rows of three assets it played its drifted start allocation, `[0.3919, 0.3328, 0.2754]`. With `bgt = BudgetCosts(; bgt = 1.0, w = fill(1 / 3, 3), vp = 0.01, vn = 0.01)` the leader stayed at the reference `[1/3, 1/3, 1/3]`. | Raises when you build the rule: a `DomainError` for a number or a range, and an `ArgumentError` for a cost estimator. The head owns the budget of one, so write `bgt = nothing` or `bgt = 1` on the held optimiser. A function schedule raises at the first fold whose value excludes one. |
| `JuMPOptimiser` whose gross budget `gbgt` has an upper end below the least magnitude of the net budget, such as `JuMPOptimiser(; slv, bgt = 1.0, gbgt = 0.5, wb = WeightBounds(; lb = -1, ub = 1))`, and a `ProgrammeAllocationSet` whose `gbgt` has an upper end below one | Built the optimiser, and its solve gave an `OptimisationFailure`, because the gross exposure is at least `abs(sum(w))`. | Raises an `ArgumentError` that names both budgets when you build it. |
| `OnlinePortfolioSelection` with a `ProgrammeAllocationSet` and a `FollowTheLeader` whose held estimator, or a member of its fallback chain, is a `NearOptimalCentering` under its default `alg = UnconstrainedNearOptimalCentering()` | Built the head. The centring model of the leader dropped rows of the set, and the rule plays the leader with no projection at `gamma = 0`, so the head played an allocation outside its set. On 80 rows of eight assets and a set with the row `a + b <= 0.05`, the head played `a + b = 0.2054`. | Raises an `ArgumentError` when you build the head, also for an expert of an `ExpertMixture` and for an entry of a schedule on `set`. Write `alg = ConstrainedNearOptimalCentering()`, which keeps every row of the set, or use a `BoundedAllocationSet` for bounds alone. |
| `ConstantRebalancedPortfolio` with a target `w` whose element type is not a number, such as `w = Any[0.5, 0.5]` | Built the rule when the elements were numbers. A vector of another element type raised an error from `sum`. | Raises a `TypeError` when you build the rule. The bound of `w` is a vector of numbers. Pass `[0.5, 0.5]`. |
| `covariance_forecast_summary` or the keyword constructor of `CovarianceForecastSummaryResult` with a level that is not a real number, such as `levels = ("a",)` | `covariance_forecast_summary` raised a `MethodError` from `zero`. The constructor built the Result. | Raises a `TypeError` at the keyword `levels`. Pass a tuple or a vector of real numbers, such as `(0.95, 0.99)`. |
| A `PerformanceSummaryResult` built by hand with an `alpha` outside ``(0, 1)``, a `ppy` that is not positive, an `rf` that is not finite, a negative count, deviation, turnover or exposure, a `risk` that does not have the names of `measures`, or a field of an entry that does not have the shape of `n_periods` | Built the Result. The constructor checked no value. | Raises a `DomainError` for a value outside its domain, an `ArgumentError` for the names of `risk`, and a `DimensionMismatch` for a shape. The shape of a field is one value for one entry, or one vector entry for each series. A summary that `performance_summary` builds does not change. |
| A linear constraint row whose coefficients are all zero under `strict = true`, such as `linear_constraints(LinearConstraintEstimator(; val = "A - A <= 0.1"), sets; strict = true)`, also in a Black-Litterman view, a centrality constraint and a precomputed `LinearConstraint` in an `ExposureConstraintEstimator` | Raised an `ArgumentError`. A precomputed `LinearConstraint` in an `ExposureConstraintEstimator` kept a row that the loadings made all zero, and `strict = true` did not raise. | Raises an `IsZeroError`, a `PortfolioOptimisersError`. Catch `IsZeroError` where you caught `ArgumentError`. `strict = false` warns and drops the row, as in v0.33, and the precomputed route drops it too. An unknown name still raises an `ArgumentError`. |
| `owa_tg`, `owa_tgrg`, `OrderedWeightsArrayTailGini`, `OWA_TG` or `OWA_TG_RG` with `a_sim < 2` or `b_sim < 2`, such as `owa_tg(10; a_sim = 1)` | `owa_tg` raised an `ArgumentError` from `range`. `OrderedWeightsArrayTailGini(; a_sim = 1)` built the measure, and a call of it failed. | Raises a `DomainError`. The grid of a tail Gini needs two points, so `a_sim = 2` and `b_sim = 2` are the smallest values. |
| `cov` or `cor` of `ImpliedVolatility` with a negative entry or a gap in `iv` | A gap raised an `IsNonFiniteError`. A negative entry passed, and on the premium path a negative last entry gave a negative volatility, whose sign the conversion to a covariance hid. | Raises a `DomainError` for each, under both algorithms. A zero entry passes. |
| `ImpliedVolatilityRegression` on data that give three or four windows, with a zero `iv` on the last row of a window, or with a window of constant returns | Three windows gave a fit with no unique coefficients, and four windows a fit with no residual. A zero `iv` on the last row of an earlier window, and a window of constant returns, raised `ArgumentError: matrix contains Infs or NaNs` from the solve. A zero `iv` on the last row of the sample predicted a volatility of zero. | Raises a `DomainError`. The fit needs more than four windows, and it takes the logarithm of each implied and realised volatility that it reads, so each must be positive. The message names the count of windows, or the value, the asset and the window. A zero `iv` on a row that ends no window is not read. |
| `RegimeAdjustedExpWeightedVariance` or `RegimeAdjustedExpWeightedCovariance` with a `regime_decay` outside ``(0, 1)``, `LogRegimeAdjusted` with an `x` or a `y` that is not positive, `FirstMomentRegimeAdjusted` with an `x` that is not positive, and a `regime_method` of your own under `debias = ExactDebias()` or `debias = LawDebias()` | Built the estimator. At `regime_decay = 1` the regime state froze, a zero parameter gave a `NaN` or an infinite multiplier, and a method of your own failed in the fit. | Raises when you build the estimator: a `DomainError` for a value, and an `ArgumentError` for a method of your own. The two bias corrections read the moments of the methods of the library alone, so a method of your own takes `debias = nothing`, the raw statistic. |
| A matrix in `TurnoverEstimator.val`, `RiskBudgetEstimator.val`, `ThresholdEstimator.val`, `WeightBoundsEstimator.lb` or `ub`, or `FeesEstimator.l`, `s`, `fl` or `fs`, such as `WeightBoundsEstimator(; lb = [0.1 0.2; 0.3 0.4])` | Built the estimator. Its resolution then raised a `TypeError`, because the result holds a number or a vector. | Raises a `TypeError` at the keyword. Each slot takes a number, a vector of numbers, a pair, a value of many estimators, or a `VectorAbstractEstimatorValueAlgorithm`. |
| `PowerNormValueatRisk`, `PowerNormDrawdownatRisk` or `RelativePowerNormDrawdownatRisk` with `p = 1` | Built the measure. The power cone of order one is degenerate, so `PRM` returned `NaN`. | Raises a `DomainError`. At `p = 1` the measure equals the conditional measure of the same series, such as `ConditionalValueatRisk`, so use that measure. |
| The functor of `GenericValueatRiskRange` on a vector of returns, `r(X * w)`, with a `ValueatRisk(; alg = DistributionValueatRisk())` tail | Raised a `DimensionMismatch`, or returned a wrong value when `X` had as many observations as assets. | Raises an `ArgumentError`, because such a tail reads the weights. Call `r(w, X, fees)` or `expected_risk(r, w, X)`, as [Calls that now answer](@ref migration-0-34-answers) states. |
| `PricesResult` with a repeated timestamp | Built the result. `port_opt_view` then found each row by its timestamp, so a view returned the wrong rows. | Raises an `ArgumentError` that names the count and the first repeat, as `ReturnsResult` does. |
| `L1UncertaintySet` or `SignedL1UncertaintySet` with an infinite entry in `sd` | Built the set. A `NaN` entry raised a `DomainError`. | Raises an `IsNonFiniteError` for a `NaN` or an infinite entry. A zero entry builds, as [Calls that now answer](@ref migration-0-34-answers) states. |
| A `FeatureDistance` under `StackObservations` with a `WeightedHamming`, `PeriodicEuclidean`, `Mahalanobis` or `SqMahalanobis` metric, on a pair of assets that shares fewer rows than the window | Raised a `DimensionMismatch` from Distances.jl. | Raises an `ArgumentError` that names the method of `stack_rescale` to add for the metric, or tells you to use `AggregateDistances`. |
| `BlackLittermanViews` with a repeated entry in `excl`, an entry outside `1:(length(Q) + length(excl))`, or a vector `views_conf` of the length of the kept views under an `excl` | Built the views. A `views_conf` of the kept length passed, and its first entry applied to every kept view, so the other confidences were lost with no error. | Raises an `ArgumentError` for a repeat, a `DomainError` for an entry out of range, and a `DimensionMismatch` for `views_conf`. `views_conf` has one entry for each view that you wrote, `length(Q) + length(excl)`. |
| `Kurtosis(; alg1 = SemiMoment())`, and every call that passes `alg1` to `Kurtosis` | Built the measure. `alg1` set the moment of the functor alone. The JuMP model read the cokurtosis of `kt` or of the prior, so under `alg1 = SemiMoment()` the model and the functor disagreed. | Raises a `MethodError`. The functor and the model both read `kt`. Write `Kurtosis(; kt = Cokurtosis(; alg = SemiMoment()))` for the semi fourth moment. |
| `owa_l_moment(T, k)` with `k > T`, such as `owa_l_moment(5, 6)` | Returned weights of `NaN`, because no subset of `k` observations exists. | Raises a `DomainError`. The L-moment of order `k` weighs `k` order statistics at a time, so `1 <= k <= T` must hold. |
| `owa_gmd(T)` with `T < 2`, and `OrderedWeightsArray()` on one observation | `owa_gmd(1)` returned the range `NaN:Inf:NaN`. `OrderedWeightsArray()([0.01])` returned `NaN`, and a JuMP model on one observation stopped at a `NaN` coefficient. | Raises a `DomainError`. The Gini mean difference averages over the pairs of distinct observations, and one observation has no pair. |
| `OrderedWeightsArray` or `OrderedWeightsArrayRange` with a weight vector whose length is not the count of observations, such as `OrderedWeightsArray(; w = owa_cvar(100))` on 39 observations, in a JuMP model or in a call of the measure | The default `ApproxOrderedWeightsArray()` read only the sum, the extremes and the norms of the vector, so it solved another problem with no error. `ExactOrderedWeightsArray()` and `SortingNetworkOrderedWeightsArray()` raised a `DimensionMismatch` that named no field, and a call of the measure raised one from `dot`. | Raises a `DimensionMismatch` that names the field, `w`, `w1` or `w2`, and the count of observations, under each formulation and in a call of the measure. A function in the field, such as the default, takes the count of observations, as in v0.33. |
| `TurnoverRiskMeasure` with a `NaN` or an infinite entry in `w` | Built the measure. | Raises a `DomainError` when you build the measure. |
| `ThirdCentralMoment` with a `NaN` or an infinite `mu`, a number or an entry of a vector | Built the measure. | Raises an `IsNonFiniteError` when you build the measure, as `MedianAbsoluteDeviation` and `Kurtosis` do. |
| `bounds_risk_measure(rs, ubs)` with a vector of measures and a vector of bounds of another length, such as `bounds_risk_measure([ConditionalValueatRisk()], [0.1, 0.2, 0.3])` | Broadcast the measures over the bounds. One measure and three bounds gave three copies of the measure, and other lengths raised the `DimensionMismatch` of the broadcast. | Raises a `DimensionMismatch` that names both lengths. Each measure takes one bound. |
| `FactorRiskContribution` with a `Frontier` or a vector in `settings.ub` of a risk measure | Logged a warning that the bound "would be silently ignored", and solved one portfolio with no bound. | Raises an `ArgumentError`, because `FactorRiskContribution` solves its model once and sweeps no frontier. A number in `ub` still warns. Use `MeanRisk` or `NearOptimalCentering` for a sweep. |
| `FactorRiskContribution`, `RiskBudgeting` or `RelaxedRiskBudgeting` with a `Frontier` or a vector return floor, such as `ret = ArithmeticReturn(; settings = JuMPReturnsSettings(; lb = Frontier(; N = 3)))`, and `RiskBudgeting` with a `Frontier` or a vector in `settings.ub` of a risk measure | Solved one portfolio with no message. No solve read the bound, so the portfolio could fall below the floor. | Raises an `ArgumentError` that names the bounds when the model is built. Give each bound one number, or use `MeanRisk` or `NearOptimalCentering`, which sweep a frontier. |
| `Frontier(; N = 1)` | Built the frontier. A sweep then raised an `ArgumentError` from `range`, because a range of one point cannot hold two different end points. | Raises a `DomainError` when you build the frontier. A sweep runs from the minimum-risk end to the maximum-return end, so it needs two points. To solve at one bound, give the bound as a number. |
| `Skewness` or `Kurtosis` on a bare returns matrix, such as `expected_risk(Kurtosis(), w, X)`, or over a `LowOrderPrior`, such as `prior(EmpiricalPrior(), X)` or the prior that a hierarchical optimiser fits with its default `EmpiricalPrior` | Read the returns, and gave a value. | Raises an `IsNothingError`, because each measure reads its comoment matrix, `sk` or `kt`. The message names `prior(HighOrderPriorEstimator(), X)`, which fits both matrices. |
| `ScoreSelector(; score = Skewness(), rule)` or `ScoreSelector(; score = Kurtosis(), rule)` | Built the selector, and scored each asset on its returns. | Raises an `ArgumentError` that names a measure of a return series: `ThirdCentralMoment()`, `ThirdCentralMoment(; ve = SimpleVariance())`, `HighOrderMoment(; alg = FourthMoment())` or `LowOrderMoment(; alg = EvenMoment())`. |
| `asset_scores(r, X)` or `asset_scores(r, rd)` with a risk measure that cannot score the return series of one asset, such as `EqualRisk()`, `Variance()`, `StandardDeviation()`, `Skewness()` or `Kurtosis()` | `EqualRisk()` gave `1 / T` to each asset, because its functor read the column as portfolio weights. The other measures failed in their functor, for example with a `MethodError`. | Raises the `ArgumentError` that `ScoreSelector` raises for the same measure, which names a measure of a return series. |
| `AssetPanel` with an empty vector in `pf` | Built a panel that held its masks alone. | Raises an `IsEmptyError`. Write `pf = nothing` for a panel that holds its masks alone. |
| `NestedClustered` or `Stacking` with a split result in `cv`, such as a `KFoldResult`, and with a `scorer` beside a `cv` that is not a `CombinatorialCrossValidation` | `OptimisationCrossValidation(; cv, scorer)` took both. | Raises a `TypeError` at the keyword `cv` for a split result, and an `ArgumentError` for the `scorer`, which selects one path of a combinatorial scheme. Pass the scheme in `cv`, and `scorer = nothing` beside a scheme that is not combinatorial. |
| `RadialTailDecay` on a prior whose `chol` is a singular square factor with no zero on its diagonal, such as `[1 1 0; 1 1 0; 0 0 1]` | Passed the check of the diagonal, and whitened the sample with a singular factor. | Raises a `DomainError`. The rule reads the diagonal of a triangular factor: `chol` when it is an `UpperTriangular`, and the R factor of its QR factorisation otherwise. |
| `NumericPanelInput` or `TensorPanelInput` with a fill policy whose `val` is a string, such as `alg = ConstantPanelFill(; val = "a")` | Built the input. `asset_panel` then failed when the fill wrote the string, with a conversion error that named no field. | Raises an `ArgumentError` that names the field when you build the input. A `CategoricalPanelInput` still takes a string `val`, the level to fill with. |
| A variance estimator, such as `SimpleVariance()`, `ExpWeightedVariance()`, `RegimeAdjustedExpWeightedVariance()` or `WindowedVariance()`, in a slot that calls `cov` or `cor`, such as `EmpiricalPrior(; ce = SimpleVariance())`, `PortfolioOptimisersCovariance(; ce = SimpleVariance())`, the `ce` of `ShrunkExpectedReturns`, `ClustersEstimator` or `NetworkEstimator`, or the `sigma` of `Variance` | Built the estimator. The first fit raised a `MethodError`, because a variance estimator has no `cov`. `NetworkEstimator(; ce = SimpleVariance(), de = Distance(; alg = VariationInfoDistance()))` with a tree type built and fitted, because that distance did not call `cor`. | Raises an `ArgumentError` in the constructor that names the slot and asks for a covariance estimator, such as `Covariance()`. A slot that calls only `var` or `std` keeps a variance estimator: the `ce` of `StandardDeviationExpectedReturns` and `VarianceExpectedReturns`, the `ve` of `GerberCovariance` and of `CrossSectionalFactorPrior`, and the `ve` of `WindowedVariance`. |
| `brinson_attribution(X, w, wb, asset_classes, col, date0, date1)` with a bound whose type does not compare with the timestamps of `X`, such as a `DateTime` bound of zoned prices, or with a bound that is not a timestamp, such as the string `"2020-01-02"` | Converted each bound with `DateTime`. A zoned `X` then raised `no promotion exists for DateTime and ZonedDateTime` for every bound, zoned ones too, and a string bound was parsed. | Compares each bound in the type of the timestamps. A bound of another type raises an `ArgumentError` that names the bound and both types, and a string raises a `MethodError`. Give a zoned bound for zoned prices, and write `Date("2020-01-02")` for a string. A `Date` bound of `DateTime` prices still works. |
| `brinson_attribution` with a window that holds no observation of `X` | Raised a `MethodError` from `X[nothing]`. | Raises an `ArgumentError` that names both bounds and the first and last timestamps of `X`. |
| `port_opt_view(rd, i, j)` of a `ReturnsResult`, and `port_opt_view(pr, i, j)` of a `PricesResult`, with timestamps `i` whose type does not compare with the timestamps of the data, such as `DateTime` timestamps of zoned data, also when `i` is a vector of an abstract element type, such as `Dates.AbstractTime[zoned_t, DateTime(2020, 3, 5)]` | Raised `no promotion exists for DateTime and ZonedDateTime`, or a `MethodError` of `getindex` on a `TimeArray`. | Raises an `ArgumentError` that names `i` and both types. A vector of an abstract element type is checked entry by entry, so a vector whose entries are all zoned still selects. |
| `ExponentialDecay(; rate = Inf)`, also as the `decay` of a `Proximity` | Built the decay. `separation_decay` gave `NaN` at the separation `0`, because `Inf * 0` is `NaN`, so `phylogeny_features` put a `NaN` on the entry of each asset itself. | Raises a `DomainError` when you build the decay. Give a large finite rate, which scores each neighbour near `0`. |
| `ScoreSelector` or `RedundancySelector` with a measure of the entropic, relativistic or power-norm family that has no solver, such as `score = EntropicValueatRisk()`, or with a `GenericValueatRiskRange` or a `RiskRatio` that holds such a measure, and `expected_risk(r, w, X)`, `asset_scores(r, X)` or the functor `r(x)` of such a measure | The constructor built the selector, and the fit raised a `MethodError` of `ERM`, `RRM` or `PRM`, several frames down. The evaluations raised the same `MethodError`. | The constructor raises an `IsNothingError` that names `slv` and shows a `Solver`, also when a child of the measure has no solver. The new `FoldRiskScorer` and `ScoreWeightBounds` refuse it in their constructors too. The evaluations raise an `IsNothingError` that names `slv`, also when `PRM` needs no model. State the solver on the measure, such as `EntropicValueatRisk(; slv = Solver(; solver = Clarabel.Optimizer))`. An optimiser still gives the measure its own solver. |
| A lens write, such as `Accessors.@set pipe.steps = (InverseVolatility(), EmpiricalPrior())`, a positional call, or a candidate of a search, that gives a `Pipeline` steps in an order that its keyword constructor refuses. The same for a value that the keyword constructor refuses in a `SampleBufferState`, a `ReturnsBufferState`, a `PipelineBufferState`, a `PricesToReturnsState`, a `PriceGapFillState`, a `PriceGapFillResult`, a `MissingDataFilterState`, an `OnlinePortfolioSelectionState`, a `StepwiseRegression` or a `DimensionReductionRegression`, in `EntropyBudget(nothing)`, and in the count, the mean or the shape of a `CovarianceState`, a `SimpleVarianceState` or a `SimpleExpectedReturnsState` | Built the value. Only the keyword constructor checked it. A search over a step of a pipeline fitted a candidate that put the optimiser before the prior. | Raises the error of the keyword constructor, because the inner constructor now holds each check. The keyword constructor of the three moment states alone checks that the values are finite, because that scan costs as much as the fold of one observation. A lens write of a symbol `crit` on a `StepwiseRegression` converts it to a `Val`, as the keyword constructor does. |

### [Calls that now answer](@id migration-0-34-answers)

| Call | v0.33 | v0.34 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A plot function other than `plot_network`, such as `plot_composition`, after `using PortfolioOptimisers, StatsPlots` with no `using GraphRecipes` | Raised a `MethodError`, because the plot extension loaded only with both packages. | Draws. The plot extension loads with `StatsPlots` alone. `plot_network` is in a second extension, `PortfolioOptimisersGraphRecipesExt`, which loads after `using GraphRecipes, StatsPlots`. Code that loads both packages does not change. |
| `CrossSectionalFactorPrior` with `ve = SimpleVariance()`, `ve = Covariance()` or another estimator with no variance series of its own, with `ve = ExpWeightedCovariance()` or `ve = WindowedCovariance(; ce = Covariance())`, and the same prior inside an entropy pooling prior with `w` | Raised a `DimensionMismatch`, because the variance series did not cut the masks and the weights of the fit to the rows of each refit. `ExpWeightedCovariance()` and `WindowedCovariance(; ce = Covariance())` raised a `MethodError` of the count of the variance, which they did not state. | Fits. Row `t` of the series reads the masks, the implied volatility and the observation weights of the rows `1` to `t`. A covariance estimator whose diagonal weights the squares as a variance estimator with a count does, such as `Covariance()`, `ExpWeightedCovariance()` or `WindowedCovariance(; ce = Covariance())`, states the count of that variance estimator, so `ResidualInflation` on that prior gives the radius of `ve = SimpleVariance()` for `ve = Covariance()`. A shrinkage estimator, a `SemiMoment` covariance and an `alg` step of your own in a `MatrixProcessing` state no count, and `ResidualInflation` then raises an `IsNothingError` that names the fix. A `PortfolioOptimisersCovariance` with a step in its `mp`, as `PortfolioOptimisersCovariance()` is, raises `ArgumentError: matrix contains Infs or NaNs` in place of the `DimensionMismatch`, as its docstring states. `WindowedCovariance()` wraps such a composite, and it raised that `ArgumentError` in v0.33 too. |
| `WindowedVariance` or `WindowedCovariance` with no weights of its own over an inner estimator with observation weights, such as `WindowedVariance(; ve = SimpleVariance(; w), window = 20)` | Raised a `DimensionMismatch`. | Cuts the weights of the inner estimator to the window. |
| `variance_series` over a panel of a `WindowedVariance` or a `WindowedCovariance` over a plain estimator, such as `WindowedVariance(; window = 4)`, when a window covers no asset | Raised an `IsEmptyError` at the first such window, so the whole series raised. | Gives `NaN` in the row of such a window, as in the row of a window that keeps no observation, and fits each other row. A single call of `var` over a window that covers no asset still raises the `IsEmptyError`. So `EWResidualVolatility(; ce = WindowedVariance(; window = 60))`, whose first windows hold the warm-up of the residual, does not raise, and it is `NaN` in each row whose window holds the warm-up. It answered in v0.33 too, at 117 of the 120 cells of a panel of 40 observations of 3 assets with no gap, because v0.33 formed a residual from the first observation. |
| `var`, `std` and `variance_series` over a panel, or with `pnl = nothing`, of a `PortfolioOptimisersCovariance` over a mask-aware estimator, such as `PortfolioOptimisersCovariance(; ce = ExpWeightedCovariance(), mp = MatrixProcessing(; pdm = nothing))` | Reduced each window to the assets that are finite and active at each of its rows, whatever the inner estimator. So an asset with one `NaN` or one inactive row in the window was `NaN`, and `var` raised an `IsEmptyError` when no asset was covered. On a panel of 30 assets and 300 observations whose first 9 rows are `NaN`, the series of the composite had 0 finite cells. | The inner estimator reads the window, as the panel method of `cov` of the composite already let it do. `var` is the diagonal of the processed covariance over the panel, so `mp` runs on each fit. On that panel the series has the 8 700 finite cells of `Covariance(; cvg = CoveragePolicy())`, and the 7 560 of `ExpWeightedCovariance()`. Over a plain estimator the composite gives the v0.33 values. Over `Covariance(; cvg = CoveragePolicy())` with no `w`, `ExpWeightedCovariance()` or `RegimeAdjustedExpWeightedCovariance()`, with no `alg` step in `mp`, the series folds each observation one time, with the matrix call and with the panel, and gives the values of a refit at each row. Over a `Covariance` with `w`, the series refits each row. |
| `expected_return(ArithmeticReturn(; mu = 0.01), w, pr)`, a number in `mu`, and each ratio and measure that reads the term, such as `ExpectedReturn` | Raised a `DimensionMismatch`. | Gives `0.01 * sum(w)`, the value of the model expression of the term. |
| `JuMPOptimiser`, `ProgrammeAllocationSet` or `FactorRiskContribution` with a vector field that mixes the members of its element type, such as `tn = [Turnover(…), TurnoverEstimator(…)]`; and `GridSearchCrossValidation` or `RandomisedSearchCrossValidation` with a vector of grids whose types differ | `TypeError` or `MethodError`, unless the caller narrowed the literal with `concrete_typed_array` | The keyword constructor narrows the literal. A wrong element raises an `ArgumentError` that names the field. |
| A JuMP optimiser with `sca = LogSumExpScalariser()` and a risk measure whose entry of the risk vector is quadratic, such as `MeanRisk(; r = [Variance(), ConditionalValueatRisk()], opt = JuMPOptimiser(; sca = LogSumExpScalariser(), …))` | The solve failed, and the Result held an `OptimisationFailure`. | Solves. An epigraph variable takes the place of each quadratic entry in the exponential cone, so the scalarised risk does not change. |
| A constraint estimator whose `val` maps names to fractions and whose `dval` is an integer, such as `ThresholdEstimator(; val = "A" => 0.05, dval = 0)` | Raised an `InexactError` when it resolved against a `UniverseSets`. | Resolves. An integer `dval` takes the type that the values of the map promote it to. Any other `dval` keeps its type, as in v0.33. |
| `sort_by_measure` with a vector of risk measures that disagree on orientation, such as `[MeanReturn(), MaximumDrawdown()]` | Raised an `ArgumentError` from `bigger_is_better`. | Sorts. Each measure is oriented so that a higher score is better before the vector is reduced. |
| `NearestQuantilePrediction`, `sort_by_measure` or `quantile_by_measure` with a `VectorToScalarMeasure` or a function as the reduction of a vector, such as `sca = MedianValue()` | Raised a `TypeError`, because `expected_risk` takes a `Scalariser` only. | Reduces the oriented scores of the measures with it. |
| `factor_attribution(w, pr, ret)` with constant weights `w` and a net return series `ret`, and its rolling twin `factor_attribution(w, pr, ret, window)` | Raised a `MethodError`, because the method on a return series took a weight history alone. | Decomposes the series. The methods on asset returns form their net series and call this method, so `factor_attribution(w, pr, X)` gives what `factor_attribution(w, pr, ret)` gives on the series that `X` forms. |
| `GridSearchCrossValidation` or `RandomisedSearchCrossValidation` with a vector of risk measures in `r`, such as `r = [ConditionalValueatRisk(), MeanReturn()]` | Raised a `TypeError`, because `r` took one measure. | Scores the vector. Each test score and each train score orients each measure so that a higher score is better, and the field `r_sca` reduces the vector in each fold or path. Its default `nothing` gives the sum, as `SumScalariser()` does. A vector whose measures disagree on orientation is admitted. A search of one measure does not change. |
| `expected_risk` and `expected_risk_from_returns` with the scalariser as the first argument, such as `expected_risk(MaxScalariser(), [ConditionalValueatRisk(), MaximumDrawdown()], w, X)` | Raised a `MethodError`, because the scalariser was the keyword `sca` alone. | Gives what the keyword form `expected_risk(r, w, X; sca = MaxScalariser())` gives, on weights, a weight path, a prior, a result and a prediction. The keyword form does not change. A single measure ignores the scalariser in both forms. |
| `GreedyAllocation` or `DiscreteAllocation` on prices with a `NaN`, such as `prices = [10.0, NaN, 20.0]` for an asset with no bar on the allocation date | `GreedyAllocation` gave `NaN` shares of that asset and a `NaN` cash, with an `OptimisationSuccess`. `DiscreteAllocation` raised an `ErrorException` from JuMP. | Allocates. No allocator can buy an asset with no price, so it holds zero shares, and the new `upw` field of `FiniteAllocationInput` states what its target weight becomes. The default `upw = nothing` keeps the money of that weight in `cash`. `ProportionalRedistribution()`, `EvenRedistribution()`, `AssetRedistribution(; idx)` and `SideRedistribution(; l, s)` move it to priced assets of the same side. |
| `FiniteAllocationInput(; ca = f)` with a plain function `f` | Raised a `TypeError`, because `ca` took an `AbstractCollateralAlgorithm` only. | Takes a function with the three methods of `AbstractCollateralAlgorithm`. |
| `SimpleExpectedReturns`, `SimpleVariance`, `Covariance`, `Coskewness` or `Cokurtosis` with observation weights `w` and a `CoveragePolicy`, such as `var(SimpleVariance(; w, cvg = CoveragePolicy()), X)` | Raised an `ArgumentError` that told you to run the batch verb, which you ran. | Fits. Each asset, each pair and each cell of a co-moment reads its own valid observations with their weights, and equal weights give the fit with no weights. A pair whose shared observations have no positive weight is undetermined, so the peel removes an asset of it, or `strict = true` refuses it. |
| `CrossSectionalFactorPrior` with `CurrencyExposure(; base)` on a universe whose every asset is in the base currency, such as `"currency" => CurrencyExposure(; base = "USD")` on assets that are all in `"USD"` | With no `E`, raised an `IsNothingError` that named an empty list of series. With an `E` that holds no currency of the universe, raised `IsEmptyError: fx cannot be empty`. | Fits. The member gives no currency factor and reads no series, so `mu` and `sigma` equal the fit without the member. |
| A fit of the view of an `OpinionPoolingPrior` of entropy pooling opinions, such as `prior(port_opt_view(op, [1, 3]), X[:, [1, 3]])` | Raised a `DimensionMismatch` from a matrix product, because each opinion kept its `sets` on every asset. | Fits. The view cuts the `sets` of each opinion. |
| A fit of `HierarchicalRiskParity`, `HierarchicalEqualRiskContribution`, `SchurComplementHierarchicalRiskParity` or `NestedClustered`, or a `JuMPOptimiser` with `ple` or `cte`, whose distance reader has a `CoveragePolicy` that refuses an asset that the prior keeps, such as `ClustersEstimator(; ce = PortfolioOptimisersCovariance(; ce = Covariance(; cvg = CoveragePolicy(; min_coverage = 0.6))))` with an asset that holds half of the observations | The hierarchical heads raised `ArgumentError: Distance matrix should be symmetric.` from `Clustering.hclust`, and the phylogeny and centrality readers raised a `DomainError` with `NaN`. | Fits. The fit drops the asset with a warning and gives it a zero weight, and the other weights equal the fit on the other assets. `strict = true` raises an `IsNonFiniteError`. A direct call of `clusterise`, `phylogeny_matrix` or `centrality_vector` raises a `DistanceReaderRefusalError` that names the columns. |
| A fit of `HierarchicalRiskParity`, `HierarchicalEqualRiskContribution`, `SchurComplementHierarchicalRiskParity` or `NestedClustered`, or a `JuMPOptimiser` with `ple` or `cte`, on an asset with zero variance, such as cash at a constant return | Under the default reader, `HierarchicalRiskParity` and `HierarchicalEqualRiskContribution` raised `ArgumentError: matrix contains Infs or NaNs`. Under the reader `ClustersEstimator(; ce = Covariance())`, `HierarchicalRiskParity` raised `ArgumentError: Distance matrix should be symmetric.`, and a k-means clustering raised `ArgumentError: weights cannot contain Inf or NaN values`. | Fits. The fit drops the asset from the investable mask with a warning and gives it a zero weight, and `strict = true` refuses it with an `IsNonFiniteError`. The correlation of the asset is `NaN` at every entry, see the entry of `cor` in [Results that move](@ref migration-0-34-numbers). |
| An online fit whose `ProgrammeAllocationSet` holds a `ple` or `cte` reader that cannot estimate the correlation of an asset that the prior of the set keeps, such as a reader whose `CoveragePolicy` asks for more observations than the prior | The reader raised an error from its graph, and the fit stopped. | Fits. At each such step the asset takes a zero weight with a warning, and the `strict` flag of the head refuses it with an `IsNonFiniteError`. The re-solve of a `FollowTheLeader` rule does the same, because the set enters the entry of the leader as a block. |
| An online fit whose `ProgrammeAllocationSet` holds a `ple` or `cte` graph reader with a plain moment estimator, such as `SemiDefinitePhylogenyEstimator(; pl = NetworkEstimator())`, beside a prior of the set that fills the gap of a young asset, such as `EmpiricalPrior(; fill_limit = 1)` | Raised an `IsNonFiniteError` from the graph reader at the first step whose rows held the gap, and the fit stopped. | Fits. The projection resolves the set through the entry of a JuMP head, so the asset takes a zero weight at each such step with the warning of the graph reader, as in a batch fit. The `strict` flag of the head refuses it with an `IsNonFiniteError`. An asset that a `FeatureDistance` of a reader of the set cannot read drops in the same way. |
| A `FollowTheLeader` rule whose `ProgrammeAllocationSet` has a prior that prices fewer assets than the prior of the leader, such as a plain `pe` in the set beside a mask-aware prior in the leader, at a step with a young asset | Raised an `ArgumentError` that named the assets that the prior of the set could not price. | Solves. The entry of the leader intersects the investable masks of its own block and of the block of the set, so each such asset takes a zero weight with a warning that names it. The `strict` flag of the head refuses it with an `IsNonFiniteError`. |
| An online fit whose `ProgrammeAllocationSet` holds a `LinearConstraintEstimator` in `lcse` or `gcarde`, at a step at which the prior of the set leaves an asset out of its investable mask | The set resolved the constraint once for each fold over every asset, and the reduction of the step kept its width. The model then raised a `DimensionMismatch` from a matrix product. | Solves. The set resolves `lcse` and `gcarde` at each step, after the reduction, as a JuMP head does. A row that names the departed asset drops, and `strict` does not refuse the name. A precomputed `LinearConstraint` of the width of the universe raises a `DimensionMismatch` that names the slot, as it does in a JuMP head. |
| `cor(MutualInfoCovariance(), X)` with the default `bins = HacineGharbiRavier()` on a matrix with a constant column | Raised an `InexactError`. | Gives the constant column `NaN` at every entry. |
| `asset_scores(r, X)` of a risk measure on a matrix with a column that holds a gap, such as `asset_scores(SCM(), X)` with `NaN` in 20 rows of one column | Raised the `IsNonFiniteError` of the moment estimator of the measure, so one gap refused the scores of every asset. | Scores each column with no gap, and gives `NaN` at a column with a gap, which every selection rule drops. On returns data, a column that the data do not cover over the window scores `NaN` too. A column with no gap and a non-finite score still raises a `DomainError`. `ScoreSelector` dropped such a column in v0.33 too, so a selection does not change. |
| A `FollowTheLeader` rule whose leader and whose `ProgrammeAllocationSet` both state `l1`, `linf`, `l2c` or `linfc`, such as `JuMPOptimiser(; l1 = 0.01, …)` in the leader and `ProgrammeAllocationSet(; l1 = 0.01, …)` | Raised an `ErrorException` from JuMP when the leader's model took the set: `An object of name :t_l1 is already registered in this model.` | Solves. The set's terms enter the leader's model under the prefix of the set, and its penalties add to the objective penalty of the leader. A cardinality, a threshold or an integer phylogeny in both now reads one binary for each asset, where v0.33 declared a second vector of binaries for the set. |
| `plot_histogram` of a return series that holds a `NaN` or an infinite return, such as the series of a walk-forward path with a fold that failed to solve | Raised an `ArgumentError`: `start and stop must be finite, got NaN and NaN`. | Draws the finite returns. The bins, the moments, every vertical line and the fitted Normal law read them alone. A series with no finite return draws the axes alone, with no bin and no line. |
| `plot_weight_stability` and `plot_cv_dashboard` of a walk-forward path with a fold that failed to solve, and `plot_weight_stability` of a population with such a path | Raised an `ArgumentError`: `quantiles are undefined in presence of NaNs or missing values`. | Draws. A failed fold holds no weight, so it is absent from every box, and a path with no fold left draws the axes alone. |
| `HierarchicalRiskParity` and `SchurComplementHierarchicalRiskParity` with a `WeightBoundsEstimator` that resolves a side to a number or to `nothing`, such as `WeightBoundsEstimator(; lb = 0.0, ub = 0.6)` or `WeightBoundsEstimator(; ub = Dict("B" => 0.2))`, whose `lb` keeps its default | Raised a `MethodError`: `no method matching view(::Float64, ::Vector{Int64})`, or `view(::Nothing, …)` for `lb = nothing`. | Solves. Each side is expanded to one bound per asset, so the weights equal those of the same bounds stated as two vectors. |
| `OnlinePortfolioSelection` with a `FollowTheLeader` whose held estimator has a `RiskTrackingError` of a `Variance` under `DependentVariableTracking` in `tr`, and a `ProgrammeAllocationSet` whose `ple` holds a semidefinite phylogeny | Every re-solve failed, because the tracking variance took the second-order cone, which that tracking mode cannot solve. Each step warned that the programme of the leader did not solve, and traded nothing, so the rule held the drift of its start. | Solves. The block of the set marks the weights before the tracking errors of the head, so the tracking variance takes the semidefinite formulation, and the leader equals the leader with the same phylogeny in the `ple` of the held estimator. On 6 assets and 120 rows with two linked pairs and `p = 0.05`, the largest `w_i w_j` of a linked pair is `0.028` in v0.33 and `4.7e-7` in v0.34. |
| `Solver(; settings)` with an `MOI.AbstractOptimizerAttribute` key, such as `settings = MOI.Silent() => true`, `MOI.TimeLimitSec() => 7.5` or `MOI.RawOptimizerAttribute("max_iter") => 3` | Raised an `ArgumentError`, because a key was a string or an `MOI.AbstractModelAttribute`. | Sets the attribute on the model of the solve. A `Symbol` key, such as `:verbose => false`, still raises an `ArgumentError`. |
| `ImpliedVolatilityRegression(; ws = 2)` | Raised a `DomainError`, because `ws` had to be greater than `2`. | Builds. A window of two rows has a corrected standard deviation with one degree of freedom. `ws = 1` raises a `DomainError`, because a window of one row has no dispersion. |
| `expected_risk` and the call `r(w, X, fees)` of a `GenericValueatRiskRange` with a `ValueatRisk(; alg = DistributionValueatRisk())` tail | `expected_risk` raised a `DimensionMismatch`, or returned a wrong value when `X` had as many observations as assets. | Evaluates the loss tail on `w` and the gain tail on `-w`, and a tail of returns on the net returns. Under `MinimumRisk`, the objective of the JuMP model equals `expected_risk` of its weights. |
| `matrix_square_root(RidgeCholeskySquareRoot(), M)` on a `Rational` matrix | Raised a `MethodError`: `no method matching eps(::Type{Rational{Int64}})`. | Factorises. The ridge and its floor take the element type of the plain factor. |
| `AggregateDistances` or `StackObservations` of a `FeatureDistance` on an integer window | Raised an `InexactError`, because a mean or a rescale went into an `Int` matrix. | Gives a float matrix. A `Rational` window stays exact, and a `Float32` window with no weights stays `Float32`. |
| `L1UncertaintySet` or `SignedL1UncertaintySet` with a zero entry in `sd` or a `NaN` entry in `mu` | Raised a `DomainError` for the zero, and an `IsNonFiniteError` for the `NaN`. | Builds. A zero scale fixes the entry at its estimate, and the worst case reads only the other assets. An infinite entry of `mu` still raises. |
| `CharacteristicUncertaintySet` with an l1 algorithm on a prior fitted on a point-in-time asset panel, whose `mu` holds a `NaN` outside its investable mask | Raised `DomainError with 0.2` from the tie check of the radius, because the `NaN` entries of `pr.mu` reached the ladder. | Fits on the investable mask of the prior, and expands the set to the full universe, as the other uncertainty sets do. `sd` is zero outside the mask, and `mu` is `NaN` there. The view of the set at the mask is the fit on the mask. |
| `BlackLittermanViews` with an `excl` longer than `Q`, such as three views of which two resolve no name under `strict = false` | Raised `DimensionMismatch: length(excl) = 2 must be <= length(Q) = 1`. | Drops the two views with a warning for each, and keeps the third. |
| A `LoGo` matrix processing under `dims = 2` with a variation of information distance | Raised a `DimensionMismatch`, because the check read the observations as the assets. | Fits. The distance gives the same matrix for both orientations. |
| `phylogeny_matrix(::ClustersEstimator, X; dims = 2)`, and `asset_phylogeny` through it | Raised a `BoundsError`, because the membership matrix took the count of observations. | Gives the phylogeny matrix of the assets. |
| A network estimator on an integer distance matrix with a zero entry | Raised a `MethodError`: `no method matching nextfloat(::Int64)`. | Fits. The repaired copy of the matrix takes a float element type. |
| A covariance or a correlation view of entropy pooling whose pair cancels, such as `cov_views = LinearConstraintEstimator(; val = "(A, B) - (A, B) == 0.1")` in `EntropyPoolingPrior` or `MeucciEntropyPoolingPrior` | `EntropyPoolingPrior` raised `Entropy pooling optimisation failed`, also for `== 0.0` and under `rho_views`. | Drops the view with a warning, as a cancelled mean view does. `prior(pe, X; strict = true)` raises an `IsZeroError`. |
| A sequential CVaR view of `EntropyPoolingPrior`, `ConditionalValueatRiskView(; alg = SequentialTailView(), views)`, where v0.33 wrote `alg = SequentialConditionalValueatRiskView()`, on a target that a posterior meets, where the worst scenario holds at least `alpha` of the prior, such as 10 equal weights of `0.1` at `alpha = 0.05`, also where the losses of the assets on the left side sum to a constant on the support | Raised `Entropy pooling optimisation failed`. The start of the sequential solve read the row of the prior, which no posterior meets, so the first solve was infeasible. | Solves, and the posterior CVaR meets the target. On 10 rows of the test data, a view halfway between the best and the worst loss of an asset gives a posterior CVaR within `5e-8` relative of the target. |
| A CVaR view at the worst loss of its asset on the support, under `>=` or `==`, and a CVaR, EVaR or RLVaR view whose left side is constant on the support, at that constant, in `EntropyPoolingPrior` | Raised a `DomainError`: `View … is too extreme`. | Solves, because a posterior that puts at least `alpha` on the worst scenarios meets the first view. Where the prior puts at least `alpha` on the worst scenarios, the posterior is the prior to the tolerance of the solver. A view with a constant left side that the prior meets drops, so the posterior is the prior. A target above the worst loss still raises the `DomainError`. `MeucciEntropyPoolingPrior` takes an `==` view at the worst loss too, where v0.33 raised the `DomainError`. It writes the view as one row, which puts at least `alpha` of the posterior on the worst scenarios, and it searches only the other CVaR views. With no other view, the posterior is the prior when the prior meets the row. Otherwise it is the prior scaled to `alpha` on the worst scenarios and to `1 - alpha` on the rest. |
| `JuMPEntropyPooling(; alg = LogEntropyPooling())` over a prior with a zero weight in `w` | Raised an `ErrorException` from JuMP: ``Affine expression contains an invalid term `Inf * x[33]` ``. | Solves. A scenario of zero prior weight takes zero posterior weight, and the `kld` equals the `kld` of `OptimEntropyPooling` within `2e-7` relative. |
| `MeucciEntropyPoolingPrior` with more than one view in `ConditionalValueatRiskView`, whose search meets a trial point at which the inner solve fails | Stopped the fit with `Entropy pooling optimisation failed`. | Rejects the trial point, and the search moves on. A failed inner solve at the start of the search, or at its last point, still raises. |
| `MeucciEntropyPoolingPrior` with a CVaR view whose posterior value at risk is below zero, such as a view at half the prior CVaR at `alpha = 0.5`, or a view just below the worst loss of an asset with few losses above zero | One view raised `CVaR entropy pooling optimisation failed`. More than one view returned a posterior that missed its target with no error, by 0.55 % in one measurement. | Solves. The search of the value at risk starts at the lowest loss of the asset, not at zero. With one view, a search whose root is above zero runs as before. When every loss of the asset on the support is above zero, the search of one view starts at the lowest loss, and the start of the search of more than one view is the midpoint of the lowest loss and the target. With more than one view, the box of the search is `[lo, B]` in place of `[0, B]`, where `lo` is the lowest loss and `B` the target, so every such posterior moves: by up to `4.2e-4` of the largest weight in two measured cases. The warning of `MeucciEntropyPoolingPrior` gives the miss under the default `dm_opt` and under a tighter one. |
| `owa_l_moment(T, k)` where a binomial coefficient of `T` overflows an `Int`, such as `owa_l_moment(1000, 8)` | Raised `OverflowError: binomial(1000, 8) overflows`. | Gives the weights. The binomial coefficients take the float type of the weights. |
| `no_bounds_risk_measure`, `no_bounds_no_risk_expr_risk_measure`, `no_risk_expr_risk_measure`, `bounds_risk_measure` and `unit_scale_risk_measure` on a measure that the JuMP model does not optimise, such as `MeanReturn()` or `ThirdCentralMoment()`, and `unit_scale_risk_measure(Skewness())` | Raised a `MethodError`. The five functions had methods for a `RiskMeasure` and a `HierarchicalRiskMeasure` alone, and `Skewness` had three of the five. | Returns the measure with no change. `Skewness` has all five methods. |
| `MaximumRatio(; kmin = 0)` | Raised `DomainError: kmin must be > 0`. | Builds, with no floor on the scale variable `k`. A negative `kmin` raises a `DomainError`. |
| `RiskBudgeting` with `AssetRiskBudgeting(; alg = MixedIntegerRiskBudgeting())` and a net budget below one, such as `bgt = 0.5` | Did not meet the budget. The model held the row `sum(w) - k >= 0`, which a net budget below one meets only at `k <= 0`. | Solves on the budget. The row is gone. |
| `port_opt_view` of a `RiskBudgeting` with `MixedIntegerRiskBudgeting()` or with `LogRiskBudgeting()` and no orthant vector | Raised `TypeError: in keyword argument alg, expected RiskBudgetingFormulation, got a value of type Nothing`. | Views the estimator, and keeps its formulation. |
| `NearOptimalCentering` with an infinite weight bound, such as `WeightBounds(; lb = 0.0, ub = Inf)`, or with a negative lower bound whose centre holds a short weight | The barrier wrote `-log(w_i)` and `-log(ub_i - w_i)` for each asset. An infinite bound put `Inf` in a cone row, and the solve failed. A negative lower bound added the hidden row `w_i > 0`, so a centre with a short weight was not feasible. | Solves. The barrier has one term for each finite bound, `-log(w_i - lb_i)` and `-log(ub_i - w_i)`, and no term for an infinite bound or a fixed weight. |
| `VarianceSkewKurtosis` with a vector bound on one term and a `Frontier` on another, such as a vector `ub` on `vr` and a `Frontier` `ub` on `kt` | Raised a `FieldError`, because the rebuild of the sweep read the vector as the `Frontier`. | Sweeps the `Frontier` of its term, and holds the vector bound of the other term. |
| The end points of a `Frontier` sweep under `MaximumRatio` with `rf > 0` | The end-point solve on the ratio model failed, and the sweep raised an `ArgumentError` that named the failed maximum-return solve. | Solves. The end points come from a model with `k = 1`, see [Results that move](@ref migration-0-34-numbers). A sweep under `MaximumRatio` still needs the solver wrapped in `ParametricOptInterface.Optimizer`, as in v0.33. |
| `RegimeAdjustedExpWeightedCovariance` on `Float32` data with `debias = ExactDebias()` or `debias = LawDebias()`, and with `regime_target = MahalanobisTarget(; recursion = GaussianLevelRecursion())` under any `debias` | Under `DiagonalTarget` with `FirstMomentRegimeAdjusted` or `LogRegimeAdjusted`, raised `ArgumentError: matrix contains Infs or NaNs`. Under `MahalanobisTarget`, gave a covariance of `NaN` at every entry with no error. | Fits, and keeps `Float32`. On twelve configurations, the largest relative gap to the `Float64` fit is `1.9e-5`. Each `Float64` fit does not move. |
| `RegimeAdjustedExpWeightedCovariance` with a `MahalanobisTarget` on `BigFloat` data at 256 bits, or at any precision whose `eps` is below `exp(-61)` | Raised a `BoundsError` from the lattice of the level bias. | Fits, within `1.1e-11` of the `Float64` fit. A `BigFloat` fit needs `GenericLinearAlgebra`, which gives the `eigen` of a symmetric `BigFloat` matrix. |
| `cov(pc)` and `cor(pc)` with no data on a `PortfolioOptimisersCovariance` that `partial_fit!` folded over an `ExpWeightedCovariance`, a `RegimeAdjustedExpWeightedCovariance`, a seed-windowed `WindowedCovariance` or a `GeodesicShrinkageCovariance`, and `cor` of a seed-windowed `WindowedCovariance` and `std` of a seed-windowed `WindowedVariance` with no data | Raised an error, such as a `FieldError` for the field `n` of the state. | Gives the matrix of the batch route on the folded rows. Under `MatrixProcessing(; dn = Denoise())`, on 150 observations of 6 assets, the largest gap to the batch route is `6.7e-14`. |
| `ThirdCentralMoment(; ve = SimpleVariance())` | Raised a `MethodError`, because `ve` was not a keyword. | Divides the third moment by the cube of the standard deviation. This is the standardised skewness that the v0.33 functor of `Skewness` gave. The default `ve = nothing` gives the raw third moment, as in v0.33. |
| `NestedClustered` or `Stacking` with an online walk-forward in `cv`, such as `cv = OnlineIndexWalkForward(20, 10)` | Raised a `TypeError`, because `OptimisationCrossValidation` took no online walk-forward. | Builds the estimator. |
| `RadialTailDecay` on a prior whose `chol` is a nonsingular square factor with a zero on its diagonal, such as the Cholesky factor with its rows permuted | Raised a `DomainError` that said that the covariance matrix is singular. | Whitens the sample with the R factor of the QR factorisation of `chol`, which states the same covariance matrix. |
| `predict` of a `PipelineResult` on returns data with no asset panel, where the training window had one, or the reverse | Raised an `ArgumentError` that said that the two windows did not come from one ingestion. | Predicts. A table with no gap and no field of the asset panel gives returns data with no panel, see [Results that move](@ref migration-0-34-numbers), so the check reads the asset names alone. |
| `CrossSectionalFactorPrior` whose exposures read the returns alone, on returns data with no asset panel | Raised an error that said that the step carries no panel. | Fits, in the batch fit and in the carry fold. A read of a named field of the panel on such data raises a `KeyError` that names both causes: the data carry no panel, or a panel of the two universe masks alone. |
| `CategoricalPanelInput` with a fill policy of your own that has no field `val` | Raised a `FieldError`, because the check read `alg.val` of every policy. | Builds. The check reads `val` of the library policies that carry one, and of no other policy. |
| A panel input on `Int` data with `ConstantPanelFill(; val = 0.5)`, where the first column has no blank and a later column has one | Raised `InexactError: Int64(0.5)`. | Fills the blank, and the field is `Float64`. |
| `panel_feature_matrix` of a categorical field of the asset panel with more levels than assets | Raised a `BoundsError`. | Gives the feature matrix. |
| A JuMP model of `GenericValueatRiskRange` whose two tails are `ValueatRisk(; alg = DistributionValueatRisk(; dist))` over the same covariance, on a law whose mean is far from zero, such as `dist = Normal(2, 1)` | Was `DUAL_INFEASIBLE`. Each tail held its own cone, and the coefficient of the gain tail was negative, so a minimisation drove its cone to infinity. `ValueatRiskRange` with the same law solved. | Solves. The two tails share one cone, with the sum of their coefficients, as `ValueatRiskRange` does, and the `ub` of each tail still bounds its own value. On 300 observations of 6 assets at `alpha = beta = 0.05` under `MinimumRisk`, the minimum is `0.0123379`, the minimum of `ValueatRiskRange`. The tails can read different `mu`: a gain tail with `mu = zeros(6)` beside the loss tail solves to `0.0116972`. A tail whose coefficient is negative beside a tail of another kind, such as `GenericValueatRiskRange(; loss = ConditionalValueatRisk(), gain)`, is not convex, and its model is still unbounded. |
| `risk_contribution` and `risk_gradient` of `ExpectedReturn` or `ExpectedReturnRiskRatio`, and every call on a measure that holds one: a vector such as `[ExpectedReturn(), ConditionalValueatRisk()]`, a side of `NonOptimisationRiskRatio`, the `rk` of `MeanReturnRiskRatio`, and the `r` of `RiskTrackingRiskMeasure` | Raised the `ArgumentError` of `risk_input_kind`, because the two measures read `pr.mu` and the route of the prior passed `pr.X` to them. `expected_risk` of either measure alone answered. | Evaluates, without and with fees. Each contribution equals `w .* g / d`, with `g` a central difference of the value and `d` the degree of the measure, within `2.2e-10` relative on the cases of the review. |
| `RiskTrackingRiskMeasure` whose tracked measure is a `MeanReturnRiskRatio`, a `NonOptimisationRiskRatio` or an `ExpectedReturnRiskRatio`, such as `RiskTrackingRiskMeasure(; r = MeanReturnRiskRatio(; rk = ConditionalValueatRisk()), tr = WeightsTracking(; w = wb))` | The constructor raised a `MethodError` from `no_bounds_no_risk_expr_risk_measure`. | Builds. `expected_risk`, `risk_contribution` and `risk_gradient` evaluate it, also with fees. The value reads the series of the active weights, net of the fee of `w`. |
| `port_opt_view(rd, t, j)` of a `ReturnsResult` with one timestamp `t`, such as `rd.ts[2]` | Raised `ArgumentError: invalid index`, because the view read the timestamp as a position. | Gives the one observation at `t`, with a matrix of one row, as `port_opt_view(rd, [t], j)` does. A timestamp that `rd.ts` does not hold raises an `ArgumentError` that names it. One timestamp in an asset or a factor index raises an `ArgumentError`, as a vector of timestamps does there. |
| `port_opt_view(pr, i, j)` of a `PricesResult` with `Date` timestamps `i` of `DateTime` prices | Raised a `MethodError` of `getindex` on a `TimeArray`. | Selects the observations at midnight of each date, as the view of a `ReturnsResult` does. |
| `port_opt_view` of a `ReturnsResult` or of a `PricesResult` with one integer or one name in an index, such as `port_opt_view(rd, 2, :)`, `port_opt_view(rd, :, "a")`, `port_opt_view(rd, 2)`, `port_opt_view(rd, :, :, 1)` or `port_opt_view(pr, :, 2)`, and `port_opt_view(pr, t)` with one timestamp `t` | A `ReturnsResult` raised a `TypeError` that named a field, such as `nx`, or `ArgumentError: invalid index` for a name. A `PricesResult` raised the `ArgumentError` that lists its call shapes. | Gives a view of one observation, one asset or one factor, with the matrix shape of the data: one integer `n` selects as `n:n` does, and one name as `[name]` does. One timestamp of a `PricesResult` selects the one observation at it, as on a `ReturnsResult`, and a timestamp that the prices do not hold raises an `ArgumentError` that names it. A name in the observation index raises an `ArgumentError`, as a vector of names does there. One `Bool` in an index raises an `ArgumentError`, as Julia refuses it, and a vector of `Bool` is still a mask of the axis. |
| `Accessors.set` on a `MultiPeriodPredictionResult` or a `PipelineResult`, such as `Accessors.@set res.pred = res.pred[1:3]` or `Accessors.@set res.names = ("a", "b")` | Raised a `MethodError`. The keyword constructor of `MultiPeriodPredictionResult` does not take `mrd`, which its inner constructor computes, and `PipelineResult` had no keyword constructor. | Gives the new Result. `mrd` is computed again from the new `pred`, and a patch of `mrd` raises an `ArgumentError` that names the fields to set. `PipelineResult(; names, results, ctx)` is the new keyword constructor. |
| A `Pipeline` whose step is a precomputed optimisation result, such as `Pipeline(; steps = (EmpiricalPrior(), res))` with `res = optimise(EqualWeighted(), rd)` | Raised an `ArgumentError` that said that the pipeline cannot infer the slot of the result. A `TimeDependent` schedule of such results ran, because the check did not see the result of each fold. | Builds. The result writes `:opt` and reads `:returns`, as an optimisation estimator does, and the fit predicts with its weights, as a fold of the schedule does. The inner constructor now checks the pipeline of each fold, so the result needs the slot. |
| `Detone` on a matrix whose leading eigenvalues tie, such as an identity matrix, which `Denoise` makes when it finds no signal, or a `MatrixProcessing` with both `dn = Denoise()` and `dt = Detone()` on such a sample | `ArgumentError: matrix contains Infs or NaNs`, or a `NaN` matrix under `pdm = nothing`. `Detone(; n = size(X, 2))` on a covariance raised a `DomainError` of `sqrt` on most samples, raised the `ArgumentError` on some, and gave a matrix of rounding noise on the others. | Removes the largest number of leading modes, at most `n`, whose smallest eigenvalue stands above the next one, and gives the matrix back when none does. An asset that the removed modes hold in full keeps a unit row, so `n = size(X, 2)` gives an identity matrix, and the diagonal of a covariance returns. |

### [Calls that return another type](@id migration-0-34-returns)

Each call in this list returns a value of another type, and the values in it do not move. Each row states the reads that do not change and the reads that raise.

- **`range_tails` returns a `RangeTails`, not a named tuple.** v0.33 returned `(; loss, gain)`. `tails.loss`, `tails.gain` and `(; loss, gain) = tails` read the same two measures as in v0.33. A range of your own returns `PortfolioOptimisers.RangeTails(loss, gain)`, a public type, from its method of `range_tails`. The constructor refuses a tail whose `settings.rke` is `true` with an `ArgumentError`, because the range registers its risk expression once. `pairs(tails)`, `keys(tails)`, `tails[1]`, `loss, gain = tails`, `merge(tails, x)` and `length(tails)` raise a `MethodError`, and `tails == (; loss, gain)` is `false`.
- **`panel_align_active` returns the plain tuple `(pnl, n)`, as `findmax` does.** v0.33 returned the named tuple `(; pnl, n)`. Write `aligned, n = panel_align_active(pnl, fields)` where v0.33 read `res.pnl` and `res.n`. `res.pnl` and `res.n` raise a `FieldError`. The aligned panel and the count do not change.
- **`cross_sectional_factor_axis` returns a `CrossSectionalFactorAxisResult`.** v0.33 returned the named tuple `(; nf, fam)`. `ax.nf`, `ax.fam` and `(; nf, fam) = ax` read the same lists as in v0.33. Two Results are not `==`, because the type compares its vectors by identity, so compare `ax.nf` and `ax.fam`. A call of `keys(ax)` raises a `MethodError`. The constructor refuses a `fam` whose length is not the length of `nf` with a `DimensionMismatch`.
- **Eight verbs of the forecast evaluation and the diagnostics return a type, not a named tuple.** `descriptor_scores` returns a `DescriptorScoresResult`, `forecast_ic_summary` a `ForecastICSummaryResult` of two `ICSummaryResult`s, `forecast_portfolio` a `ForecastPortfolioResult`, `forecast_quantile_spread` a `ForecastQuantileSpreadResult`, `forecast_holding_period` and `forecast_decay` a `ForecastWindowResult`, `forecast_calibration` a `ForecastCalibrationResult` whose `curve` is a `ForecastCalibrationCurveResult`, and `idio_calibration_summary` an `IdioCalibrationSummaryResult`. Each field keeps its name, so a read as `res.ann_ir` or `(; slope, curve) = res` does not change. `keys`, `pairs`, `merge` and a splat of a result raise a `MethodError`, and `values(res)` returns the result itself: read `fieldnames(typeof(res))` or `getfield(res, k)` instead. The library defines `==` on no struct, so compare two answers by their fields.
- **The results of the forecast evaluation and of the factor model diagnostics check their values.** `exposure_ic_summary` over a bare matrix returns the `ExposureICSummaryResult` of its other two methods, with `nothing` in `nf` and `fam`. `DataFrame(res)` writes a `ForecastWindowResult` or a `ForecastCalibrationCurveResult` as a table. Each constructor of the eight verbs above checks its values: a p-value, a hit rate or a coverage share outside `[0, 1]` and a deviation below zero raise a `DomainError`, and `NaN` passes. `ForecastSummaryResult`, `ExposureICSummaryResult` and `FactorSummaryResult` check their values in the same way now, and each takes a keyword constructor.
- **`covariance_forecast_step` and `covariance_forecast_portfolio` return a type, not a named tuple.** `covariance_forecast_step` returns a `CovarianceForecastStepResult`, where v0.33 returned `(; n_valid, mahalanobis_ratio, diagonal_ratio, qlike, frobenius, standardised_return, portfolio_qlike)`. `covariance_forecast_portfolio` returns a `CovarianceForecastPortfolioResult`, where v0.33 returned `(; standardised_return, portfolio_qlike)`. A caller that reads a field by name, as `step.qlike` or `(; qlike) = step`, does not change. A splat `(; step...)`, `keys`, `pairs` and `merge` raise a `MethodError`. `==` and `isequal` of two Results compare their arrays by identity, so compare the fields. Each constructor checks its values: a negative `n_valid`, an `n_valid` above the count of assets, and portfolio fields of two lengths or two sizes raise. `CovarianceForecastSummaryResult` checks its values now too, and it takes a keyword constructor; v0.33 checked nothing.
- **`forecast_evaluation_summary(fe)` and `covariance_forecast_summary(cfer)` give scalar fields.** v0.33 gave a vector of length one in each field of a summary of one evaluation. Read `fs.spearman_mean_ic` where v0.33 read `fs.spearman_mean_ic[1]`. A spread of the forecast summary and `exceedance` of the covariance summary become a vector over the tail fractions or the levels. Pass a vector, `[fe]`, to keep the shape of v0.33.
- **A `Pipeline` keeps the container form of its steps.** v0.33 converted the steps of `Pipeline(; steps = [EmpiricalPrior(), InverseVolatility()])` to a `Tuple`, and its names too. v0.34 keeps a vector of steps as a vector, and its `names`, and the `names` and `results` of its `PipelineResult`, are vectors too. A `Tuple` of steps gives tuples, as in v0.33. The weights and every other value do not change. A read by index, by name, `length`, iteration and `map` do not change. A method of your own that dispatches on `pipe.steps::Tuple` or `res.results::Tuple` raises a `MethodError` for the vector form, so pass the steps as a `Tuple` to keep the form of v0.33.

  The positional `Pipeline(names, steps, cache)` takes a vector of names and a vector of steps too, where v0.33 raised a `MethodError`. `PipelineStep(; reads = [:returns], ...)` keeps the vector that you give, where v0.33 raised a `TypeError`. The keyword constructor of `PipelineResult` is new, and it takes a vector of names and a vector of results, as the positional `PipelineResult(names, results, ctx)` did in v0.33. A tuple of steps compiles the methods of a pipeline for each set of step types, and a vector compiles them once for each element type, so a vector can cost less time to the first result. An online step of a vector of two steps took 19.3 µs, against 15.0 µs for the tuple form, at 20 assets.
- **`gradient_state_seed` of `GradientMomentum`, `RootMeanSquareGradient` and `AdaptiveMomentGradient` returns a `PortfolioOptimisers.GradientAverageState`, and `transform_gradient!` returns the state with the transformed gradient.** v0.33 returned the zero average, the zero squared average or the pair of both from `gradient_state_seed`, and `ĝ` alone from `transform_gradient!(grad, gs, g)`. Write `gs, ĝ = transform_gradient!(grad, gs, g)` where v0.33 wrote `ĝ = transform_gradient!(grad, gs, g)`, and read `gs.v` and `gs.m` where v0.33 read the vector or `gs[1]` and `gs[2]`. A call with the state of v0.33, such as `transform_gradient!(GradientMomentum(), zeros(N), g)`, raises a `MethodError`, and so does `gs[1]`. The transformed gradient does not change.
- **`traverse_concrete_subtypes` returns a `Vector{Type}`.** v0.33 returned a `Vector{Any}`. The entries do not change. Pass a `Vector{Any}` as `ctarr` to collect into a vector of your own type.
- **Returns, prices and panel fields of integer data hold a float copy.** `ReturnsResult`, `PricesResult`, `NumericPanelField` and `TensorPanelField` convert an integer element type once, when you build them, so `eltype(rd.X)` of `ReturnsResult(; nx, X = [1 2; 3 4])` is `Float64`, where v0.33 kept `Int`. The values do not change, and a fit on such an input gives the fit of the float data, as in v0.33. A `Float32`, a `Rational` or any other element type is kept, as in v0.33.
- **`describe` of an asset panel with no field gives `Float64` share columns.** v0.33 gave the columns `missing` and `active_missing`, and the `missing` column of a `by` report, the element type `Any`.

### [Lot units of a finite allocation](@id migration-0-34-lot-units)

Both finite allocators buy each asset in whole lots of its own lot unit, for fractional shares and for board lots. The `unit` field takes a `LotUnit`, one number for every asset or one value for each asset, or a `LotUnitEstimator`, which names the lot units by asset or group and resolves against the new `sets` field of the allocator.

| v0.33 | v0.34 |
| --- | --- |
| `GreedyAllocation(; unit = 10)` | `GreedyAllocation(; unit = LotUnit(10))`. A bare number raises a `TypeError`. |
| `GreedyAllocation(; unit = 0.01, kwargs = (; digits = 2))` | `GreedyAllocation(; unit = LotUnit(0.01))`. The `args` and `kwargs` fields are gone. The first pass floors the lot count, so a fractional lot unit buys whole lots with no rounding keyword. |
| `PortfolioOptimisers.roundmult` | Gone, with the rounding that it did. |
| `DiscreteAllocation(; slv)`, which bought whole shares only | Takes `unit`, `sets` and `strict`. The default `LotUnit(1)` buys whole shares, as in v0.33, and the default fallback reads the same `unit`. The model counts lots in its integer variable and holds the shares in an expression, so a side with more than one optimal book can return another of them, with the same objective. |

### [A count or a fraction states its reading](@id migration-0-34-count-fraction)

A field that takes the size of a part of a set holds a `CountOf` or a `FractionOf`. `CountOf(n)` is `n` members, an `Integer` of at least one. `FractionOf(f)` is a share of the set, with `f` in `(0, 1]`. v0.33 read the type of a plain number: an `Integer` was a count, and any other real number was a fraction. Julia promotes the elements of a vector literal, so a search grid could turn a count into a float. A plain number is still a shorthand, and the field then holds the wrapper.

| v0.33 | v0.34 |
| --- | --- |
| `x.active == 0.2`, `x.sbar == 25`, `cv.subset_size == 3`, and the same read of `neighbours`, `window_size`, `train_size` and `test_size` | The field holds `FractionOf(0.2)`, `CountOf(25)` or `CountOf(3)`. Read `x.active.val` for the number. |
| A plain `1.0` in one of these fields | Raises a `DomainError`, because a grid promotes the count `1` to it. Write `CountOf(1)` for one member, or `FractionOf(1)` for the whole set. |
| `QuantileRule(; best = 0.2)` | `RankRule(; best = FractionOf(0.2))`, or the shorthand `RankRule(; best = 0.2)`. `QuantileRule` is gone. One rule can now take a count from one end and a fraction from the other. |
| `RankRule(; best = 0, worst = 5)` | `RankRule(; worst = 5)`. A count is at least one, so `nothing` states a tail that takes no asset. |
| `HopCount(; n = HopCountQuantile(; q = 0.25))` and `PathLength(; dmax = PathLengthQuantile(; q = 0.25))` | `HopCount(; n = FractionOf(0.25))` and `PathLength(; dmax = FractionOf(0.25))`. The budgets do not move. `HopCountQuantile` and `PathLengthQuantile` are gone. `q = 0` is refused, and `CountOf(1)` states that hop budget. |
| `HopCount(; n = 2).n == 2` | `HopCount(; n = 2).n` is `CountOf(2)`. Read `.n.val` for the number of hops. A plain float, such as `HopCount(; n = 0.25)`, is a fraction, where v0.33 refused it. In `PathLength(; dmax = 0.25)` a plain number stays a radius. |
| `ConditionalValueatRisk(; alpha = ScenarioCount(; n = 25))`, and every other significance slot | `ConditionalValueatRisk(; alpha = CountOf(25))`. The level is `25 / T`, as before. `ScenarioCount` is gone. A plain number in `alpha` is still the level itself. |
| `JuMPOptimiser(; l2c = EffectiveAssetFloor(; fraction = 0.5))`, and the same in `linfc`, `lpc` and `ProgrammeAllocationSet` | `JuMPOptimiser(; l2c = FractionOf(0.5))`. The ceiling does not move. `EffectiveAssetFloor` is gone, and `CountOf(m)` states the floor as `m` effective assets. A plain number in `l2c` is still the ceiling itself. |
| `WildBootstrapMeanTest(; min_periods = 2.5)`, and the same `OverlapMeanTest` | Raises a `TypeError`. `min_periods` is a count of windows, so it is an `Integer`. |
| `ActiveAssetsUncertaintyAlgorithm(; active = 1 // 5)` | Builds and calibrates. v0.33 raised a `TypeError` when you built the algorithm, because `active` took an `Integer` or an `AbstractFloat` only. |

### [Arrays of rank three hold their observations last](@id migration-0-34-layout)

Every array of rank three that the library takes or gives holds its observations on its last axis, so the matrix of one observation is one contiguous block. A v0.33 array of shape `T × N × K` is a v0.34 array of shape `N × K × T`, and `permutedims(A, (2, 3, 1))` converts one. A matrix over observations, such as the returns `X`, the weights `W`, a mask, the factor returns `f` or a one-factor exposure, does not change.

| Where | v0.33 | v0.34 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `Z` of `cross_sectional_regression`, `predict`, `cross_sectional_r2` and `mean_cross_sectional_r2`; the field `Ms` of `CrossSectionalFactorModel`; `Ms` of `factor_family_basis` and `reduce_exposures`, and the result of `reduce_exposures`; `B` of `factor_attribution`, `forecast_factor_correlation`, `plot_forecast_factor_correlation`, `exposure_ic`, the `exposure_*` diagnostics and the `cs_regression_*` diagnostics | `observations × assets × factors` | `assets × factors × observations` |
| `factor_exposure` of `OneHotExposure` and `CurrencyExposure`, and `B` of `cross_sectional_groups` | `observations × assets × levels` | `assets × levels × observations` |
| `cs_gram`, and a stack given to `expand_factor_covariance` or `reduce_factor_covariance` | `observations × factors × factors` | `factors × factors × observations` |
| `dropped_factor_weights(fcb)` with no observation index | `observations × constrained families × reduced factors` | `constrained families × reduced factors × observations` |
| `descriptor_scores(...).S` | `observations × assets × descriptors` | `assets × descriptors × observations` |
| `vals`, `omsk` and `pmsk` of a time-varying `TensorPanelField` or `TensorPanelInput`, and `panel_field_values` of such a field | `observations × assets × labels` | `assets × labels × observations` |
| `feature_matrix` and `panel_feature_matrix` of a time-varying panel, and the `Z` of `distance(::FeatureDistance, Z)` and `cor_and_dist(::FeatureDistance, Z)` | `observations × assets × features` | `assets × features × observations` |

A static `TensorPanelField` and a static feature matrix keep `assets × labels` and `assets × features`.

### [Results that move](@id migration-0-34-numbers)

- **The last bits of a few kernels on arrays of rank three.** The matrix of one observation is now contiguous, so a product on it runs in BLAS. The stack of `expand_factor_covariance`, a realised `factor_attribution`, the panel collapse of a meta-optimiser and the mean of `AggregateFeatures` can differ from v0.33 in the last bits.
