On a panel of 60 assets, 24 features and 300 observations, the mean of `AggregateFeatures` moves by `4e-16` relative and the panel collapse by `4e-16`. A realised `factor_attribution` of 20 assets moves by up to `4e-15`, in its unattributed remainder. A distance near zero amplifies the change of the mean, so the angular distance of `FeatureDistance(; alg = AggregateFeatures())` on that panel moves by `1e-13` relative to its largest entry. `AggregateDistances` at `dims = 2` moves by `3e-15`. The new layout keeps every value of a `CrossSectionalFactorPrior` fit bit for bit.
- **The default `lambda = PrecisionBlend()` of a `CrossSectionalFactorPrior` reads the effective count of the factor moments.** The rule gives the factor mean the weight `max(0, 1 - K / (T_e B))`. v0.33 took `T_e` as the count of observations. v0.34 takes the smaller of the effective counts that the factor prior states for its mean and its covariance, and an estimator that weights or decays its rows states fewer. So the weight falls, and it can reach `0`. On a panel of 60 assets, 4 industries and 300 observations, with a market, an industry and two style factors, the resolved `lambda` is `0.1700` in v0.33 and `0` in v0.34. With no return forecast, every expected return of that fit is then `0`. To keep the v0.33 `mu`, state the `lambda` of the v0.33 fit as a number, which `res.rr.lambda` of that fit gives.
- **`NearestQuantilePrediction`, `sort_by_measure` and `quantile_by_measure` orient each measure before they reduce a vector.** v0.33 reduced the raw risks and then applied one sign, so a vector of measures that disagree on orientation, which `NearestQuantilePrediction` admitted, gave a number with no orientation and a wrong path. v0.34 multiplies each measure by `-1` when a smaller value is better, before the reduction. The result depends on the orientation of the measures:
  - **Measures of which a smaller value is better**, one or a vector. Under `SumScalariser`, the three functions select and sort as in v0.33. A vector under `MaxScalariser` now selects and sorts by the best measure of each path, where v0.33 used the worst risk. Write `MinScalariser()` to keep the v0.33 result, and `MaxScalariser()` for the v0.33 result of `MinScalariser`. `LogSumExpScalariser` also moves.
  - **Measures of which a bigger value is better**, one or a vector. `sort_by_measure` sorts as in v0.33 under every scalariser. Under the default call with no sign, `NearestQuantilePrediction` and `quantile_by_measure` now read `q = 1` as the worst path. Write `1 - q` to keep the v0.33 path, when `q_kwargs` keeps `alpha` equal to `beta`, as the default does.
- **`GreedyAllocation` with a lot unit below one.** v0.33 rounded the first pass with `Base.round`, which rounds to a whole share under the default `kwargs`. So a target could round above itself and stop the first pass early. v0.34 floors the lot count of each target. The shares of such an allocation differ, and they are multiples of the lot unit. A lot unit that is a whole number gives the v0.33 shares. A lot unit above one that is not a whole number gives other shares too, because v0.33 rounded the first pass to a whole share: at a lot unit of `1.5`, v0.33 bought `218` shares where v0.34 buys `217.5`.
- **The `cash` of a finite allocation is the cash that the account holds after the trade.** v0.33 reported the cash that the long side did not spend. That number left out the cash that no side received: the cash outside a budget below one, and the cash of a book whose long cash the floor at zero of `ProceedsCollateral` sets. On `w = [0.3, 0.25, 0.15, 0.1]`, `prices = [12.3, 45.6, 7.89, 101.1]` and `cash = 10000`, the shares do not change, and `cash` is `2038.6`, where v0.33 gave `38.6`. Under `ProceedsCollateral`, `sum(cost) + cash + fees` is the input cash. Under `CashCollateral`, `sum(abs, cost) + cash + fees` is. A negative `cash` is money that the book borrows.
- **`performance_summary` of a prediction whose optimiser gives many portfolios gives one entry for each member.** v0.33 gave the scalar figures of the first member and dropped the others. Each field is now a vector with one entry for each member, and each member reads its own series, benchmark, turnover and weights. Read `ps.sharpe[1]` to keep the v0.33 number. The same holds for weights in a `VecVecNum`, and for an `OptimisationResult` of a frontier, which v0.33 refused.
- **`log_wealth_regret` of a prediction whose optimiser gives many portfolios gives one Result for each member.** v0.33 gave one `LogWealthRegretResult` for the first member and dropped the others. v0.34 gives a `Vector` of `LogWealthRegretResult`, one entry for each member. Each member meets its own benchmark series, and the `path_length` of an entry reads the targets of its own member of the comparator. Two sides with many members pair by index, and a different count of members raises a `DimensionMismatch`. A side with one series pairs with every member of the other side. Read `regs[1]` to keep the v0.33 number. A pair of single series gives one Result, as in v0.33.
- **A selection rule and a redundancy algorithm drop a non-finite score.** v0.33 compared a `NaN` through `isless` and `>`. `RankRule` and `QuantileRule` (now `RankRule` with a `FractionOf`) could then return an empty mask, `action = :drop` kept the `NaN`, `QuantileRule` counted it in the size of the universe, a `ThresholdRule` with one bound admitted an infinite score on the open side, `CorrelationComponents` and `ClusterGroups` kept no member of a group whose first member had a `NaN`, and `PairwiseCorrelation` dropped both assets of a pair with one `NaN`. In v0.34 a `NaN` or an infinite score is outside every tail and every band, under each `action`, and the partner of a `NaN` asset survives their pair. A selector with a risk measure raises a `DomainError` on a non-finite score of a column with no gap, as in v0.33, so the change reaches a direct call of `rule_keep` or `redundancy_keep`, and the `NaN` that `FoldRiskScorer` and `WeightMassScorer` write at an asset that too few folds scored.
- **A corrected weighted fit with no positive divisor is `NaN`.** A corrected fit on one observation of positive weight divides by zero, and the weighted mean of one value can differ from it by one unit in the last place. So v0.33 gave `Inf` where the fit with no weights gives `NaN`: `var(SimpleVariance(; w = aweights([w])), x)` was `Inf` in 493 of 2000 draws and `NaN` in the others. Frequency weights that sum to less than one gave a variance of zero or below, such as `-0.0` for `fweights([0.4])`. v0.34 gives `NaN` in each case, in `SimpleVariance`, in the weighted `cov` of `GeneralCovariance` and `Covariance`, and in `StdValue` and `VarValue`. A fit with a positive divisor gives the v0.33 number bit for bit.
- **`var(SimpleVariance(; w, cvg = CoveragePolicy()), x)` on one series reads `w`.** v0.33 ignored the weights of the vector arm with no message, and gave the variance with no weights. On 12 observations with analytic weights, v0.33 gave `0.0013162` and v0.34 gives `0.0011678`, the weighted variance.
- **The default drop score of `PairwiseCorrelation` and `CorrelationComponents` skips a correlation that is not defined.** Under a `ce` with no matrix repair, such as `Covariance()`, the correlation with a constant column is `NaN`, so in v0.33 the summary of every column was `NaN`. `PairwiseCorrelation` then dropped both assets of each correlated pair, and `CorrelationComponents` raised an `IsEmptyError`. v0.34 summarises the finite entries of each column, and a weighted `measure` loses the weight of each skipped entry. On a correlated pair `A`, `B`, an unrelated `C` and a constant `D`, both algorithms keep `["B", "C", "D"]`, where v0.33 kept `["C", "D"]` and raised. The default `ce = PortfolioOptimisersCovariance()` refuses the `NaN` in its repair, as in v0.33.
- **The correlation of a constant column is `NaN` on the diagonal too.** The correlation of an asset with zero variance is `0 / 0` at every asset, itself included. v0.33 gave such an asset `NaN` off the diagonal and one on it under `Covariance`, `GeneralCovariance`, `ExpWeightedCovariance`, `RegimeAdjustedExpWeightedCovariance`, `ImpliedVolatility`, `SpearmanCovariance` and `KendallCovariance`, and a `NaN` diagonal under a `CoveragePolicy`. Now every one of them gives the `NaN` diagonal, as `DistanceCovariance` did. So `cor(Covariance(), X)[i, i]` is `NaN` for a constant column `i`, and `distance` of a covariance matrix with a zero variance gives a `NaN` row. A weighted `SimpleCovariance` correlation with fewer than two observations of positive weight is `NaN` at every entry, as the unweighted fit on one observation is. An estimator that defines the dependence of a constant, such as `GerberCovariance` or a shrinkage in `Covariance(; ce)`, keeps its values.
- **A constant other than zero gets the same `NaN` row.** The mean of a constant can differ from it by one unit in the last place, so under `Covariance`, `GeneralCovariance` and the default `PortfolioOptimisersCovariance`, cash at a constant return of `0.0005` has a variance of about `1e-38`, not zero. v0.33 gave its row a correlation of about `1e-18` with each asset and one on the diagonal, and `HierarchicalRiskParity` and `HierarchicalEqualRiskContribution` gave the cash the whole weight. Now the row is `NaN` at every entry for every constant, also under observation weights, where a column that is constant on the observations of positive weight counts. The fit drops the asset, as it drops cash at `0.0`. The normalised mutual information of `MutualInfoCovariance` gives every constant the `NaN` row too, under each bin rule.
- **`number_effective_assets`, and the `n_eff` of `performance_summary`, read the concentration of a book and not its leverage.** The function is `sum(abs, w)^2 / dot(w, w)`, where v0.33 took `1 / dot(w, w)`. The two forms give one value on every book whose absolute weights sum to one, such as a long-only book with a budget of one. Elsewhere the value moves: `[1.0, -1.0]` gives `2`, where v0.33 gave `0.5`, and a book of zeros gives `NaN`, where v0.33 gave `Inf`. `performance_summary` also gives `long_exposure`, `short_exposure`, `gross_exposure` and `net_exposure` for each entry that holds weights, and `DataFrame(ps)` writes them after `n_eff`, so a measure cannot take one of those names.
- **`currency_excess_index` holds a cash index across a gap inside its span.** A deposit accrues interest on every day, so a cash total-return index has a value at every observation from its first value to its last one. v0.33 divided by the gap, so the index was `NaN` on each holiday of the market of one cash index, and so was every currency factor of that observation. v0.34 holds the last value of the cash index there, so the index keeps the exchange-rate move of that day, and the observation that ends the gap carries the accrual of the whole gap. On `fx = [2.0, 2.5, 3.0]`, a cash index `[1.0, 1.0, 1.0]` and a base cash index `[1.0, NaN, 1.5]`, v0.33 gives `[2.0, NaN, 2.0]` and v0.34 gives `[2.0, 2.5, 2.0]`. A gap of `fx`, and a gap before the first value or after the last value of a cash index, stay gaps.
- **A `PriceGapFill` under `CarriedPrice` keeps an asset with no observed training price.** v0.33 gave such an asset no entry in the `PriceGapFillResult`. So `apply_preprocessing` on a later window left every gap of the asset open, also after its first price in that window, where `partial_fit_transform` filled the gap. v0.34 keeps the asset in `nx` with a `missing` seed, so the fill reads the prices of the later window alone, as the online step does. `nx` under `CarriedPrice` now names every asset of the training window, and `fit_preprocessing` of a stepped `PriceGapFill` keeps the same assets. On a training window where `B` has no price, and a later window where `B` is `[5.0, NaN, 6.0, 7.0]`, v0.33 gives `[5.0, NaN, 6.0, 7.0]` and v0.34 gives `[5.0, 5.0, 6.0, 7.0]`. A fill with a statistic, such as `MedianValue()`, still gives the asset no entry.
- **A product of fields of the asset panel takes the promoted type of its fields.** A side of `EWVolumeRatio` that names a vector of fields reads their product. v0.33 multiplied in the type of the first field, so a `Float32` first field cut a `Float64` second field to `Float32`. v0.34 promotes the types, as the sum of fields does. With a `Float32` field times a `Float64` field near one, each entry of the product moves by up to `1e-7` relative, and an `EWVolumeRatio` over 30 observations moves by about `1.3e-8` relative. A product of fields of one type does not move.
- **A walk-forward with a `BudgetCosts` or a `BudgetMarketImpact` in `bgt` charges each fold the cost of its own trade.** v0.33 did not pass the previous weights to `bgt`, so every fold charged the cost of the move from the caller's reference `w`, and not from the book that the fold trades from. v0.34 replaces the reference with the previous weights, as a `Turnover` does, and the folds run in order. On the returns `0.01 .* randn(StableRNG(7), 200, 5) .+ 0.0005`, with `BudgetCosts(; bgt = 1.0, w = fill(0.2, 5), vp = 0.05, vn = 0.05, up = 0.5, un = 0.5)` and seven folds of `IndexWalkForward(60, 20)` under `MaximumReturn`, v0.33 ends at `[0.243, 0, 0.7, 0, 0]` and v0.34 ends at `[0.498, 0, 0.454, 0, 0]`. Write `fixed = true` on the estimator to keep the v0.33 result.
- **A `BudgetCosts` or a `BudgetMarketImpact` budget is a ceiling, and the floor of a `BudgetRange` in its `bgt` bounds the invested weights alone.** v0.33 wrote `sum(w) + cost == bgt` on a split of the trade into a buy and a sale, and nothing kept one of the two at zero. So a programme that gained from cash bought and sold one asset together, and held the false charge as cash. v0.34 writes `sum(w) + cost <= bgt`, and `sum(w) >= lb` for a floor. The unused budget is cash. With every mean negative, `MaximumReturn` gave `[0.15, 0.25, 0.25, 0.197, 0.15]` and gives `fill(0.15, 5)`. `MinimumRisk` under `bgt = 1.0` gave `sum(w) = 0.9514`, and it now sells to cash. Bound the cash with `bgt = BudgetRange(; lb = 0.95, ub = 1.0)`, or pin `sum(w) + cost` with `xbgt = true` on a `BudgetCosts`, under a mixed-integer solver.
- **A `Frontier` sweep of `MeanRisk` with a Custom Objective Term counts the term once.** v0.33 added the term to the objective penalty each time the model got an objective, and the penalty kept every copy. A return or a risk frontier sets the objective of the minimum-risk end point, of one maximum-return end point for each swept term, and of the sweep. So the end points solved with the term counted once and twice, and each point of a one-term sweep with the term counted three times. Now each solve counts the term once, so the points and the span of the sweep move. A sweep with no `cobj`, and a solve with no `Frontier`, do not move.
- **A fallback leader of `FollowTheLeader` solves on the allocation set of the head.** The set enters the entry of each member of the fallback chain of a JuMP leader. v0.33 gave the set to the held optimiser alone, so a fallback leader solved without it. The re-solve also sets the net budget of the held optimiser, and of each fallback, to the budget of one. So a fallback with `bgt = nothing` holds weights that sum to one. A solver-free fallback of a `BestConstantRebalancedPortfolio` takes the bounds of a `BoundedAllocationSet`, as the held estimator does.
- **A semidefinite phylogeny of a `ProgrammeAllocationSet` binds in the re-solve of `FollowTheLeader`.** v0.33 gave a `Variance` of the leader the second-order cone, because the variance read the phylogeny of the block of the leader alone. The rows of the set then sat on a lift that no term held down and that took no penalty, so the weights did not move with the set's `p`. v0.34 marks the weights in phase 1 of each block that holds a semidefinite phylogeny, so every `Variance` on them takes the semidefinite formulation. A variance that the objective minimises then holds down the lift that the rows bind, as in a batch `MeanRisk` with the same phylogeny in its own `ple`. On 6 assets and 120 rows with two linked pairs and `p = 0.05`, the largest `w_i w_j` of a linked pair is `0.01997` in v0.33 and `1.6e-7` in v0.34, where the batch `MeanRisk` gives `1.9e-7`. A leader with no variance does not move.
- **A `Variance` ceiling of a `ProgrammeAllocationSet` in the re-solve of `FollowTheLeader` takes the semidefinite formulation when the leader holds a semidefinite phylogeny in its own `ple`.** The mark of the previous item reaches every variance on the weights, so the ceiling bounds the lift that the rows of that phylogeny bind. v0.33 gave the ceiling the second-order cone.
- **A `TimeDependent` schedule in a field of the leader of an online rule resolves at each fold.** v0.33 resolved the schedules in the fields of the head alone. Each re-solve of the leader then reset a schedule in it to its fold-less value, the `default` or the static default of the field, so its entries never ran, and no message said so. v0.34 resolves each schedule of the rule tree at the fold, also in a leader among the experts of an `ExpertMixture` and under a `FollowTheLeadingHistory`. A schedule that reads `ctx.w_prev`, such as a `PreviousWeightsFunction`, gets the weights of the previous fold, and a walk-forward then runs its folds in order. On 10 rows of three assets and five folds of `OnlineIndexWalkForward(5, 1)` or `IndexWalkForward(5, 1; expand_train = true)`, a `MeanRisk` leader with `wb = TimeDependent(fill(WeightBounds(; lb = 0.0, ub = 0.4), 5))` had a largest weight of `1.0` at each fold in v0.33, and has `0.4` in v0.34.
- **The `cobj` of a `ProgrammeAllocationSet` enters the re-solve of a `FollowTheLeader` leader.** v0.33 dropped it with no message, so a custom objective term on the set had no effect on the leader. v0.34 adds it once to the objective of the leader, as the same term on the held optimiser. On 40 rows of three assets, a term that costs 10 times the weight of the first asset gave `[1, 0, 0]` on the set in v0.33, and gives `[0, 1, 0]` in v0.34, the weights of the term on the held optimiser.
- **`plot_weight_stability` boxes the rows of `prediction_weights`.** v0.33 stacked the weights of each fold on the universe of the caller and labelled the boxes with the names of the first fold. A fold whose universe differs from the first fold now boxes each weight under its own name, as the composition plots do, and a failed fold is absent from every box.
- **A search over a `MultipleRandomised` scheme with no `seed` draws its folds from `rng` itself.** v0.33 drew one seed from `rng` and split the data for each candidate with that seed. v0.34 splits the data once and passes the folds to each candidate. So at the same `rng`, the search scores other folds than v0.33, and `rng` ends in another state. Every candidate still scores the same folds. A scheme with a `seed` gives the folds of v0.33.
- **`plot_portfolio_cumulative_returns` of a population draws one curve for each member.** v0.33 drew an empty figure. Each curve takes the label of its member, the key of the path that `portfolio_report` uses, and the keywords of the call, as the curve of a path does.
- **A `TimeDependent` schedule inside an `Online(pipe)` step of a `Pipeline` resolves at each fold.** v0.33 read such a step as a step with no schedule, so each fold of an online scheme ran the `default` of the schedule, and a schedule with too few entries ran with no message. v0.34 reads the step as the pipeline that it wraps, so the step gives the answer of `Online(pipe)` run alone, and a schedule whose count of entries is not the count of folds raises a `DimensionMismatch`. On 150 rows of four assets with `TimeDependent([EqualWeighted(), InverseVolatility(), EqualWeighted()]; default = EqualWeighted())` and `OnlineIndexWalkForward(60, 30)`, fold 2 gave the equal weights in v0.33, and gives the inverse volatility weights in v0.34.
- **`EWMean`, `EWVolumeRatio` and `DaysToCover` divide their state by the sum of its weights.** The state of the recursion starts at zero, so after `n` valid observations of an asset it is `1 - decay^n` times a weighted mean. v0.33 gave that state as it is. So every value of an asset with a short history, such as an asset listed late, was shrunk toward zero, and its days to cover was too large by the inverse. v0.34 divides by `1 - decay^n`, as `EWVolatility` already did. This holds for the presets `EWMomentum`, `EWShareTurnover` and `EWAmihudIlliquidity` too. On a constant share turnover of `0.01` and the default half-life of `21`, the first value of an asset listed late was `0.005` in v0.33, and is `0.01` in v0.34. Its first days to cover halves. The values converge to the v0.33 values as the count `n` grows.
  `debias = nothing` on `EWMean` or `EWMomentum` keeps the state undivided, as v0.33 did. On a panel that the restart of the next row does not move, it gives the v0.33 values bit for bit. For a volume ratio, multiply by `1 - decay^n`, and divide a days to cover by it.
- **An inactive observation restarts `EWMean`, `EWVolumeRatio` and `DaysToCover`.** v0.33 wrote `NaN` at an inactive cell and kept the state and the count of the asset. So an asset that delisted and listed again carried its history from before the delisting, and a finite value at an inactive cell advanced the state. v0.34 sets the state and the count to zero at an inactive cell, as `EWVolatility` and the beta Descriptors do, so the warm-up runs again after a relisting. With a `skip`, the recursion after the restart reads no return of an observation at or before the inactive one. This holds for the presets `EWMomentum`, `EWShareTurnover` and `EWAmihudIlliquidity` too. A panel whose assets stay active after their first listing, and whose inactive cells hold no finite value, keeps its numbers. No keyword gives the v0.33 values back.
- **The degrees of freedom of an idiosyncratic variance of a `CrossSectionalFactorPrior` read the active mask.** The variance restarts an asset where it turns inactive, but v0.33 counted every finite residual of the asset. So an asset that listed again kept the count of its rows before the delisting, and a delisted asset, whose variance is `NaN`, had the full count. On a synthetic panel of 80 assets and 420 observations, with 18 to 28 delisted assets by the draw, each delisted asset had about 113 degrees of freedom under the market factor alone, and about 96 with an industry factor and a size factor too. Each one now has `0`. The count of an exponentially weighted variance now reads the rows after the last inactive row of each asset. A variance estimator of your own reads the mask in its own method, see [If you extend the library](@ref migration-0-34-extending). A bound that `ResidualInflation` sets moves only on a panel with a delisting or a relisting.
- **The exponentially weighted beta Descriptors regress the observations of each asset alone.** v0.33 advanced the market mean and the market variance at every observation, and the covariance of an asset only at its own valid observations, from a mean that started at zero. So an asset that is listed late, or that misses observations, divided a covariance of its own observations by a variance of all of them, and its beta was biased toward zero.
  v0.34 keeps one set of weighted moments per asset, of the market, of the reference series of an `EWMacroSensitivity` and of the asset, over the valid observations of the asset, so the beta is the slope of a weighted regression with an intercept on those observations. On the parity panel of 80 observations and 12 assets, the asset listed at observation 21 has an `EWMarketBeta(; half_life = 8)` of `1.24` at observation 29, where v0.33 gave `0.906`. An asset listed on the whole panel moves too, because v0.33 started its means at zero, and that start keeps the weight `decay^n` after `n` observations. On the same panel, such an asset moves by up to `0.29` at the first value of `EWMarketBeta(; half_life = 8)`, at observation 8, and by up to `0.0028` at observation 40. With the default `EWMarketBeta()`, it moves by `0.007` to `0.032` over observations 60 to 80. The same rules give five more changes:
  - **An inactive observation resets the asset** in `EWBeta`, `EWMacroSensitivity` and `EWDownsideBeta`, as it did in the residual volatility. A relisted asset starts a new history, and its beta is `NaN` until it has the observations of a new warm-up: `max(2, min_obs)` for a market beta, `max(3, min_obs)` for the partial beta of an `EWMacroSensitivity`, and `min_obs` for an `EWDownsideBeta`. v0.33 resumed the state that it left.
  - **A window of `agg_obs > 1` averages the series of an asset over the same observations**, those where the asset is active and every series is finite. An inactive observation clears the open window of its asset.
  - **A beta is `NaN` where it has no denominator.** That is where the market does not vary over the observations of the asset, where the market falls short of the target of an `EWDownsideBeta` on none of them (v0.33 gave `0`), and where the market and the reference series of an `EWMacroSensitivity` are almost perfectly correlated. The partial beta needs three observations, or two where the market is constant, which the intercept then absorbs.
  - **The shrinkage of an `EWBeta` with a `group` weighs each beta against the sampling variance of the weighted least-squares slope**, which needs three observations. v0.33 divided the residual variance by twice the half-life. A beta with no finite variance turns `NaN` where its group shrinks it, and a group whose prior variance is zero keeps the lower bound of the weight.
  - **`EWResidualVolatility` forms a residual from the `beta_min_obs`-th observation of the beta**, and its warm-up counts the residuals. With the defaults and no missing return, the first value is at observation `69`, where v0.33 gave one at observation `60`.
- **The JuMP model of `MeanAbsoluteDeviation` is exact for every target of the deviations.** v0.33 registered the one-sided row `mad_t >= tgt - x_t` and reported `2 mean(mad)`, which is the mean absolute deviation only when the target is the mean of the net series. Under `mu = 0.0` the v0.33 model held `0.003182`, where the functor gives `0.003990`. v0.34 adds `mean(net_X) - tgt`, so the model and the functor differ by `2.6e-10` relative at most under every target. A portfolio with MAD and a target other than the mean moves. So does a portfolio whose prior mean is not the sample mean, such as a James-Stein mean, under the default target.
- **The entropic and the relativistic measures, the power-norm measures and the even moment model read no observation of zero weight.** The programmes of `ERM`, `RRM` and `PRM`, the JuMP models of `EntropicValueatRisk`, `RelativisticValueatRisk`, `PowerNormValueatRisk` and their drawdown and range forms, and the JuMP model of `LowOrderMoment(; alg = EvenMoment())` held one cone row for each observation, also for an observation of weight zero. Such a row holds no probability, but the row of a large loss stopped the solver far from the optimum, and the solver reported success. v0.34 builds the rows on the observations of positive weight. With a crash of `-0.3` on a zero-weight row of 150, the EVaR weights equal the weights without the row. At `p = 3` the v0.33 even moment model held `3.8389e-5`, where the functor gives `3.7756e-5`, and its weights moved by `6.5e-4`. With no zero weight, each model is the programme of v0.33.
- **`PRM` returns the largest loss with no solver where ``\alpha \pi^{-1/p} \le 1``.** The measure is then the largest loss. v0.33 solved the programme there, at the boundary of the cone, where a solver can stall and give `NaN`.
- **A `ub` on the gain tail of a `GenericValueatRiskRange` with a `DistributionValueatRisk` tail binds.** The v0.33 model bounded the negated value of the tail, so the bound did not bind the portfolio. v0.34 bounds `dot(mu, w) + z * g`, the value that the functor gives on `-w`. Under `MaximumReturn`, the gain tail is `0.017057` with no bound, and with `ub = 0.0085285` on the gain tail it is `0.0085285` at the optimum. A portfolio with such a bound moves.
- **`WorstRealisation` beside `Range` keeps its bound, and a second instance of a measure that reads no index warns.** `MaximumDrawdown`, `WorstRealisation`, `NoRisk`, `Range` and `BrownianDistanceVariance` register one risk expression in a model. In v0.33 a second instance of one of these types lost its bound and its scale with no message. In `[Range(), WorstRealisation(; settings = RiskMeasureSettings(; ub))]`, the `WorstRealisation` added no bound and no risk term, because both read one key. v0.34 builds the bound of `WorstRealisation` there, and its value at the optimum equals `ub`. A second instance of one type still has no effect, and it logs a warning that names its type and its settings.
- **`owa_rg(1)` is `[0.0]`.** v0.33 wrote `-1` and then `1` into the one entry, so it returned `[1.0]`, and the ordered weights range of one observation was that observation. `OWA_RG()` of one observation now gives `0.0`, as `Range` does. For `T >= 2` the weights do not change.
- **Three fits of the regime-adjusted moments move.** `LogRegimeAdjusted` with an `x` other than `1/2` scales the volatility by `exp(s / 2)`, where v0.33 took `exp(x s)`, which is correct only at `x = 1/2`. The raw statistic of `DiagonalTarget` for the first moment, `debias = nothing`, which v0.33 spelled `RawStatistic()`, divides by the mean of a ``\chi(n)`` law, ``\sqrt{2} \Gamma((n + 1) / 2) / \Gamma(n / 2)``. v0.33 divided by ``\sqrt{n}``, so at `n = 6` the raw statistic moves by the factor `1.08655` before the clip. Under the default `ExactDebias()`, the same fit moves by round-off alone, `7.4e-16` relative at most. A row with no valid asset counts as one lag of the HAC variance, where v0.33 skipped it, so the returns on the two sides of a holiday no longer read as adjacent. At `hac_lags = 2`, the variance of the first moment on a panel with a holiday moves by `0.14 %` at the median and by `0.47 %` at most.
- **`AngularDist`, and so the default `FeatureDistance`, keeps a `Float32` or a `BigFloat` input.** v0.33 promoted the type with `Float64`, so a `Float32` feature matrix gave a `Float64` distance matrix under every collapse. An `Int` or a `Rational` input still gives `Float64`.
- **`matrix_square_root(RidgeCholeskySquareRoot(), M)` keeps the type of a `Float32` matrix that needs a ridge.** v0.33 read the ridge as a `Float64`, so such a matrix got a `Float64` factor, where its plain factor is `Float32`.
- **`vcat` of two asset panels whose equal static fields have different element types promotes the type.** v0.33 took the element type of the first part, so a `Float32` field and an equal `Float64` field gave a `Float32` field. The field stays lazy.
- **A `DateWalkForward` with a negative `period_offset` keeps its last anchor.** v0.33 added the offset to the whole range of anchors. Under a calendar step the shifted range restarted its step from the shifted start, so it dropped an anchor in the last `abs(period_offset)` of the data, and the fold of that anchor. v0.34 shifts each date. On daily data from 2021-01-01 to 2021-02-25, with `period = Month(1)` and `period_offset = Day(-10)`, the anchor 2021-02-19 now opens a second test fold under `reduce_test = true`. The test windows are `[22:49, 50:56]`, where v0.33 gave `[22:56]`. A fixed step and a fixed offset, such as `Day` or `Week`, keep a `StepRange`.
- **`OptimalNumberClusters(; alg = SecondOrderDifference())` selects one cluster on two assets.** The ceiling of the count is `floor(Int, sqrt(2)) = 1`, and v0.33 selected `k = 2` on the hierarchical and on the non-hierarchical branch. v0.34 returns the ceiling, as `SilhouetteScore` and a stated `k` do.
- **`BayesianBlackLittermanPrior` forms its posterior mean before `mp` processes the covariance.** v0.33 formed the mean from the processed covariance. So a processing step that changes the covariance, such as `MatrixProcessing(; dn = Denoise())`, moved the mean, and the equation `mu == rr.M * fpr.mu + rr.b + rf` of its docstring failed by `0.0060`. The default `MatrixProcessing()` leaves a positive definite covariance unchanged, so a default fit does not move.
- **`OWAJuMP(; alg = MaximumEntropy())` maximises the entropy of the L-moment coefficients.** v0.33 maximised the entropy of an auxiliary vector that every feasible point let take the value `1/T`, so the objective did not depend on the weights, and the weights were where the solver stopped. So the two encodings, `RelativeEntropy()` and `ExponentialConeEntropy()`, gave weights `6.1 %` apart. v0.34 has one maximiser, and the weights of the two encodings differ by `2.7e-6` at most. The OWA weights of `MaximumEntropy` move. The default `so` resolves to `1e-3` under `MaximumEntropy`. At `so = 1`, Clarabel stopped short on 11 of 15 programmes of the new objective, and each of them fell back to `ncrra_weights`.
- **The radius of a distributionally robust measure reads the count of the rows that the measure holds.** This holds for `DistributionallyRobustConditionalValueatRisk`, `DistributionallyRobustConditionalValueatRiskRange` and `DistributionallyRobustConditionalDrawdownatRisk`. v0.33 read the count of the observations behind the moments. Under a cap on the scenarios, `EmpiricalPrior(; max_scenarios)`, the rows are fewer, so the ball was too small. With `max_scenarios = 250` over 1250 observations, the radius of `ConcentrationRadius` and of `DualNormRadius` grows by `sqrt(5)`, and the radius of `DimensionalRateRadius` by `5^(1/N)` on `N` assets. The new field `n` of `CalibrationContext` carries the count, see [If you extend the library](@ref migration-0-34-extending). With no cap and no observation weights, the radius does not move.
- **`RateRadius` and `RateSignificance` read the count of the rows that the measure prices, and the observation weights.** v0.33 read the count of the observations behind the moments, and no observation weights. `RateRadius` now reads the count of its site, as the three rules of the row above do. `RateSignificance` reads the count that `CountOf` reads. With no cap on the scenarios and no observation weights, neither rule moves. Three cases move:
  - **A distributionally robust measure with `RateRadius` under a cap.** With `max_scenarios = 250` over 1250 observations, `RateRadius()` gives the radius `1 / sqrt(250) = 0.0632456`, where v0.33 gave `1 / sqrt(1250) = 0.0282843`. So the ball grows by `sqrt(5)`, and it equals the ball over the same 250 rows with no cap. `RateRadius` in a regularisation slot, `l1` or `linf`, under the cap does not move.
  - **A tail measure with `RateSignificance` under a cap.** Its level grows by `sqrt(5)` in the same way, from `c / sqrt(1250)` to `c / sqrt(250)`.
  - **Observation weights.** A robust measure or a regularisation slot with `RateRadius`, and a tail measure with `RateSignificance`, read the Kish count of the weights, where v0.33 read the count of the rows. On 250 rows with weights `0.99 .^ (249:-1:0)`, whose Kish count is `169.158`, both rules give `1 / sqrt(169.158) = 0.0768872`, where v0.33 gave `1 / sqrt(250)`.
- **A `MedianAbsoluteDeviation` with observation weights takes the weighted median of the absolute deviations.** v0.33 gave the deviations to `StatsBase.mad`, which reads no weights, so the weights moved the centre alone. A measure with no weights does not move.
- **`bigger_is_better` of a `NonOptimisationRiskRatio` follows its numerator, and `bigger_is_better(ThirdCentralMoment())` is `true`.** v0.33 gave `false` for each. So a search or a selection rule that scored on a ratio with a return numerator, such as `NonOptimisationRiskRatio(; r1 = MeanReturn(), r2 = ConditionalValueatRisk())`, or on the third central moment, put the worst candidate first. A ratio with a risk numerator still gives `false`.
- **`risk_contribution` divides each term by its own degree of homogeneity.** v0.33 read a ratio as a measure of degree one, and `VarianceSkewKurtosis` as one measure, so the contributions of a ratio of another degree and of `VarianceSkewKurtosis` did not sum to the risk. In v0.34 the contributions of a homogeneous measure sum to its risk. `EqualRisk` and a measure of degree zero give zero contributions.
- **`risk_contribution` gives the plain marginal contributions `w .* g` of a measure that is not homogeneous.** `g` is the gradient of the risk. v0.33 read the degree from the type of the measure alone, and divided by it. A fixed target other than zero, or a fixed fee, makes a measure not homogeneous, so no degree applies. These cases move:
  - A measure of degree 2, 3 or 4 with a fixed target other than zero: a `LowOrderMoment`, a `HighOrderMoment` or a `ThirdCentralMoment` with a number target or a `VecScalar` target with an offset, and a `TrackingRiskMeasure` with `SquaredL2Norm` against a benchmark other than zero. v0.33 divided by the degree.
  - A measure of degree other than one that reads the net returns, under a one-off fixed fee, which is `fl`, `fs` or `flq` without `AmortisedFees`, or under a periodic fee that does not cancel from its risk: a turnover against weights other than zero, `lq`, or an amortised fixed fee.
  - A ratio whose side is one of these measures.

  The contributions of these measures do not sum to the risk. A `RiskTrackingRiskMeasure` against benchmark weights of zero is its inner measure, so it divides by the degree of that measure, where v0.33 divided by one. Its contributions then sum to its risk.
- **`risk_contribution` and `risk_gradient` of a measure whose value is a solve read its worst-case distribution.** These are `EntropicValueatRisk`, `RelativisticValueatRisk` and `PowerNormValueatRisk`, their drawdown forms, their relative drawdown forms and their ranges, twelve measures in all, and every vector, ratio, tracking measure and `GenericValueatRiskRange` that holds one. v0.33 took a finite difference of one solve for each asset, and the gap of each solve was as large as the signal. So the contributions missed the risk: on the data of the review, `sum(rc) / risk` was `0.999957` for EVaR and `1.000159` for RLVaR. v0.34 solves once and differentiates the expected loss under the worst-case distribution. The contributions sum to the risk within `1e-6`, and `risk_gradient` equals a central difference within `2.4e-5`.
- **`ApproxOrderedWeightsArray` builds no p-norm cone for a weight vector with at most one entry strictly between its smallest and its largest value.** `owa_cvar` and `owa_wr` are such vectors, and so are both tails of `OWA_CVaR_RG`. v0.33 built the cones of every order in `p` for each vector. Such an optimum sits at the apex of every cone, and the default Clarabel stopped at `SLOW_PROGRESS`: the minimum-risk range of two CVaR tails gave `0.017781`, where the exact optimum is `0.017823`. v0.34 solves an exact linear programme for such a vector, and its value equals the exact optimum within `2.9e-8`. A vector with more inner entries, such as `owa_gmd` or `owa_tg`, keeps its cones and does not move.
- **`EqualRisk` keeps the element type of the weights.** v0.33 returned `inv(length(w))`, a `Float64`, for `Float32` weights.
- **The barrier of `NearOptimalCentering` reads both weight bounds.** v0.33 wrote `-log(w_i)` for each asset, so a lower bound other than zero added the hidden row `w_i > 0`, and the centre was not the analytic centre of the bounds. v0.34 writes `-log(w_i - lb_i)`. A portfolio with a lower bound other than zero moves. The default bounds `lb = 0` and `ub = 1` do not move.
- **`LogRiskBudgeting` with an orthant vector `z` drops the row `sum(z .* w) - k >= 0`.** No source states the row. It does not bind at the default budget of one, so only a portfolio whose gross exposure passes one moves.
- **A sweep on two terms of `VarianceSkewKurtosis` gives one portfolio for each pair of levels.** v0.33 swept the first `Frontier` and dropped the second with no message. A `Frontier(; N = 3)` on `vr` and on `kt` now gives nine portfolios, and the cap `max_frontier` counts the product.
- **Models (B) and (C) of `RelaxedRiskBudgeting` hold the factor `n` of their source, and `p` of Model (C) is its `λ`.** `RegularisedRelaxedRiskBudgeting` writes `ρ² >= n w'Σw`, where v0.33 wrote `ρ² >= w'Σw`, and `RegularisedPenalisedRelaxedRiskBudgeting` writes `ρ² >= n p z'Θz`, where v0.33 wrote `ρ² >= p z'Θz`. `n` is the count of the entries of the risk budget, and `Θ` is the diagonal of the covariance of `z`. Write `p / n` to keep the Model (C) of v0.33. No field keeps the Model (B) of v0.33. With a target return 1.2 and 1.5 times the return of the risk parity portfolio, Model (B) moves by `0.044` and `0.031`, and Model (C) at `p = 5` by `0.045` and `0.112`. `BasicRelaxedRiskBudgeting` does not move.
- **`RiskBudgeting` puts a floor on its scale variable `k`.** v0.33 left `k` free. The new field `kmin` defaults to `nothing`, which gives the floor `1e-4 / b`, with `b` the absolute upper end of the net budget, and zero for a budget of zero or an infinite budget. A negative `kmin` raises a `DomainError`. Where the floor does not bind, the solve moves within its precision: the pinned solves of the test suite moved by a median of `5e-5` to `1.6e-4`, and by `1.8e-2` at most.
- **A `Frontier` sweep under `MaximumRatio` solves its two end points on a model with `k = 1`.** v0.33 solved them on the ratio model, so at `rf = 0` every point of the sweep was the tangency portfolio, and at `rf > 0` the end-point solve failed. v0.34 builds a second model for the end points, and the sweep spans the range from the minimum-risk portfolio to the maximum-return portfolio. A level above the risk of the tangency portfolio still gives the tangency portfolio.
- **`RandomisedSearchCrossValidation` draws `n_iter` joint trials.** v0.33 drew `n_iter` values of each key and searched the grid of their product, so three keys gave up to `n_iter^3` candidates. v0.34 draws `n_iter` points of the joint grid. Over grids of vectors, it draws `min(n_iter, |Λ|)` distinct points, so on three keys of four values, `n_iter` of 3, 10, 64 and 200 gives 3, 10, 64 and 64 trials. With a distribution, each trial draws one value of each key, and a multivariate distribution gives one vector for each trial. The same `rng` gives the same trials. An `n_iter` at or above the size of a grid of vectors gives the grid search.
- **The functor of `Skewness` is the raw third moment of its coskewness matrix.** `expected_risk(Skewness(), w, pr)` gives `w' M3 (w ⊗ w)` of `pr.sk`, the value that a bound on the measure holds. v0.33 gave the standardised skewness of the returns of the portfolio. On 250 observations of 5 assets with a skewed asset, over a high order prior, v0.33 gave `0.0227` and v0.34 gives `2.92e-9`. Every reader of the functor moves, such as the span of a sweep on a skewness floor. Write `ThirdCentralMoment(; ve = SimpleVariance())` for the v0.33 value. The functor of `Kurtosis` reads `kt`, which each JuMP model of the measure read in v0.33 too, so the models do not move. Over a high order prior, its readers move by rounding alone: `expected_risk` by `1.0e-13`, `HierarchicalRiskParity` and `HierarchicalEqualRiskContribution` by `3.5e-14` at most, and `risk_contribution` by `6.4e-10`.
- **A weighted moment reads its effective count.** v0.33 read the count of the rows of `X` at each site that prices the sampling error of a weighted moment. v0.34 reads the Kish count `(sum(w))^2 / sum(w .^ 2)` of analytic or probability weights, `sum(f)` of frequency weights, and the count of the rows of a window. With no weights and with unit weights, 69 of 70 values do not move. The one that moves is a rolling window under a denoiser. These results move:
  - **A denoiser** over a weighted `Covariance`, `GeneralCovariance` or `DistanceCovariance`, over either exponentially weighted covariance, over a rolling or a seed `WindowedCovariance`, and the denoiser of a weighted `Cokurtosis` or `Coskewness`. A denoiser keeps the same factors at both counts when no eigenvalue lies between the two edges of the band of noise, so the matrix moves only when one eigenvalue does. On 90 observations, `WindowedCovariance(; window = 40)` under a denoiser moves by `1.79` relative, because the denoiser reads the 40 rows of the window.
  - **A prior with a weighted `me` or `ce`.** Its normal uncertainty sets, its calibration rules, the default `tau` of Black-Litterman and the denoisers of its assembled matrices read the Kish count. The normal sets of an entropy pooling or an opinion pooling prior read the Kish count of `w` in place of its perplexity, so they are wider.
  - **A `ShrunkExpectedReturns` over a weighted `me`.** `JamesStein`, `BayesStein` and `BodnarOkhrinParolya` price the error of the sample mean at the effective count of `me`. On the reference data of the test suite, three shrunk means over `SimpleExpectedReturns` with observation weights move by up to `6.8 %`, `10.6 %` and `62 %`. An unweighted mean keeps `T = size(X, 1)`, and its result does not move.
  - **A prior over a `StandardDeviationExpectedReturns` or a `VarianceExpectedReturns` with a weighted `ce`.** The mean states the count of the covariance of `ce`. So its normal mean set, its Black-Litterman `tau` and its calibration rules read a Kish count, such as `95.6`, where v0.33 read 200 rows.
  - **An `EmpiricalPrior` over a `PriorExpectedReturns` whose inner prior weights its mean or pools.** The outer mean states the count of the inner prior. So the normal uncertainty sets, the calibration rules and the default Black-Litterman `tau` of the outer prior read that count, where v0.33 read the rows. Under an inner mean with the weights `0.98 .^ (200:-1:1)` on 200 rows, the count is `95.58`. The moments do not move.
- **The bare `ImpliedVolatility()` does not repair a matrix that is not positive definite.** The v0.33 default held `mp = MatrixProcessing()`, which holds a `Posdef` repair. On 30 observations of 50 assets, the smallest eigenvalue was `1.7e-16` in v0.33 and is `-2.3e-19` in v0.34, and the entries move by `8.9e-17` at most. On 200 observations of 10 assets the matrix does not move. `PortfolioOptimisersCovariance(; ce = ImpliedVolatility(; …))` gives the v0.33 matrix, also as the `ce` of another estimator, such as `ClustersEstimator(; ce = PortfolioOptimisersCovariance(; ce = ImpliedVolatility(; ppy = 252)))`.
- **A table of prices with no gap and no field of the asset panel gives no asset panel.** `prices_to_returns` of such a table gives `pnl = nothing`, where v0.33 gave a panel whose masks were all true. A panel that holds its masks alone holds `pf = nothing`, where v0.33 held an empty vector. A table with a gap keeps a panel of its masks, and a table with a field of the asset panel keeps its panel. Code that reads `rd.pnl` of such data now reads `nothing`.
- **`CountOf` in a significance slot and `EntropyBudget` read the rows of the measure under a cap on the scenarios.** Under `EmpiricalPrior(; max_scenarios)` with no observation weights, the level of `CountOf(n)` is `n` over the count of the rows that the measure holds, and `EntropyBudget` reads the same count. v0.33 read the effective sample size of the observations behind the moments. In the test of the fix, v0.33 gave the level `0.02` where the rows give `0.1`, and an `EntropyBudget` paid `-2.716` on a budget of `-5`. With no cap, the level does not move.
- **The separate path of `RegimeAdjustedExpWeightedCovariance` with HAC lags scores an asset only on a built block.** With a `cor_decay` and `hac_lags > 0`, v0.33 wrote the block of the correlation only when every active asset had a diagonal. So a late listing kept the block of every other asset at the row before, and the next row scored those assets against it. v0.34 writes a zero row for an asset with no diagonal, and the other assets take their row. A covariance with one decay does not move.
- **The feature matrix of a categorical field of the asset panel with an observed mask holds one mask column.** The column is `"<name>::observed"`, where v0.33 gave one column for each level, such as `"sector=T::observed"`. The mask of a tensor with one label is `"<name>=<label>::observed"`.
- **`OWA_RG`, `OWA_CVaR_RG` and `OWA_TG_RG` return an `OrderedWeightsArrayRange`, and their optimum under `ApproxOrderedWeightsArray` moves toward the exact optimum.** v0.33 returned an `OrderedWeightsArray`. The value of each functor and the minimum risk under `ExactOrderedWeightsArray` do not move. On 100 observations of 8 assets, the exact value of each measure at the approximate optimum was above the exact optimum by `0.31 %`, `1.03 %` and `0.44 %` in v0.33. In v0.34 it is above it by `0.00 %`, `0.00 %` and `0.06 %`.
- **The `edof` of a `CrossSectionalFactorPrior` on a rank-deficient period counts the rank of the design.** Each period spends the rank of its design, where v0.33 spent its count of columns. A market column beside a one-hot industry block is such a design. On the panel of the test suite, with five columns of rank four, the `edof` of most assets is `96.316`, where v0.33 gave `93.319`.
- **A bound on `BrownianDistanceVariance` under `MaximumRatio` binds.** v0.33 bounded the risk expression, which is of degree two in the weights. Under the scale variable of the ratio such a row does not hold the bound, so the tangency portfolio could break it. v0.34 bounds the square root of the expression, which is of degree one, as `Variance` does. The bound then holds under `MaximumRatio`, and a `Frontier` on the measure sweeps the square root.
- **A JuMP solve with a risk bound or a return bound can stop at another point within the tolerance of the solver.** v0.34 divides each bound row by the size of its bound, and each anchor cone of `NearOptimalCentering` by the width of its region. A positive scale does not change the feasible set, so the optimum does not move, but the solver meets the rows on another path. At the default tolerances of Clarabel, the wealth of a point of a `MeanRisk` frontier at its return bound moves by `1.3e-5` against v0.33, and by `5.8e-7` at tolerances of `1e-12`. Its performance summary and its regret move with it. A ceiling of `NearOptimalCentering` on a measure such as `Kurtosis` or `EntropicValueatRisk`, which the unscaled rows broke at the default tolerances, holds on the cases of the review.
  A centring model that v0.33 stopped at `SLOW_PROGRESS` can solve: on the last 253 rows of `SP500.csv.gz`, with `r = UncertaintySetVariance(; ucs)` of a box set and the default Clarabel settings, v0.33 returned an `OptimisationFailure` and v0.34 solves.
- **A logarithmic return on a weighted prior reads the observation weights of the prior.** This holds for `expected_return(LogarithmicReturn(), w, pr)`, for `expected_risk` of an `ExpectedReturn` or an `ExpectedReturnRiskRatio` over a `LogarithmicReturn`, and so for each ratio and summary over a log return, on a prior with weights, such as an entropy pooling prior. v0.33 took the plain mean of `log1p.(X * w)`, where the JuMP model read the weights. On an entropy pooling prior with one view on a mean, `expected_return(LogarithmicReturn(), w, pr)` moves from `0.000556308` to `0.000982213`, and at the optimum of the model it equals the model within `1.9e-12` relative. A prior with no weights, an `ArithmeticReturn`, and `expected_risk` of a population of weight vectors do not move.
- **The warning of a JuMP optimiser that no solver finishes states each failed stage and its termination status.** v0.33 logged `Failed to solve optimisation problem.` and a pointer to `result.retcode.res`. v0.34 logs `Model could not be solved satisfactorily (n solver trial(s)).`, then one line for each failed stage of each solver, with the termination status in words, such as `INFEASIBLE: no solution satisfies every constraint`, and then the same pointer. Each failed trial in `res.retcode.res` holds `:termination_status`. The solve that builds a `JuMPResult` warned one line for each failed stage in v0.33 too, and it adds the line of the status. Each failed trial in its `trials` holds `:termination_status`. A test or a log filter that matches the v0.33 text must match the new text.
- **A `JuMPEntropyPooling` posterior puts no mass on a scenario of zero prior weight.** v0.33 wrote the relative entropy cone over every scenario. A zero prior weight forces a zero posterior weight only in exact arithmetic, so the solver gave such a scenario a small positive weight, and `kld` was `Inf`. On 60 scenarios with two of zero prior weight, the two posterior weights were `3.4e-10` and `8.3e-10` under a mean view, and `6.3e-10` and `3.4e-10` under a CVaR view inside its band. v0.34 fixes these weights at zero and writes the cone over the support alone, so `kld` is finite and equals the `kld` of `OptimEntropyPooling` to the tolerance of the solver. A prior that is positive on every scenario does not move. `LogEntropyPooling` now solves over such a prior, as [Calls that now answer](@ref migration-0-34-answers) states.
- **An `OptimEntropyPooling` fit gives the same posterior on each call.** The dual solve keeps the point of its last evaluation, and skips the update of the objective and the gradient when the next point is equal. v0.33 started that store as uninitialised memory. When a freed array of an earlier fit held the start point exactly, the first evaluation read a stale objective and gradient, so two equal `prior` calls in one process could give two posteriors. On four CVaR fits of `MeucciEntropyPoolingPrior`, each run 60 times after a loop that frees arrays, the v0.33 store gave 134 of 240 posteriors that differ from the fit that reads no stale store, by up to `1.5e-3`, and 2 of 240 fits raised `Entropy pooling optimisation failed`. v0.34 fills the store at the start point, and the same loop gives no difference and no error. A fit that did not read a stale store does not move.
- **`brinson_attribution` with one bound attributes the window of that bound.** v0.33 applied the window only when the call gave both `date0` and `date1`, and a single bound attributed the whole of `X` with no message. v0.34 applies each bound on its own. A call with `date0` alone runs from `date0` to the last observation, and a call with `date1` alone runs from the first observation to `date1`. Write `brinson_attribution(X, w, wb, asset_classes, col)` with no bound to keep the v0.33 number.
- **`expected_return` of an `ArithmeticReturn` with a fitted uncertainty set gives the worst-case return over the set.** v0.33 gave the nominal `dot(w, mu)`, where the model expression of the term, and each bound on it, is the worst case. So a return `Frontier` on such a term took its levels from the nominal return, and its last levels were above the largest worst case: on the last 253 rows of `SP500.csv.gz`, with an `L1UncertaintySet` of `CountOf(5)`, v0.33 swept `[0.013519, 0.030196]` and its last point was infeasible. v0.34 sweeps `[-0.001437, 0.022103]`, and every point solves.
  The value is the one that the functor of `UncertaintySetVariance` gives for a fitted set of the covariance. It reaches each ratio and each measure that reads the term, such as `expected_ratio` and `ExpectedReturn`, and the anchors of `NearOptimalCentering`. An optimiser fits an estimator in `ucs` once, before it builds the model, so the resolved terms in `res.jor.ret` hold the fitted set, and `expected_return(res.jor.ret, res.w, pr)` gives the worst case. An estimator in `ucs` outside an optimisation, and a term with no set, give the nominal return as in v0.33. To keep the v0.33 number, call `expected_return` on the same term with `ucs = nothing`.
- **`Accessors.set`, and a search, on a type whose constructor computes a field.** `LogRegimeAdjusted` computes `kappa` from `x` and `y`, `NormalEquationsFit` computes `coef` from its sums, and `CustomValueReturnForecastResult` and `TargetReturnForecastResult` set `hist` to `nothing`. v0.33 passed every field, the computed one too, to the positional constructor that `@concrete` writes, and that constructor skips the inner constructor. So the computed field kept its old value, and no check ran.
  `Accessors.@set la.x = 3.0` on `la = LogRegimeAdjusted()` kept `kappa = -1.2704`, where `LogRegimeAdjusted(; x = 3.0).kappa` is `1.6159`, and `x = -1.0` gave no error. `GridSearchCrossValidation` and `RandomisedSearchCrossValidation` write each candidate through `Accessors.set`, so a search over `x` or `y` fitted every candidate with the `kappa` of the start value. v0.34 calls the keyword constructor, which computes the field again and runs each check. A patch of the computed field raises an `ArgumentError` that names the fields to set.

### [If you extend the library](@id migration-0-34-extending)

- **Seven public types now subtype a root type, where v0.33 subtyped `Any`.** `AttributionComponent`, `AttributionBreakdown`, `AssetAttributionBreakdown`, `AssetFactorContribution` and `Resume` subtype `AbstractResult`. `CarriedPrice` and `PortfolioOptimisers.SubPortfolioUniverse` subtype `AbstractAlgorithm`. `PortfolioOptimisers.RangeTails`, which v0.33 did not have, subtypes `AbstractEstimator`. A method of your own on a root type now reaches them, and so do the library methods on the roots: the REPL shows each one field by field, and `length`, `iterate` and `getindex` treat it as a scalar. A method of your own that takes one of the seven and a root type in two positions can now be ambiguous.

- **A range of your own subtypes `PortfolioOptimisers.RangeRiskMeasure`, and the library builds it.** Every Range variant of the library subtypes `RangeRiskMeasure <: RiskMeasure`, a public type, where v0.33 subtyped `RiskMeasure`. A range of your own that decomposes into two point measures declares `PortfolioOptimisers.range_tails(r)` and `PortfolioOptimisers.range_risk_key(r)`, the key under which the model stores the expression of the sum. The method of `set_risk_constraints!` for a `RangeRiskMeasure` then builds both tails and registers their sum, so the range needs no builder of its own. v0.33 gave a range of your own no builder. `range_tails` of a measure that is not a range raises a `MethodError`, where v0.33 raised an `ArgumentError`. A range that fuses its tails still raises the `ArgumentError`.

- **A fill policy or a panel field input of your own takes the active mask in its own method.** `asset_panel` calls `panel_fill(alg, v, act, name)`, `panel_placeholder(alg, v, act)` and `panel_resolve(inp, amsk)`, as in v0.33. v0.33 also reached a policy that defined only `panel_fill(alg, v, name)` or `panel_placeholder(alg, v)`, and an input that defined only `panel_resolve(inp)`, through fallbacks that dropped the mask. These fallbacks are gone. Write `panel_fill(alg::MyFill, v, ::Option{<:AbstractVector{Bool}}, name)`, `panel_placeholder(alg::MyFill, v, ::Option{<:AbstractVector{Bool}})` and `panel_resolve(inp::MyInput, ::Option{<:AbstractMatrix{Bool}} = nothing)` to ignore the mask, or read it. A policy or an input with the v0.33 methods alone raises a `MethodError` in `asset_panel`. The library policies take `act = nothing` for a column with no mask, so `panel_fill(ForwardPanelFill(), v, nothing, name)` gives what `panel_fill(ForwardPanelFill(), v, name)` gave.

- **A method of yours that reads an array of rank three reads the observations last.** A method of `cross_sectional_regression` for an estimator of your own, of `unseen_member_design` for a rule of your own for an unseen member, of `factor_exposure` for an observed exposure of several factors, or of `attribution_leverage_split` for a rule of your own reads `Z[:, :, t]` where v0.33 read `Z[t, :, :]`, and gives an array of rank three with the observations last. See [Arrays of rank three hold their observations last](@ref migration-0-34-layout).
- **A rule of your own that reads the mean out of a recursion from zero** subtypes `AbstractZeroStartDebias`, which is public, and adds a method of `zero_start_mean(rule, s, decay, n)`, which is public too. `ExpWeightedExpectedReturns`, `EWMean` and `EWMomentum` take it in their `debias` field, and so do the three gradient transforms `GradientMomentum`, `RootMeanSquareGradient` and `AdaptiveMomentGradient`.
- **A gradient transform of your own returns its state with the transformed gradient.** `transform_gradient!(grad, gs, g)` returns `(gs, ĝ)`, the state of the transform after the period and the transformed gradient, where v0.33 returned `ĝ` alone and the rule kept the state that it passed in. Return the state that you wrote in place, as `return gs, ĝ`. A transform with the v0.33 return raises a `TypeError` at its first step. A transform with no method of `gradient_state_seed` keeps the state `nothing`, where v0.33 raised a `MethodError`. The three momentum transforms of the library keep a `PortfolioOptimisers.GradientAverageState`, which is public, with the count of the steps since the seed, where v0.33 kept a vector or a pair of vectors. A method of `learning_rate` for a schedule of your own that reads `st.gs`, the state of the transform in the state of the rule, reads `st.gs.v` and `st.gs.m` now.
- **A variance estimator of your own as the `ce` of `EWResidualVolatility` or `EWResidualDownsideVolatility` adds a method of `PortfolioOptimisers.reads_active_mask`, which is public.** The constructor refuses a `ce` that is not mask-aware, as [Calls that now raise](@ref migration-0-34-raises) states. It cannot see a method of `variance_series` over an asset panel that your type defines, so it refuses your type with an `ArgumentError`, where v0.33 called that method. If the method reads the active mask of each asset, add `PortfolioOptimisers.reads_active_mask(::MyVariance) = true`. A windowed series and a `PortfolioOptimisersCovariance` read the same method, and give your type the whole window.
- **A selection rule or a redundancy algorithm of your own drops a non-finite score.** A method of `rule_keep` for a subtype of `AbstractSelectionRule` returns `false` at every `NaN` and every infinite score, under every parameter of the rule. A method of `redundancy_keep` for a subtype of `AbstractRedundancyAlgorithm` returns `false` at every asset whose given score is not finite. A subtype of `AbstractAssetScorer` writes `NaN` at an asset that it cannot score, and the asset must not survive a selection.
- **The Gerber, Smyth-Broby and Gerber IQ algorithms and the shrinkage targets are not moment forms.** `GerberCovarianceAlgorithm`, `SmythBrobyCovarianceAlgorithm`, `GerberIQCovarianceAlgorithm` and `AbstractCovarianceShrinkageTarget` subtype `AbstractAlgorithm`, where v0.33 had `AbstractMomentAlgorithm`. A method that you wrote on `AbstractMomentAlgorithm` no longer reaches their members. An algorithm of your own in one of these families keeps working with the estimator that owns the family.
- **`DiscreteAllocation` and `GreedyAllocation`** have the fields `unit`, `sets` and `strict` before `fb`. `DiscreteAllocation` takes eight positional arguments, where v0.33 took five, and `GreedyAllocation` takes `unit`, `sets`, `strict` and `fb`, where v0.33 took `unit`, `args`, `kwargs` and `fb`. A method that names the type parameters of `DiscreteAllocation` by position moves, because `fb` is now the eighth. In the model of `DiscreteAllocation`, the integer variable is the lot count `z`, and `x` is the expression `lu .* z`. `finite_sub_allocation`, `finite_sub_allocation!` and `set_allocation_fees!` take the lot unit after the prices.
- **A collateral algorithm of your own needs a third method.** `ca(w, prices, cash, smoney, sfee, lmoney, lfee)` returns the cash that the account holds after both sides trade, and both allocators report it as `cash`. A rule with only the two v0.33 methods raises a `MethodError` when an allocator reports its cash. `ProceedsCollateral` returns `cash + smoney - sfee - lmoney - lfee`, and `CashCollateral` returns `cash - smoney - sfee - lmoney - lfee`. A `NaN` price in `prices` marks an asset with no price on the allocation date, and its weight in `w` is zero.
- **`FiniteAllocationInput`** has a field `upw` after `ca`, so its positional constructor takes nine arguments, where v0.33 took eight. The keyword constructor keeps every v0.33 keyword.
- **`EntropyPoolingPrior` and `MeucciEntropyPoolingPrior`** have a field `diagnose` after `opt`, so each positional constructor takes 18 arguments, where v0.33 took 17. The keyword constructors keep every v0.33 keyword. The default `diagnose = nothing` fits as v0.33 did. A solver, or a vector of solvers, in `diagnose` runs a feasibility programme over the scenarios before the entropy pooling solve, and a set of views that no posterior meets together raises a `DomainError` that names the view fields. The programme can cost more than the fit: on 1008 scenarios, from 1.6 to 69 times the time of the fit.
- **`GridSearchCrossValidation` and `RandomisedSearchCrossValidation`** have the fields `s_sca` and `n_best` after `scorer`. `n_best` is the count of the best candidates that the search keeps. `s_sca` reduces the values of a vector of search scorers in `scorer` to one value per candidate, and the default `nothing` gives the sum. They also have the field `rank_prediction` after `train_score`. With `rank_prediction = true`, the Result keeps the prediction that the search scored for each kept candidate, and the default `false` keeps none. Their positional constructors take three more arguments. The keyword constructors keep every v0.33 keyword, and `n_best = 1` with one scorer gives the v0.33 search.
- **`HighestMeanScore` has a field `scale`**, the weight of the scorer in a vector of search scorers. `HighestMeanScore()` gives `scale = 1.0`, and the scale is inert for one scorer, so a v0.33 search does not move. A method that dispatches on `HighestMeanScore` keeps working, and the type now has one type parameter. The constructor raises an `IsNonFiniteError` for a scale that is not finite.
- **`SearchCrossValidationResult`** has the fields `ranked`, `rank_idx`, `rank_scores`, `rank_predictions` and `path_ids` after `idx`, so its positional constructor takes 11 arguments, where v0.33 took 6. The keyword constructor keeps every v0.33 keyword, and the new keywords default to `[opt]`, `[idx]`, `nothing`, `nothing` and `nothing`. Under `EachValue()` in `r_sca` or `s_sca` of the search, `opt`, `idx`, `ranked`, `rank_idx`, `rank_scores` and `rank_predictions` hold one entry for each value, in a vector or a `K × L` matrix, and `test_scores` and `train_scores` hold one entry per measure under `r_sca = EachValue()`. So the bounds of these fields widen, and a method that dispatches on their types also meets these containers. Under `r_sca = PerCandidate(sca)` with a vector of measures, `test_scores` and `train_scores` also hold one entry per measure, and the other fields keep one ranking. A search of one value keeps the v0.33 types.
- **A search scorer of your own** can define `candidate_scores`, which is public, to give one value per candidate. The search then orders the candidates by it, and it can keep more than one. It refuses values of another length with a `DimensionMismatch`, and a `NaN` value with an `IsNonFiniteError`. A scorer that defines only the index form keeps working at `n_best = 1`, and a search at `n_best > 1` raises an `ArgumentError` that names the method to add.
- **A function scorer gives what a search scorer gives.** It gives a vector of one value per column of the score matrix, bigger is better, or the index of one winner, as in v0.33. The search reads the form from the return. A function that gives an index with `n_best > 1` raises an `ArgumentError` when the search runs, not when you build it. A scorer that gives an index raises an `ArgumentError` in a vector of scorers under a reduction in `s_sca`, and under `r_sca = PerCandidate(sca)`, because an index has no value to combine. Under `s_sca = EachValue()` it keeps its one winner, and its entry of `rank_scores` is `nothing`. A subtype with a `scale` field defines `PortfolioOptimisers.scorer_scale`, which is public, to give its weight in a vector. The fallback gives `1`.
- **Fewer type parameters on a Result.** A Result does not carry the type of an estimator in a type parameter, so a verb on it compiles one time for every configuration of the estimator. `MultiPeriodPredictionResult` has no type parameter for `opt`. `MeanRiskResult`, `RiskBudgetingResult`, `FactorRiskContributionResult`, `NearOptimalCenteringResult`, `HierarchicalRiskParityResult` and `SchurComplementHierarchicalRiskParityResult` have none for `r`. The fields keep their bounds and their values. A method that names the type parameters of these types by position moves. `Resume` has a second field, `opt`, the estimator that its Result carries, and its second type parameter is the type of that estimator. Dispatch on it in place of the fourth parameter of `MultiPeriodPredictionResult`.
- **No type parameter for a scalariser or a returns estimator.** `ResolvedJuMPOptimiser`, the `jor` of every JuMP optimisation Result, has none for `ret` and `sca`. It is `ProcessedJuMPOptimiserAttributes`, the `pa` of v0.33, see [Renamed keywords and changed defaults](@ref migration-0-34-renames). `HierarchicalRiskParityResult` has none for `sca`, and `HierarchicalEqualRiskContributionResult` has none for `ri`, `ro`, `scai` and `scao`. The fields keep their bounds and their values. A method that names the type parameters of these types by position moves.
- **`PredictionResult` has no type parameter for `res`.** Its three type parameters are the types of `rd`, `hw` and `idx`, and `res` keeps the bound `NonFiniteAllocationOptimisationResult` on the field. So the folds of every optimiser have one type, and `MultiPeriodPredictionResult` narrows its folds to that type: `eltype(p.pred)` is concrete, where v0.33 gave `PredictionResult`. Write `PredictionResult{<:Any, <:HeldWeightsResult}` in place of `PredictionResult{<:Any, <:Any, <:HeldWeightsResult}`, and `PredictionResult{<:PredictionReturnsResult{…}}` in place of `PredictionResult{<:Any, <:PredictionReturnsResult{…}}`. A `concrete_typed_array(p.pred)` by hand is no longer needed.
- **`LowOrderPrior` and `HighOrderPrior` have the field `eoc`, the effective number of observations of each moment.** It comes after `ens` in `LowOrderPrior` and after `skmp` in `HighOrderPrior`, so the positional constructors take 15 and 10 arguments, where v0.33 took 14 and 9. The keyword constructors give `eoc = (nothing, nothing)` when you omit it, and an entry of `nothing` means the number of rows of `X`. The property `pr.eoc` of a `HighOrderPrior` gives the four counts, the two of its low order prior first. `eoc` is not a type parameter, because an entry is `nothing` or a count by its value. So the type parameters are the 14 of `LowOrderPrior` and the 9 of `HighOrderPrior` of v0.33, in the same order, and a stated and an unstated count give one prior type.
- **`Stacking` stores a vector `opti` as a `Vector{OptE_Opt_TD}`.** v0.33 narrowed it to the union of the types of its elements, so each combination of inner optimisers gave a new type of `Stacking`. A method that dispatched on the element type of `opti` dispatches on each element. A mixed literal needs no `concrete_typed_array`.
- **An estimator of your own that holds a child on another axis than the assets.** The check of a `UniverseSets` walks the fields of each estimator that `asset_axis_fields` names. For a `@propagatable` struct with a `@vprop` field, these are the `@vprop` fields. For any other struct, these are all the fields that can hold an estimator, an algorithm or a vector. A struct of the second kind that holds a child that it fits on other data, such as the factor returns, defines `asset_axis_fields` for its type, and leaves that child out. Otherwise a correct universe in that child is refused.
- **`PerformanceSummaryResult`** has the fields `names`, `paths`, `members`, `date0` and `date1` before `n_periods`, `rf` after `compound`, and `n_eff`, `long_exposure`, `short_exposure`, `gross_exposure`, `net_exposure`, `measures` and `risk` after `turnover`. Its positional constructor takes 29 arguments, where v0.33 took 16. Build it with `performance_summary`, or with the new keyword constructor `PerformanceSummaryResult(; n_periods, ppy, …)`, which takes each field by name. The keyword constructor gives `nothing` to the keys and the dates, and an empty `NamedTuple` to `measures` and `risk`, when you omit them. In a summary of many entries, an omitted key or date is `nothing` at each entry. Both constructors check the values, as [Calls that now raise](@ref migration-0-34-raises) states.
- **`ClusterNode` has two type parameters, `ClusterNode{T, H}`, the types of `id` and `height`.** v0.33 had five, one for each field, so the type of a node held the shape of its subtree, and each new dendrogram compiled the methods that read it again. A method that names the type parameters of `ClusterNode` by position moves. `left` and `right` are `nothing` or a `ClusterNode{T, H}`, and the constructor raises a `DomainError` for a child of another id type or height type, which v0.33 accepted. `level` is an `Int`.
- **`CrossSectionalLinearRegression` and `CrossSectionalTargetRegression`** have a field `leverage` before `ex`, so their positional constructors take four and five arguments, where v0.33 took three and four. The keyword constructors keep every v0.33 keyword, and the default `leverage = true` runs the leverage check of v0.33.
- **`BudgetCosts` and `BudgetMarketImpact`** have a field `fixed` after their last field, and `BudgetCosts` has a field `xbgt` after `fixed`. So both positional constructors take eight arguments, where v0.33 took six and seven. The keyword constructors keep every v0.33 keyword, and `fixed = true` keeps the reference `w` at every fold, as in v0.33.
- **`JuMPOptimiser` holds its feasible set in a `ConstraintBlock`, in the field `cbe`.** The block holds the slots `wb`, `sbgt`, `gbgt`, `lt`, `st`, `lcse`, `cte`, `gcarde`, `sgcarde`, `smtx`, `sgmtx`, `slt`, `sst`, `sglt`, `sgst`, `tn`, `sets`, `tr`, `ple`, `ccnt`, `cobj`, `ss`, `card`, `scard`, `l2c`, `lpc`, `linfc`, `l1`, `l2`, `lp`, `linf` and `xbgt`. The positional constructor takes 11 arguments, `JuMPOptimiser(pe, slv, cbe, bgt, fees, ret, sca, sc, so, strict, cache)`, where v0.33 took 42. The keyword constructor keeps every v0.33 keyword, and it also takes `cbe`. A read such as `opt.wb`, a search key such as `"opt.wb"`, `Accessors.set` and a pipeline route into a slot give the values of v0.33. A method that names the type parameters of `JuMPOptimiser` by position moves, and so does code that reads a slot with `getfield(opt, :wb)`: read `opt.wb` or `opt.cbe.wb`. A path that names a slot, such as in an error message or in `group_frames`, now passes through the block, as `"opt.cbe.tr.tr"`.
- **`ProgrammeAllocationSet` holds its constraint kinds in a `ConstraintBlock`, in the field `cbe`, as `JuMPOptimiser` does.** `r`, `ret`, `sc` and `so` stay fields of the set. The positional constructor takes 7 arguments, `ProgrammeAllocationSet(pe, slv, cbe, r, ret, sc, so)`, where v0.33 took 38. The keyword constructor keeps every v0.33 keyword, and it also takes `cbe`. `set.wb` and `Accessors.set` give the values of v0.33. A method that names the type parameters by position moves, and so does a `getfield(set, :wb)` read: read `set.wb`. A path that names a slot passes through the block, as `"set.cbe.ple"`. A `TimeDependent` in a slot raises an `ArgumentError`, where v0.33 raised a `TypeError`: schedule the whole set.
- **A Custom Constraint or a Custom Objective Term of your own receives a `ResolvedJuMPOptimiser`.** Write `attrs::ResolvedJuMPOptimiser` where v0.33 wrote `attrs::ProcessedJuMPOptimiserAttributes`. `attrs.pr`, `attrs.fees`, `attrs.ret`, `attrs.wb` and the other resolved constraints of v0.33 read as before. The norm ceilings `l2c`, `lpc` and `linfc` and the penalties `l1`, `l2`, `lp` and `linf` are resolved in `attrs.cbr`, with each calibration rule replaced by its value, where v0.33 resolved them in `assemble_jump_model!` and held them on the optimiser alone. The four builders `set_weight_norm_2_constraints!`, `set_weight_norm_inf_constraints!`, `set_l1_regularisation!` and `set_linf_regularisation!` no longer check their number, because the stage 2 of the block, `resolve_constraint_block`, checks it.
- **`attrs.cbr` can be a vector.** When the head solves with more than one block, `attrs.cbr` is a vector of `ResolvedConstraintBlock`s with the block of the head first, and `attrs.wb` and the other forwarded reads give the block of the head.
- **A head of your own builds its weights and stops there.** `assemble_jump_model!` now adds the weight bounds and the budgets, in phase 1 of the block of the head, with the net budget `opt.bgt`. A head that called `set_weight_constraints!(model, attrs.wb, opt)` before `assemble_jump_model!` now raises an `ArgumentError` that names the key `w_lb` or `w_ub`, because the model registers the row twice: remove the call. A long-only head checks its bounds with `assert_long_only_weight_bounds(wb)`, where v0.33 passed `long = true` to `set_weight_constraints!`. That positional argument is gone.
- **`set_weight_constraints!` and the budget builders take `prefix`.** `set_weight_constraints!`, `set_budget_constraints!`, `set_long_short_budget_constraints!` and `set_gross_budget_constraints!` register their rows under the prefix of a block, and the default `Symbol("")` gives the names of v0.33. A `BudgetCosts` or a `BudgetMarketImpact` under a non-empty prefix raises an `ArgumentError`, because it registers shared entries. `assemble_allocation_set!` is gone. `set_block_constraints!` and `set_block_post_risk_constraints!` add a block, and `set_allocation_risk_return_bounds!` adds the ceilings and the floor of a `ProgrammeAllocationSet`.
- **A Custom Objective Term of a `ProgrammeAllocationSet` receives the `ResolvedConstraintBlock` of the set.** In a projection, `add_custom_objective_term!` gets the block Result of the set as `attrs`, where v0.33 gave `nothing`. The set also gives its `ccnt` and its `cobj` the price-adjusted allocation of the step through `factory`, as it gives its turnover and its tracking errors. `set_allocation_set_constraints!` returns the bounds of the step and the block Result, `(wb, cbr)`, where v0.33 returned `nothing`. On a `BoundedAllocationSet`, `cbr` is `nothing`.
- **A projection gives a set the price-adjusted allocation before it views the set at the investable mask.** The projection programme calls `factory(set, w)` on every asset, and then views the set at the mask of the step, as a JuMP head does. v0.33 viewed the set first, and then called `factory` with `w` on the mask. A Custom Constraint or a Custom Objective Term of your own that defines `port_opt_view` and holds `w` through `factory` now meets the view after `factory`, so its `port_opt_view` cuts `w` to the mask too. `set_allocation_set_constraints!` takes a three-argument form, `(model, set, cbr)`, which adds a block that the entry resolved. The four-argument and the five-argument forms resolve the step themselves and refuse an asset that the prior of the set cannot price, as in v0.33. `allocation_set_cobj` is gone, and `set_block_objective_terms!` adds the `cobj` of a block.
- **The rows of a set in a leader's model are registered under `blk_2_`.** The bounds and the short and gross budgets of a `ProgrammeAllocationSet` or a `BoundedAllocationSet` were anonymous rows in the model of a `FollowTheLeader` leader. The set is now the second block of the model, so its rows are `blk_2_w_lb`, `blk_2_w_ub`, `blk_2_sbgt` and the others, and a reader of the model can find them.
