The budget of one is the row `bgt` of the leader, because the re-solve sets the net budget of the leader to one. The return floor of a set clears its `rte`, so the floor no longer enters `ret_vec` of the leader. No weight moves, because the leader read `ret_vec` before the set added its floor.
- **A head of your own passes `blocks` to `assemble_jump_model!`.** A JuMP head that gets more than one block adds the rows of every block to its model, where it added the rows of its own block alone. Its `_optimise` takes the keyword `blocks` by name, gives it to `resolve_jump_optimiser`, and gives the same value to `assemble_jump_model!(...; blocks = blocks)`. Block `i` after the first registers its rows under the prefix `blk_i_`, so the second block gives `blk_2_w_ub`. A head of your own that leaves out the keyword of `assemble_jump_model!` adds its own block alone, as before. The unconstrained `NearOptimalCentering` adds the bounds and the budgets of each later block, and no other row of it.
- **A Custom Objective Term gets the objective of the model, once for each model.** `assemble_jump_model!` calls `add_custom_objective_term!` once, with its own `obj`, the declared objective of a `MeanRisk`. v0.33 called it in `set_portfolio_objective_function!`, once for each objective that the model got. So the end points of a `Frontier` sweep gave the term `MinimumRisk` and `MaximumReturn` as `obj`, and now give it the declared objective. A method that dispatches on `obj` to change the term at an end point no longer can. `set_portfolio_objective_function!` takes `(model, obj)`, where v0.33 took `(model, obj, optimiser, attrs)`. A head of your own that calls `assemble_jump_model!` gets its terms from it, and calls `set_portfolio_objective_function!(model, obj)`.
- **More entries of a JuMP model have a name.** A `BudgetCosts` registers its rows `wp_ub`, `wn_ub` and `cost_bgt_trade`, and a `BudgetMarketImpact` also registers `wip_pcone` and `win_pcone`, where v0.33 left them anonymous. The gain tail of a range of returns registers the negated series `gain_net_X`, and a drawdown measure registers the negated path `neg_dd`, under the prefix of the measure. A Custom Constraint of your own that registers one of these names in the same model raises an `ErrorException` of JuMP. The rows and the weights do not move.
- **A test of the mean of your own returns a `MeanTestResult`.** `mean_test` returns a `MeanTestResult`, which is public, where v0.33 returned the named tuple `(; t_stat, p_value, mean_ci_lower, mean_ci_upper)`. A method of `mean_test` for a subtype of `AbstractMeanTestEstimator` builds it with `MeanTestResult(t_stat, p_value, mean_ci_lower, mean_ci_upper)` or with the same four keywords. The constructor raises a `DomainError` for a p-value outside `[0, 1]` and for a lower bound above the upper bound, and a `NaN` passes. A caller that reads the fields by name, as `res.p_value` or `(; t_stat, p_value) = res`, does not change. A caller that calls `values`, `keys` or `merge` on the answer reads the four fields instead.
- **`factory` returns every value that has no method of its own.** `factory(a, args...; kwargs...) = a` takes `::Any`, where v0.33 took an estimator, an algorithm, a result or `nothing`, and raised a `MethodError` for any other value. A struct of yours with an `@fprop` field that holds data, or a covariance estimator of `StatsBase`, needs no method. The methods of `factory` that only returned their input on `StatsBase.CovarianceEstimator`, `AbstractBaseRiskMeasure`, `MomentMeasureAlgorithm`, `ValueatRiskFormulation`, `DimensionReductionTarget`, phylogeny estimators and results, a number with a weights vector, and an `AbstractRegressionTarget` with an `InterceptColumn` are gone, and the leaf gives the same answer. The array method takes an `AbstractArray` of any shape, where v0.33 took an `AbstractVector`.
- **A return forecast estimator of your own takes a last regression argument.** The prior calls `return_forecast(rfe, rd, csfm, cre)` with `cre` as `orthogonal_forecast_regression` gives it, `nothing` under every rule but `OrthogonalPartCalibration`. A member that reads no regression writes `return_forecast(rfe::MyMember, rd, csfm, ::Nothing = nothing)`, and a member that folds writes `return_forecast_step(rfe::MyMember, P, csfm, fs, ::Nothing = nothing)`. A member with only the three-argument method of v0.33 raises a `MethodError` in the prior.
- **A cross-sectional solve algorithm of your own takes six arguments in one method.** The regression calls `cross_sectional_solve(alg, A, y, t, F, ws)`, with the factorisation `F` that it took and the workspace `ws` of its task. v0.33 called a method of four arguments, `cross_sectional_solve(alg, A, y, t)`. Write `cross_sectional_solve(::MySolve, A, y, t, F = nothing, ws::NamedTuple = (;))`, and ignore the two slots when the member reads neither. A member with only the four-argument method raises a `MethodError` in the regression.
- **`resolve_deferred_quantities` takes a prior result.** A call with a second argument that is not an `AbstractPriorResult` raises a `MethodError`, where v0.33 returned the first argument. No caller in the library made such a call.
- **A cross-sectional solve algorithm of your own returns a `CrossSectionalSolveResult`.** `cross_sectional_solve` and `cross_sectional_target_solve` return a `CrossSectionalSolveResult`, which is public, where v0.33 returned the vector of the factor returns. The field `f` holds that vector. The field `edof` is new: the residual count of the fit, which the regression writes into `edof` of `CrossSectionalRegression`. A method for a subtype of `AbstractCrossSectionalSolveAlgorithm` builds it with `CrossSectionalSolveResult(f, edof)` or with the same two keywords. The constructor raises a `DomainError` for a residual count that is not finite, and a count below zero passes. A method that returns a vector, as in v0.33, or a named tuple raises a `MethodError` in the regression. A caller that used the vector reads `res.f`. A caller that compares two answers with `==` compares their fields instead.
- **A descriptor of your own that folds returns a `DescriptorStepResult`.** `descriptor_step` returns a `DescriptorStepResult`, which is public, where v0.33 returned the named tuple `(; de, D)`. A method for a subtype of `AbstractDescriptorEstimator` builds it with `DescriptorStepResult(de, D)` or with the same two keywords, from the estimator with its new state and the matrix of the descriptor. The carry fold of the prior reads it through `descriptor_carried`, which takes `nothing` or a `DescriptorStepResult`, so a method that returns the named tuple raises a `MethodError` in the fold. A caller that reads the fields by name does not change.
- **An unseen member rule of your own returns an `UnseenMemberDesignResult`.** `unseen_member_design` returns an `UnseenMemberDesignResult`, which is public, where v0.33 returned the named tuple `(; Z, P)`. A method for a subtype of `AbstractUnseenMemberRule` builds it with `UnseenMemberDesignResult(Z, P)`, or with the keywords `Z` and `P`, whose default is `()`. `P` is a vector or a tuple of the pairs `t => P_t`. The constructor raises a `DomainError` for a pair `t => P_t` whose `t` is not an observation of `Z`, and a `DimensionMismatch` for a `P_t` that is not square on the factor axis of `Z`. Build the type in a method of yours: the readers of the library read the two fields by name, so they do not refuse the named tuple, but they get no check of it. A caller that reads the fields by name does not change.
- **A rule of your own for an unknown entry returns a `LeverageSplitResult` and an `AttributionErrorsResult`.** `attribution_leverage_split` returns a `LeverageSplitResult` or `nothing`, and `attribution_leverage_errors` returns an `AttributionErrorsResult`. Both types are public. v0.33 returned the named tuples `(; assets, factors)` and `(; sys, factor, family)`. A method for a subtype of `AbstractUnknownEntryRule` builds them with `LeverageSplitResult(assets, factors)` and `AttributionErrorsResult(sys, factor, family)`, or with the same keywords. The defaults of `AttributionErrorsResult` are `nothing`, the answer of an attribution with no standard errors.
  The constructor of `LeverageSplitResult` raises an `ArgumentError` for marks that name no asset, because a rule that marks nothing answers `nothing`. The constructor of `AttributionErrorsResult` raises an `ArgumentError` when `sys` and `factor` are not both stated or both `nothing`, and when `family` is stated without `factor`. A method that returns a named tuple raises a `MethodError` in `attribution_leverage_nan` or in the realised attribution. The argument `ses` and the answers of `pass` are an `AttributionErrorsResult` too. A caller that reads the fields by name, as `s.assets` or `(; sys, factor) = ses`, does not change. `keys` of an answer raises a `MethodError`, and a destructure by position, as `assets, factors = s`, raises a `BoundsError`. Two answers are not `==`, because the types compare their vectors by identity, so compare their fields.
- **An orthogonal forecast fit of your own returns a `ForecastSplitResult`.** `orthogonal_forecast_rescale` returns a `ForecastSplitResult`, which is public, where v0.33 returned the named tuple `(; g, ap)`. A method for a subtype of `AbstractOrthogonalForecastFit` builds it with `ForecastSplitResult(g, ap)` or with the same two keywords. The constructor raises an `ArgumentError` when one part is `nothing` and the other is not. A method that returns the named tuple raises a `MethodError` in the prior. A caller that reads the fields by name does not change.
- **The buffer of `centring_lag_records` holds a `CentringLagRecord`.** Each record of the HAC term of an `EstimatedCentring` is a `CentringLagRecord`, which is private, with the fields `obs_count`, `valid` and `P`. v0.33 held the named tuple `(; obs_count, valid, P)`, with the same values. A method of `centring_lag_factor` of your own that reads a record by field name does not change. A method of `centring_lag_records` of your own that makes a buffer of the named tuple raises a `MethodError` when the fold pushes a record into it. Make the buffer with the element type `typeof(PortfolioOptimisers.CentringLagRecord(Int[], falses(0), P))`. A caller that calls `keys` or `merge` on a record raises a `MethodError`.
- **An online portfolio selection rule of your own takes the last slot of `online_update!` and of `rule_state_seed`.** Write `online_update!(alg::MyRule, st, w, x, rows, set, ::Option{<:AbstractVector} = nothing)` where v0.33 wrote the six-argument form, and `rule_state_seed(alg::MyRule, w, ::Option{<:AbstractAllocationSet} = nothing)` where v0.33 wrote the two-argument form. The last slots are the point at which an `ExpertMixture` asks its experts for a gradient, and the set of allowed weights that the head gives the seed. v0.33 had a generic method of each long form that dropped the slot, and v0.34 has none. A rule with only the six-argument form raises a `MethodError` as an expert of an `ExpertMixture`. A rule with only the two-argument form gets the default state `nothing` from the head. A first-order rule that gets `nothing` as the point of its gradient takes its own iterate.
- **A geometry of your own reaches the projection programme on a `ProgrammeAllocationSet`.** `project(proj::AbstractProjectionGeometry, set::ProgrammeAllocationSet, q, w)` solves the programme for every geometry, with the objective that `set_projection_objective!` writes for it. v0.33 had a method for each geometry of the library alone, so a geometry of your own raised a `MethodError` in `project`. It now raises the `MethodError` in `set_projection_objective!`, a private function, unless it has a method `project(proj::MyGeometry, set::ProgrammeAllocationSet, q, w)`, which is more specific than the method of the library.
- **A dynamic observation weight of your own returns one concrete weight type.** A moment risk measure now rebuilds itself with the weights that your `get_observation_weights` method returns. When the method infers a `Union`, as `eweights(1:n, lambda)` does, each call of the measure allocates 80 more bytes than before. Return one concrete type, as `eweights(n, lambda)` does, and the call allocates no more than before. The value does not change. The docstring of `DynamicAbstractWeights` shows the form.
- **A JuMP optimisation Result of your own declares its own property forwarding.** v0.33 defined `getproperty` and `propertynames` on `RJR_NRJR`, so a subtype of `RiskJuMPOptimisationResult` or `NonRiskJuMPOptimisationResult` read `res.w` and the fields of `jor` through its field `jr` with no declaration. These methods are gone, because they gave no `setproperties` method, and `Accessors.set` refused every patch of a `MeanRiskResult` or a `NearOptimalCenteringResult`. Write `@forward_properties MyResult begin forward(jr) end` after the struct, as each Result of the library does. A subtype without the block raises an error at its first read of `res.w`, because `w` is not one of its fields. The block gives `MeanRiskResult` and `NearOptimalCenteringResult` the names that the methods on `RJR_NRJR` gave, in the same order, and a patch of a field now gives a new Result. The names of `jor` are those of its v0.34 type, so a name of v0.33 that left `jor` is not a name of the Result either.

- **The carried states of the exponentially weighted beta Descriptors.** `EWBetaState` holds the `EWMoments` of each asset, the moments of the squared weights for the shrinkage, the open `EWReturnWindow`, the held betas and the count of the observations, and it is the state of an `EWBeta` too, where v0.33 used `EWBlockState`. `EWMacroSensitivityState` holds an `EWBetaState`, the constant-market flag of each asset and the two counts of the reference windows. `EWDownsideBetaState` holds the second moment of the market shortfall per asset in `vd`, a vector, and has no field `t`. A state of v0.33 does not fold into v0.34: fit the Descriptor again.
- **`CalibrationContext`** has a field `n` after `cs`, the count that the site prices, so its positional constructor takes five arguments, where v0.33 took four. The keyword constructor keeps every v0.33 keyword, and the default `n = nothing` reads the effective sample size of the prior, as v0.33 did. A radius rule of your own reads `ctx.n` where it is not `nothing`, as the radius rules of the library do.
- **A systematic repair of your own takes the count `T`.** The prior calls `systematic_processing!(srep, mp, sigma, X, T; kwargs...)`, with `T` the effective count of the observations, where v0.33 called the form of four arguments. Write `systematic_processing!(::MyRepair, mp, sigma, X, T::Real = size(X, 1); kwargs...)` for a subtype of `AbstractSystematicRepair`. A method with the v0.33 arguments alone raises a `MethodError` in the prior.
- **A variance estimator of your own takes the active mask in `variance_count`.** A `CrossSectionalFactorPrior` calls `variance_count(ve, X, amsk)`, a private function, with the active mask that its variance read, where v0.33 called `variance_count(ve, X)`. A method of your own with the two v0.33 arguments alone is not called there. The fallback then states no count, so the block records no degrees of freedom, and `ResidualInflation` raises an `IsNothingError` that asks for `dof`. Write `PortfolioOptimisers.variance_count(ve::MyVariance, X::PortfolioOptimisers.MatNum, amsk::Union{Nothing, AbstractMatrix{<:Bool}} = nothing)`. A looser bound on `X` or on `amsk` is ambiguous with the fallback, and the call raises a `MethodError`. When your variance restarts an asset where the mask turns `false`, count the rows after the last inactive row of the asset, as the exponentially weighted estimators of the library do.
- **A regression target of your own subtypes one of two families.** A target that subtypes `AbstractRegressionTarget` directly subtypes `AbstractUnpenalisedRegressionTarget` or `AbstractPenalisedRegressionTarget` now, both public. An unpenalised target takes every criterion of `StepwiseRegression`. A penalised target takes `:aic`, `:aicc` and `:bic`, as `ElasticNet` does. A target in neither family raises a `MethodError` in `StepwiseRegression` under the default `PValue()`, `:r2` and `:adjr2`.
- **`RiskBudgeting`** has a field `kmin` before `fb`, so its positional constructor takes six arguments, `RiskBudgeting(opt, r, rba, wi, kmin, fb)`, where v0.33 took five. The keyword constructor keeps every v0.33 keyword.

## From v0.34.0 to v0.34.1

### [Calls that now raise](@id migration-0-34-1-raises)

| Call | v0.34.0 | v0.34.1 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `partial_fit!` of a `Coskewness` or a `Cokurtosis` whose `mp` has an `alg` step that reads the values of the sample, such as `mp = MatrixProcessing(; alg = LoGo(; de = Distance(; alg = VariationInfoDistance())))` or an algorithm of your own, or whose `mp` is a matrix processing estimator of your own | Folded the rows. The call with no data, `coskewness(ske)` or `cokurtosis(kte)`, then gave the step a matrix of zeros in place of the sample. Under a variation of information distance, `LoGo` raised a `DomainError` about a `NaN` distance, and the batch verb answered. | Raises an `ArgumentError` at the fold, as `partial_fit!` of a `PortfolioOptimisersCovariance` does. `supports_partial_fit` answers `false`, so a prior or a pipeline runs the batch verb over its own rows. Wrap the estimator in `Online` to fold it with the step. The new public trait `reads_sample_values` states which steps read the sample. A `LoGo` under a distance that reads the correlation matrix alone, such as the default `LoGo()`, reads none, so it still folds, and `partial_fit!` of a `PortfolioOptimisersCovariance` now folds it too. An algorithm of your own that reads no value of the sample folds when it adds `PortfolioOptimisers.reads_sample_values(::MyAlgorithm) = false` and a method `matrix_processing_algorithm!(alg::MyAlgorithm, sigma; kwargs...)` with no sample. A distance estimator or a distance algorithm of your own that reads the correlation matrix alone folds inside `LoGo` when it adds `PortfolioOptimisers.reads_sample_values(::MyDistance) = false`. |
| `descriptor` of `SalesToPrice()`, `GrossProfitability()`, `AssetTurnover()` or `SalesToEnterpriseValue()` over a panel with a negative sales figure on an active, observed cell | Divided the negative sales figure, as for any other number. `GrossMargin()` already refused it. | Raises a `DomainError` that names the field and the cell, as `GrossMargin()` does. Each of the four constructors sets `nonneg = [num]`, or `nonneg = [sales]` for `GrossProfitability`, because a sales figure below zero is a data error. A zero sales figure stays valid. To read the sales figure without the guard, build `PanelFieldRatio` with the same `num`, `den` and `gt0`, and no `nonneg`. |
| `descriptor` of `DebtToAssets()` over a panel with a negative debt on an active, observed cell | Divided the negative debt. `BookLeverage()` and `MarketLeverage()` already refused it. | Raises a `DomainError`, as `BookLeverage()` and `MarketLeverage()` do. `DebtToAssets` sets `nonneg = [num]`. To read the debt without the guard, build `PanelFieldRatio(; num = "total_debt", den = "total_assets", gt0 = ["total_assets"])`. |

## From v0.34.2 to v0.34.3

### [Results that move](@id migration-0-34-3-numbers)

- **A `RiskBudgeting` whose mixed-integer rows use a long and a short bit for each asset.** A short threshold `st`, a fixed fee, or `xbgt` beside another mixed-integer row builds these bits, on a model that splits the weights into a long and a short part. v0.34.2 applied the weight bounds, the thresholds and the fixed fees to the unscaled weights, which sum to `k` and not to the budget. A bound of one then held each weight under `1 / k`, so a book could come out equal-weighted, and a threshold did not hold on the weights that the optimiser returned. v0.34.3 scales each of them with `k`, as the long-only bits always did. A model whose thresholds v0.34.2 could meet only in the unscaled weights can now be infeasible.
- **A `RiskBudgeting` with `LowOrderMoment(; mu, alg = EvenMoment())` and a number or a `VecScalar` as `mu`.** v0.34.2 subtracted the target from the returns of the unscaled weights, so it read a target of `mu / k`. v0.34.3 scales the target with `k`, as the other algorithms of `LowOrderMoment` do.
- **A `RiskBudgeting` with an upper bound, `settings.ub`, on one of five risk measures.** They are a `Variance` in the semidefinite form, an `UncertaintySetVariance` with a box, an ellipsoid or a norm ball, a `Kurtosis`, a `LowOrderMoment` with `EvenMoment`, and a `VarianceSkewKurtosis` with a bound of its own or of a child. v0.34.2 compared the risk of the unscaled weights with `ub * k`, so the bound was too tight by a power of `k`, and a bound that does not bind could make the model infeasible. v0.34.3 bounds the risk of the weights that the optimiser returns. A second model of the risk states the bound, so a solve with such a bound is larger. A measure with no bound, and every other optimiser, builds the same model as before.
- **A `RiskTrackingError`, or a `RiskTrackingRiskMeasure` with an upper bound, on a tracked measure of degree two, under `MaximumRatio` or `RiskBudgeting`.** Such measures are a `Variance`, an `UncertaintySetVariance`, a `LowOrderMoment` with a second or an even moment, a `Kurtosis`, a `NegativeSkewness`, a `BrownianDistanceVariance`, a `VarianceSkewKurtosis` and a `TrackingRiskMeasure` with `SquaredL2Norm`. v0.34.2 compared the tracked risk of the unscaled weights with the bound times `k`, so the bound was too tight by a power of `k`. v0.34.3 bounds the tracked risk of the weights that the optimiser returns.
- **A `RiskTrackingRiskMeasure` with `DependentVariableTracking` in the objective, and a tracked measure of degree two, under `MaximumRatio` or `RiskBudgeting`.** v0.34.2 subtracted the benchmark risk times `k` from a risk of degree two. v0.34.3 states both at degree one.
- **A `RiskTrackingError` with a small `err`.** The row of the bound divides by its bound, as the other bound rows do, so a solver holds a bound that is smaller than its tolerance.
- **A `Frontier` in the `settings.ub` of a `RiskTrackingRiskMeasure` with `IndependentVariableTracking`.** The levels are evenly spaced in the units of the expression that the bound of the tracked measure acts on, as they are for the tracked measure itself. For a `Variance` these are the units of the deviation, where v0.34.2 spaced the levels in the units of the variance. The two ends of the span do not move.
- **A `FactorRiskContribution` with a `RiskTrackingError` or a `RiskTrackingRiskMeasure` of a `Variance` under `IndependentVariableTracking`.** v0.34.2 bounded the variance of the portfolio in place of the variance of the weight difference, so a bound could make the model infeasible. v0.34.3 reads the weight difference, and it refuses an `rc` on such a `Variance`.

### [Calls that now succeed](@id migration-0-34-3-success)

- **`DependentVariableTracking` of a measure with a quadratic risk expression**, such as `Variance()` without a semidefinite phylogeny. v0.34.2 logged a warning at the construction of a `RiskTrackingRiskMeasure`, and the solver refused the model. v0.34.3 states the risk with a rotated second-order cone, and the constructor logs no warning.

## From v0.34 to v0.35

### [Cardinality caps as a count or a fraction](@id migration-0-35-cardinality-caps)

The cardinality caps `card` and `scard` of `ConstraintBlock`, `JuMPOptimiser` and `ProgrammeAllocationSet`, and the `B` of `IntegerPhylogenyEstimator` and `IntegerPhylogeny`, take a `CountOf` or a `FractionOf`. A fraction resolves against the set that the fit sees, so `card = FractionOf(0.2)` keeps its meaning when a view or a resample reduces the universe, or when an asset leaves it. A plain `Integer` keeps its meaning, and the slot holds the same integer. A fraction rounds down, to at least one member.

| Call | v0.34.3 | v0.35 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `JuMPOptimiser(; card = 0.2)`, and a plain real number between zero and one in `scard` or in `B` | A `TypeError` | Reads `FractionOf(0.2)`. |
| `JuMPOptimiser(; card = 3.0)` or `card = 1.0`, and the same in `scard` and `B` | A `TypeError` | Raises a `DomainError`. Write `3`, `CountOf(3)` or `FractionOf(1)`. |
| `IntegerPhylogeny(; A, B).B` with a `FractionOf` in `B` | A `TypeError` | A vector of integers, one cap for each row of the stored `A`. |
| A projection onto a `ProgrammeAllocationSet` that reads no rows, in a direct call of `project`, with a precomputed `lcse` or `gcarde` whose width is not the number of assets | The model raised the error | Raises the `DimensionMismatch` of the constraint width, as the same set raises it on rows. |
| `JuMPOptimiser(; smtx = [A, B], …)` with precomputed matrices and no `sets` | Raised a `MethodError` at the fit | Solves, as one precomputed matrix does. |

### [Counts that a fraction gives](@id migration-0-35-fraction-counts)

Some readers of a fraction rounded the floating-point product of the fraction and the size, so the count could be one away from the count that the fraction states. They now compare the share of the count with the fraction. The default fractions and significance levels keep their counts.

| Reader | Case | v0.34 | v0.35 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `NearestNeighbourMatch(; neighbours = FractionOf(0.29))` | 100 candidates | 28 neighbours | 29 |
| `IntegerConditionalValueatRiskView(; sbar = FractionOf(0.07))` | 100 observations | 8 losses | 7 |
| `IntegerConditionalValueatRiskView(; sbar = nothing)`, the floor of `2 * alpha * T` | `alpha = 0.07`, 100 observations | 15 losses | 14 |
| `RankRule(; best = FractionOf(0.575))`, and the same in `worst` | 100 assets | 57 assets | 58 |
| `LowerTailDependenceCovariance(; alpha = 0.07)` | 100 observations | 8 observations | 7 |
| `HillTailDecay` and `RadialTailDecay` at `alpha = 0.07` | 100 pooled values or distances | 8 values | 7 |
| `MCDCovariance(; alpha = 0.55, shr = MCDShrinkage())` | 100 observations | 56 observations | 55 |
| `MCDCovariance(; alpha = 0.58)` | 101 observations of one asset | 58 observations | 59 |
